# RE7 multiplayer: change history

The history of every build, newest first. What the mod does *now* is in README.md.

## What changed in v10.27 (release **0.11** unchanged; protocol v6 unchanged; **script only: the exe's code did not change; rebuild re7mp.exe so it carries the new script and `defaults.json`**)

Your 23 November request (17:59): "this is the data from probes of jack in the car, me getting into the car and the car being stationary, ill lyk when something else comes up but I want you to investigate the frame dips too" (nine files attached), and your 18:20 note: "the story flow was turned off".

**What the files showed**
- **The frame dips** (1,480 s at about 184 frames a second, 272,208 frames). 73 gaps over 100 ms, 71 of them the script's own work, 3 over 500 ms. Three causes, all of them mine: (a) the automatic model collection (the box you had ticked) stopped the game for 0.12 to 0.17 s every 20 s, 74 times; (b) the save-slot watch (it asks the game about 21 save slots every 5 s while a friend is there) stalled the game for about 220, 500 and 625 ms, each time 0.7 to 2.6 s into one of the game's 8 saves; (c) one 266 ms write of the debug file, 2.7 s into a save. The 714 ms gap at 50.8 s (a menu was open) was the game's: the script used 7 ms of it. Always there: the network part used 1.0 ms of every frame (4.5 minutes of the session) and the host's enemy scan 8.4 ms once a second.
- **The car.** The car module found the car (`sm9104_EthanCar_Garage`) at 1,321 s and read its state as an empty text for the whole session, so nothing that starts on a state (the claim, the other player's forced get-in, the puppet) could ever begin: 0 claims, 0 messages. The actor probe, asking the same state machine through the game object instead of the car's `Fsm` field, saw 19 state changes ending at **HitWall**. The module's story number was "nil" as well (next point). The probe's field list stopped at exactly 220 fields (its cap): `IsEnemyDriving`, `IsPlayerDriving`, `FlagWindowBroken`, the crash counters and the tyre flags are beyond it (the module itself finds 4 tyre flags, so they exist), and what I wrote in the earlier analysis, that these do not exist, was wrong. The 152 methods were listed whole; `CurrentAction` is an `app.CarInGarage.Action` (a delegate; the car has a `process<Mode>` method for each of its modes), `NextAction` was unset.
- **The story flow.** Your note is right about the cutscene sync: `story.on` was false, and the exe's "Sync story cutscenes" box said off from 7.1 s, so the story match was off too. The small read-only "story points" reader was on (it is on by default) and read the five Chapter 3 flows 1,773 times but could not turn any step number into a number: 7,871 readings "unreadable". The game's number holder is a `via.userdata.Variable`, and the reader asked it for methods it does not have (`get_Value`, `get_Int` ...); the flow probe lists the real ones (`get_CastS32`, `get_S32`, `get_CastU32`, `get_U32` ...). That is a code fault, not the switch.

**What changed**
- **1. The automatic model collection waits for a calm moment.** A menu is open, my Ethan is in a scripted move (a cutscene), or I have stood still for 1.5 s (less than 25 cm in half a second). If none comes it scans anyway after 2 minutes, and it never scans while the game is saving or loading. F11 and the button always scan at once. The scan walks the scene with a cheaper walk (patterns compiled once, no table built per object) that finds the same objects as the old one (checked name by name, with the inherited matches and the 8-level limit). The panel text says what it does, a "waiting" line says why it has not scanned, and a "last scan ... ms" line shows what the scan cost. The debug file's `model_collection` has `scan_cost` and `auto_scans` (how many were calm and how many forced).
- **2. The save-slot watch and the debug-file write wait while the game is saving** (and 1.5 s after it; a saving flag that stays on for 15 s is ignored; the "Save the debug file now" button always writes). `health.perf.frame_gaps.saving_flag.waits` says how often and how long each waited.
- **3. The network part is four timed parts**, not one: `net: pose gather`, `net: pose file write`, `net: pose file read`, `net: inbox read`. The 1.0 ms a frame is not fixed yet (it is about 67 file operations a second plus the flag reads when the pose is gathered); the next file says which of the four it is, and then it gets fixed.
- **4. The story number is read through the holder's real methods.** `get_CastS32` first, then `get_S32`, `get_CastU32`, `get_U32`, `get_OriginS32`, `get_CastF32`, `get_F32`, then the old names. Only a whole number from 0 up to 10 million is taken (not -1, not NaN, not 1e12); the method that worked is tried first from then on and is written in `story_flow.number_read_by`. Because the number is readable now, the car module knows the story step (its gate "the story is at N" works) and the story-points message (kind 18) goes to the other player for the first time in the real game.
- **5. The car's state is read through whichever route answers.** The car's `Fsm` field and the game object's `getComponent(via.fsm.Fsm)`; the one that answers is remembered, and `garage_car.fsm` says which (`via`), what the other one answered (`field_answer`, `component_answer`) and how many reads came back empty (`empty_reads`). A text only counts as a state when it has letters in it.
- **6. The windows are asked one by one when the car has no `FlagWindowBroken` number:** `isWindowBroken(1..6)` becomes the same bit mask (bit n = window n, as the other player's game breaks them). A car that will not answer is not asked again.
- **7. The fight rules.** Starting a claim stays strict: the states `PlayerGetInto`, `WaitEnemyGetPlayerOut`, `EnemyGetInto`, `ExecEnemyCrashEvent`, and now `HitWall` (the one the real game showed), or a driving / hijack flag. Ending is more careful: the fight is "left" only when the car is at rest (nobody drives, and its state is `WaitPlayerGetInto` or unreadable) for 10 s. Before, any state not on the list ended it after 10 s, and the real car has more states than the list. A state nobody listed neither starts a fight nor ends one.
- **8. The car journal says more.** Every state change records the action (the method `CurrentAction` holds), speed, engine, hijack, the hit flags, crashed, door, windows and the story number; `garage_car` has `my_action` and `my_extra`.
- **9. The car probe** lists up to 520 fields and 400 methods (and says `cut` when a cap was hit), also dumps `app.CarDamageControllerInGarage`, `app.CarStrikeControllerInGarage` and `app.CarInGarage.Action`, describes `CurrentAction` and `NextAction` (type, text, the method held, what it is bound to), reads the state machine both ways (with both objects' addresses, so it shows whether they are the same object), and reads the two controllers' fields.
- **10. The actor probe** looked at only 3 objects of a type (the car probe's limit: players and enemies counted), so a busy scene hid the rest; it takes up to 80 now. Each object with a state machine keeps a `fsm_trail`: its states in the order they came, with the second and the story number (the last 80), instead of only the last state and a count.
- **11. The build label is 10.27** (script `BUILD`, `defaults.json` build "10.27"; the checks that name the label were moved with it). The release stays 0.11.

**What to check at home (rebuild re7mp.exe first):** (a) play as before with "Collect by itself every 20 s" ticked: the hitch every 20 s should be gone while you run and fight (a scan comes in menus, cutscenes and when you stand still, and at the latest after 2 minutes); (b) save the game at a tape recorder with a friend there: no stall of 200 to 600 ms (the file's `saving_flag.waits` shows the waits); (c) with a friend in the garage, mode 3 (the default): get in the car: the first state change in `garage_car.transitions` is no longer an empty text, `garage_car.fsm.via` says the route, and the other game's player gets in too; (d) `story_flow.number_read_by` says `get_CastS32` (or another) and every part has its number; (e) in the garage press the car probe once before the fight and once in it, and switch the actor probe on for the whole fight.

**What could not be tested here.** All of it ran against the fake game. The real calls the new code makes for the first time are the number holder's `get_CastS32`, the delegate's `get_Method().get_Name()`, `isWindowBroken(n)`, the Fsm's address, and the two controllers; a call that the game refuses is caught and written down (`field_answer`, the probe's `text` and `type`), it does not stop anything. The cost of the cheaper scan in the real game is not known until the next file (`model_collection.scan_cost`).

**Tests.** Lua: `tools/lua_smoke_1027.lua` (32 checks: the walk finds the same objects, calm-moment gating, the save waits, the four net parts, the version); the car file `tools/lua_smoke_1019.lua` has a new section 10 (22 checks: the story number through `get_CastS32` / `get_S32` and a number that is not one, the state through the second route and unreadable on both, the windows by call, the action delegate, `HitWall`, a state nobody listed, the probe's 520 fields, the delegate description, both state routes, the controllers, the actor probe behind six enemies and its state trail). Eight deliberate breakages (number names, one state route, the end rule, the object limit, the field cap, the window fallback, `HitWall`, the trail) each made at least one of them fail. The older files' build label moved to 10.27. The C++ is unchanged.

## What changed in v10.26 (release **0.11** unchanged; protocol v6 unchanged; **script AND exe: rebuild re7mp.exe, it carries the script and `defaults.json`**)

Your 23 November request (16:39): "The alpha builds on the github are labelled as pre release and named along the likes of v0.11 (alpha 1,2, 3 etc) and should be treated as in between versions, for example the v0.13 will be treated as more recent than the v0.12 alpha 10. The ethans friend AI should no longer have the option to be beside you, the choice when it is selected should be follows or stays put, they should be taken out of the exe and it should be a hotkey in the game of F7 and it should say in the corner F7 to follow or stay to switch, this will clear the exe a bit". Then (16:42): "we can make it a different hotkey if theres a conflict".

**What changed**
- **1. Alpha releases are numbered: "v0.11 alpha 3".** The title or the tag says `v<release> alpha <n>` (`v0.11 alpha 3`, tag `v0.11-alpha-3`) and the release is flagged pre-release on GitHub. `<n>` is the alpha's own count for that release (1, 2, 3 ...), not the internal build. The old dotted form (`alpha 10.24`) is no longer an alpha number (no alpha was ever published); a release with the word "alpha" and a name that cannot be read is still never offered to a player on the normal releases, and the alpha channel skips it and says so. Code: `has_alpha_word`, `alpha_number_in`, `without_alpha_label` in `src/updater.cpp`; `ReleaseInfo` has `alpha` / `alpha_no` instead of `build`.
- **2. Alphas are in-between versions.** Releases are compared by release number first, then alpha number (a normal release is alpha 0): the normal **v0.13 is newer than "v0.12 alpha 10"**, alpha 10 is newer than alpha 9, 0.9 is older than 0.10. `compare_release` in `src/updater.cpp`. **The order inside one release number (changed in the same build, after you said v0.11 is not public yet): alpha n of v0.12 is OLDER than the normal v0.12** (the alphas lead up to the release they are named for, between the release before and that one). My first reading was the opposite; with it, a tester on "v0.11 alpha 5" would never have been offered the final v0.11. The other order is one switch: `kAlphaFollowsItsRelease = true` in `src/updater.hpp`; the tests pass for both (checked by building each).
- **3. A player on the alpha is offered the newest of ALL the releases.** Before, "Check now" on the alpha only looked at alpha builds, so an alpha tester was never told about a new normal release. Now an alpha tester on "v0.12 alpha 10" is offered the normal v0.12 when it comes out, or v0.13 ("v0.13 is available (you have v0.12 alpha 10)."), and stays on the alpha channel after installing it. Pre-releases that are not alphas are ignored; with no usable alpha published at all the button still says none is. The normal updater skips every release with the word alpha, as before. "Leave the alpha..." still goes to the newest normal release whatever its number. Code: `check_alpha(http, repo, version, alpha)` in `src/alpha_channel.hpp`; every window text now says "v0.11 alpha 3" (and "you have v0.11").
- **4. The script says which alpha it is: `ALPHA = "0"`.** `0` is a normal release; an alpha release says its number. re7mp.exe reads it out of its own copy of the script (`script_alpha`, like `BUILD`), the updater refuses a script in a release whose `RELEASE` or `ALPHA` does not match the title, the debug file has `alpha`, and `tools/pack_alpha.py` refuses `ALPHA = "0"` (a normal release), an exe built with another number and an exe from before this build. The zip is `re7mp-alpha-<release>-<n>.zip`, the text file `alpha-<release>-<n>-release.txt`. For a normal release `ALPHA` stays `"0"`.
- **5. Ethan's friend: the follows / stays put / beside you radios are gone from re7mp.exe, and "Beside you" is gone altogether** (`BotMode::Beside`, `--bot-beside`, the ini value). The row now has the label, "Move friend to me" (moved up next to it) and a one-line note that F5 does the rest. An old `bot=` line in `re7mp.ini` is ignored and removed at the next save.
- **6. F5 in the game: the friend follows you or stays put.** Press it and the friend stays where it stands (it is not moved to you); press it again and it walks after you again. The script sends kind 11 `mode:follow` / `mode:stay` at once and again every 10 s (so a friend started later, or an exe that missed one, gets the current choice); the exe obeys (`bot_mode_command`, `src/bot_logic.hpp`; `src/engine.cpp`). **The key is F5, not F7: F7 is already the item "Give" key** (and its hints say "[F7] give ..."), so you said a different key is fine; F5 was free. A line in the top right corner says which it is: "Ethan's friend follows you  (F5 = stay put)" / "Ethan's friend stays put  (F5 = follow you)". It shows only while the friend is the other player; with a real player F5 does nothing and nothing is sent.
- **7. The alpha is named for the release it is heading to (your 17:17 sequence): v0.11 stable, then v0.12 alpha 1, 2, 3 ..., then v0.12 stable, then v0.13 alpha 1 ... .** No change to the order logic (alpha n of 0.12 is newer than v0.11 and older than v0.12: the order set earlier today). What it means for publishing: v0.11 is built with `ALPHA = "0"` and carries the button; the first alpha bumps `RELEASE` in the script AND `kReleaseVersion` in `src/version.hpp` to 0.12 and sets `ALPHA = "1"`. **The packer now checks the release number too:** it refuses a tree where `src/version.hpp` and the script's `RELEASE` differ (an exe built like that would call itself "v0.11 alpha 1", see "v0.12 alpha 1" as newer and offer itself the same update forever; the C++ tests already compared the two, the packer did not), and an exe whose embedded script carries another `RELEASE`. (Earlier advice from me to publish "v0.11 alpha 1" before v0.11 was wrong for this sequence.)
- **8. The build label is 10.26** (script `BUILD`, `defaults.json` build "10.26"; the checks that name the label were moved with it). The release stays 0.11.

**What to check at home (rebuild re7mp.exe first):** in the window, the friend row has "Move friend to me" and the F5 note and nothing overlaps; play with Ethan's friend: the line is in the top right, F5 flips it and the friend stops where it stands, F5 again and it follows; F7 still gives an item; try F5 with the friend far away (it should walk after you, not jump). For the alpha: after v0.11 stable is public: set `RELEASE = "0.12"` (script) and `kReleaseVersion = "0.12"` (`src/version.hpp`), `ALPHA = "1"`, rebuild, `python tools/pack_alpha.py --exe re7mp.exe`, publish as described in `dist/alpha-0.12-1-release.txt`, and press "Join the alpha..." from a plain exe.

**What could not be tested here.** The window code (`src/gui_win.cpp`) cannot be built on this machine: it was only syntax-checked against stand-in Windows headers, and the layout was checked by the script that reads every control's position out of the source (nothing outside the 760 x 870 window; the only new overlap pair is the Steam ID / peer fields, which never show together). Nothing was seen in the real game: the F5 key, the corner line and the friend's stop are tested only against the fake game.

**Tests.** C++: the whole suite passes (new: `RE7MP_ONLY_BOT=1` runs the friend tests quickly; the mode tests walk the player AWAY from the friend, because the old fake player walked toward it and a follower just stands; `test_alpha_channel` was rewritten for alpha numbers, your v0.13 / v0.12 alpha 10 example, and the script label). Three deliberate breakages of the new code (the alpha number ignored in the order, the normal updater no longer skipping alphas, the alpha channel ignoring normal releases) were each caught. Lua: the whole suite, 6,983 checks, all pass (new file `tools/lua_smoke_1026.lua`, 16 checks; the first full run had one failure: the 10.24 channel checks used exe build "10.25" to mean "the same build as the script", a stale fixture, now "10.26"; `RE7MP_ONLY_1024=1` re-run 33 of 33). Packer: `python tools/test_pack_alpha.py` 25 checks (new: an exe with another RELEASE, a version.hpp that disagrees with the script, and a full pack of "v0.12 alpha 1"; removing either new guard made exactly its case fail).

## What changed in v10.25 (release **0.11** unchanged; protocol v6 unchanged; **exe only: rebuild re7mp.exe. The script's code did not change: only its `BUILD` label (and `defaults.json`'s) say 10.25**)

Your 23 November request (15:26): "lets make the exe bigger, a bigger window and a toggle at the top for biorand and a warning that it is not implemented yet and we are just preparing ... we also should add a VR toggle for the future too with the same warning, you dont need to break the code very much, we just need to prepare the exe for the future and get the alpha ready, im planning to make the alpha a pre release version on the github releases and make the normal release the stable".

**What changed**
- **1. A bigger window with a new strip at the top.** 760 x 870 (it was 680 x 750). Every old control is the same control moved down by exactly 80 (the strip's height); the ones that used to reach the right edge are 80 wider (the folder, peer and Steam fields, the status values, the update row, the sync boxes, "Move friend to me", the log). The log is 190 high (was 150) and is made shorter, down to 70, on a small or scaled screen so that the window still fits above the task bar. (At 150% scaling on a 1080p screen no version of this window fits: the old one did not either.)
- **2. Two "coming later" boxes in the strip: "BioRand 7 randomizer" and "VR support", under the line "NOT IMPLEMENTED YET - we are only preparing".** They start off, are saved in `re7mp.ini` as `biorand=0|1` and `vr=0|1`, and do nothing else: nothing is written for the script, the game or the other player.
- **3. Ticking one opens a warning window** (OK only): not implemented yet, we are only preparing, it only saves your choice, nothing was changed in your game; the BioRand text adds that playing a BioRand 7 seed together with this mod has not been tried, and the VR text that nothing about VR has been built or tried. Unticking says nothing. The log notes every click, and the next start says "Coming later: ... ticked (from last time)" so that a report that says "it did nothing" has its reason.
- **4. The alpha is linked to the main repository, `kingkiller1195/re7mp`.** An alpha is a release there whose title or tag says `alpha <build>` and that is flagged **pre-release**; the normal (stable) release is one that is not. GitHub's own "latest" skips pre-releases and the updater skips anything labelled alpha even when the flag was forgotten, so normal players never see an alpha. "Join the alpha..." now looks at that repository; until the first alpha is published it says so and changes nothing.
- **5. The packer checks the link properly.** 10.24 only looked for the repository's name inside the exe, and the normal updater's own web addresses contain it, so an exe built BEFORE the link would have passed. The link is now one text inside the exe, `re7mp-alpha-link=owner/name`, and `tools/pack_alpha.py` refuses an exe without it, with an empty one, or with another repository than the source says (`--allow-unlinked` still packs one for a trial). `tools/test_pack_alpha.py` checks it with made-up exes (11 checks).
- **6. The build label is 10.25** (script `BUILD`, `defaults.json` build "10.25"; the checks that name the label were moved with it). The release stays 0.11.

**What to check at home (rebuild re7mp.exe first):** the window opens bigger, nothing overlaps and nothing is cut off at the right or the bottom; the strip at the top shows "Coming later:", the two boxes and the "NOT IMPLEMENTED YET" line over a thin divider; every old control is where it was, only lower. Tick "BioRand 7 randomizer": a warning window opens (OK only), the log says it is saved only, and `re7mp.ini` has `biorand=1`. Untick it: no window, `biorand=0`. The same for "VR support". Close and reopen: a box that was ticked is still ticked and the log says so once. Then "Join the alpha...": before an alpha release exists it should say that none is published at github.com/kingkiller1195/re7mp yet.

**What could not be tested here.** The window code (`src/gui_win.cpp`) cannot be built on this machine: it was only syntax-checked against stand-in Windows headers, and its layout was checked by a script that reads every control's position out of the source (everything inside the 760 x 870 area; all 58 old controls moved by exactly 80; the only new overlap is the Steam friend field over the TCP peer field, which share a row and are never shown together, as before). A real compile may still show something, and how it LOOKS (the divider, the two-line warning, the wider fields) only shows on your screen. What is tested on its own, with deliberately broken copies to prove the checks catch it: the texts and ini rules of the boxes, the window and log-height arithmetic, the link and the packer's guards.

**Tests.** C++: the whole suite passes (new: `test_future_ui`, and the link checks in `test_alpha_channel`; `RE7MP_ONLY_ALPHA=1` runs both quickly). Seven deliberate breakages of the new code (an ini value that is accepted wrongly, the VR warning losing its words, a log that never shrinks, a strip height that disagrees with the window code, an empty link, a link to another repository, a default repository not cut from the link text) were all caught. `python tools/test_pack_alpha.py`: 11 of 11, and a deliberately weakened packer (one that accepts an exe without the link text) fails two of them. Script: the whole regression, 6,967 checks, 0 failed (the build label is the only change in the script).

## What changed in v10.24 (release **0.11** unchanged; protocol v6 unchanged; **script AND exe: rebuild re7mp.exe, it carries the script, `defaults.json` and the new button**)

Your 23 November requests: "...a public alpha branch where people can test new stuff as it comes out... we also need to write into the exe a button to opt into the alpha branch with a warning window that pops up with whats changed and a warning saying things will be even more broken or fixed, not uploaded everything yet but it should be there ready for me to tell you to link it", then "the stable build should be able to opt in to alpha". (The Google Drive backup you asked for first was dropped at your word: the Drive connector only takes small inline files, not a 46 MB zip.) **Nothing here has been seen on Windows or in the game: the exe's window code could not be compiled where it was written (see "What could not be tested").**

**What changed**
- **1. "Join the alpha..." is a button in re7mp.exe, in every build, the stable one included.** It is in the Updates row, second line, right of "Check for updates when this opens". Everybody starts on the normal releases; a stable player presses it to join. Until an alpha is linked (see 7) it shows the warning as a preview and installs nothing. Linked, it looks up the newest alpha build first (the button says "Looking..."), then asks in a window. If the look fails (no network, no alpha published) a window says why and nothing changes.
- **2. The window: "JOIN THE ALPHA?"** It shows WHAT'S CHANGED (the notes of the newest alpha release, the first 700 characters), then the warning: *expect things to be EVEN MORE BROKEN, or FIXED, compared with the normal release*; back up your saves; both players need the SAME alpha build; press "Open data folder" and send debug.json when something goes wrong (it records the build); you can leave any time. It names the source (`github.com/owner/name`) and asks. The default button is No. Yes: the choice is saved (`channel=alpha` in re7mp.ini), and when the newest alpha is newer than the build this exe carries it is downloaded and installed at once, like an update (exe, script and defaults; no second question), and the exe restarts. Already on the newest: it only switches the channel.
- **3. On the alpha, the Updates row looks for alpha builds.** "Check now" and the check at start ask the alpha source; the status row says "Alpha build 10.25 is available (you have 10.24)"; the button reads "Update to alpha 10.25" and asks the usual question first (with the release notes).
- **4. "Leave the alpha..."** (the same button once you are in). Yes: the newest NORMAL release is downloaded and put in place of the alpha build, and re7mp restarts (no downgrade tool was needed: the normal release has the same number, so the check is told to offer it whatever the number). No: stay on this build and stop looking for alpha builds (the next normal release replaces it). Cancel: nothing.
- **5. An alpha release is an ordinary GitHub release whose title or tag says `alpha <build>`** ("v0.11 alpha 10.24", tag `alpha-10.24`). Builds are compared as numbers (10.9 is below 10.24); the highest wins; drafts never. The script inside must say the same BUILD as the title, or it is not used (the alpha builds share a release number, so the build is the identity). **The normal updater never offers an alpha**, whatever its version or its pre-release flag, so the alpha can live in the same repository as the normal releases; and "latest" being an unflagged alpha no longer breaks the normal check (it falls back to the list).
- **6. The script says which channel and build it came from.** The exe writes `re7mp_channel.json` (`{"channel":"alpha","exe_build":"10.24"}`) into the game's data folder at Start, at program start and when the button is used. The script reads it every 10 s and only SAYS it: the debug file has `channel` and `exe_build` (and `health.channel_tick`, with `build_matches_exe`), and an alpha tester's panel has a line "ALPHA channel (re7mp.exe build 10.24): expect things to break...". Nothing in the game depends on it. Also: when the other player runs another BUILD of the same release, the panel and the log say so (a note, never a "problem"; a different release gets the old warning as before).
- **7. Linking it, and trying it first.** `kAlphaRepo` in `src/alpha_channel.hpp` is empty: tell me "link it" with the repository (`owner/name`) and it is one line, then rebuild. Without rebuilding, `alpha_repo=owner/name` under `[re7mp]` in re7mp.ini does the same (it beats the built-in one). **The next normal release has to be built from this code and from a build with the alpha linked**, or the stable players have a button that only previews.
- **8. `tools/pack_alpha.py`** packs an alpha release: `python tools/pack_alpha.py --exe C:\path\to\re7mp.exe` writes `dist/re7mp-alpha-10.24.zip` (re7mp.exe, lua/re7mp.lua, data/re7mp/defaults.json: what the updater reads) and `dist/alpha-10.24-release.txt` (the title, the tag, what to tick, and the notes to paste, made from the numbered items of this file). It REFUSES an exe that was not built from this script (the exe carries a copy of the script, so it can tell), a `defaults.json` of another build, and an alpha with no repository linked (`kAlphaRepo` empty, or an exe built before the link was set: such an exe would send every tester back to the normal releases the first time it starts; `--allow-unlinked` packs it anyway, for a trial).
- **9. The build label is 10.24** (script `BUILD`, `defaults.json` build "10.24"). The release stays 0.11.

**What to check at home (rebuild re7mp.exe first):** the window opens and the Updates row has a second button, "Join the alpha...", right of the checkbox, with nothing overlapping. Pressing it shows "THE ALPHA IS NOT OPEN YET" and a warning, OK only; nothing in re7mp.ini changes. Then put `alpha_repo=<owner>/<name>` in re7mp.ini (a repository with one release made by `python tools/pack_alpha.py --exe re7mp.exe --allow-unlinked`: the packer refuses an alpha with no linked repository otherwise, and this flag is for exactly this trial) and press it again: "Looking...", then the join window with that release's notes; No leaves everything as it was; Yes saves `channel=alpha` (a release of a LATER build than your exe also downloads and restarts; the same build only switches the channel and says "Up to date (alpha build X)"); the button now says "Leave the alpha..."; the log says "Alpha channel: ON". Start, then open RE7: the panel shows the ALPHA line and the debug file has `channel` and `exe_build`. "Leave the alpha...": Yes puts the normal release back.

**What could not be tested here.** The window code (`src/gui_win.cpp`) cannot be built on this machine: it was only syntax-checked against stand-in Windows headers (no errors in the new code; the stand-ins do not check Win32 signatures, so a real compile may still show something). The things that are tested on their own: how an alpha release is told from a normal one, the newest-alpha lookup, the repository-name check, the text of every window, the channel file, the alpha package check (`tests/core_tests.cpp`, `test_alpha_channel`; run it alone with `RE7MP_ONLY_ALPHA=1`), and the script's side (`tools/lua_smoke_1024.lua`: 33 checks; the whole script test is 6,967 checks, 0 failed). I broke the new code on purpose in 13 different ways (accepting any channel word, comparing builds as text, a "?" allowed in a repository name, the normal updater offering an alpha, ...) and the tests caught every one. **Not built, on purpose:** alpha defaults with the experimental boxes ticked (needs a decision on which ones are safe for strangers: see `claude/re7-alpha-plan.md`).

## What changed in v10.23 (release **0.11** unchanged; protocol v6 unchanged; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json`**, either by rebuilding re7mp.exe (it carries a copy of both) and pressing Install / update mod, or by copying the two files by hand)

Your 23 November request: "Move the enemies to the debug menu and get rid of the death execution log and test, Mia's hair physics don't matter since now we can move the real model over where we need her, get rid of that too." The release number is the same (0.11), so a player on build 10.22 and a player on this build still connect: the version check compares release numbers.

**What changed**
- **1. The Enemies section is in Developer tools now.** The whole section (194 lines) was moved and indented one level; every control, label and saved key is the same. It sits inside "Developer tools (for the mod's author ...)", after "Debug files for the developer" and before "Testing tools (dev build)". Both nodes are closed until you open them, so the enemy controls are two clicks away. **Why moving the controls changes nothing else:** the enemy sync mode is a setting (default 3, key `enemy_mode2`), not something the panel holds, and the player's own switch is the exe's box "Sync enemies and the Molded" (ticked by default). A new test proves it: with the panel never drawn, a joiner on mode 3 still has its own Em2000 moved onto the host's. The joiner's top line now says where the settings went: "(settings: Developer tools > Enemies)". Older sections of this file and the README that say "panel: Enemies" mean this place from now on.
- **2. The execution test is gone** (`RE7.exec`, 195 lines): the log of the grab, finisher and game-over calls and the option to block the game over and go down instead. Gone with it: the hooks it installed (the grab classes and the game-over screen), `pstat.can_catch` and `pstat.exec_catch`, `execution_test` in the debug file, its two boxes and status lines in the Death section, and its saved keys (an old settings file that has them is read without them). **Kept:** "Executions end the game for BOTH players" (the team wipe from 9.27). It decides from the health alone and installs no hook: a game over of a player who is not down, with a health above 1.5, is an execution (`death.looks_like_execution`). The only callback that still returns "skip" to the game is the downed player's healing-item block (`H.item_blocked`); that path was deliberately kept. The three "Test:" buttons in the Death section are still there.
- **3. Mia's hair physics are gone** (`CFG.hair`, 321 lines): the experiment that copied her chain physics onto a clone. Gone with it: the box, its status line, the "Read her hair components" button, `health.hair`, the file `hairstate.json`, the saved key `hair_physics` (also from the shipped defaults) and the crash-marker stage "hair" (an old marker is passed over silently). **Kept:** Mia's head parts (the donors) and "Hide hair parts". What you can see: Mia as an avatar has the still mesh's hair (no swing). Your reason: the real model will be moved to where she is needed instead.
- **4. Shipped defaults:** `defaults.json` build "10.23" and no `hair_physics` (50 settings keys now); `tools/build_defaults.py` has `hair_physics` in NEVER_SHIP, so a rebuild from your own settings file cannot bring it back.
- **5. The build label is 10.23.** Ask for 0.12 if you want a new release number.

**What to check at home (the new script on both PCs):** the panel title says v0.11 and the build says 10.23. Main panel: *Items* is followed by *Story sync*, with no *Enemies* node. Developer tools > Enemies has the enemy-sync combo and the boxes you know. The Death section has no "Execution test" lines but still has "Executions end the game for BOTH players" and the three "Test:" buttons. A joiner without Developer tools opened still has its enemies moved onto the host's (watch Mia or Jack in the garage fight). If a hang or crash ends a session, send both debug files and `lastcall.json`.

**Tests.** New file `tools/lua_smoke_1023.lua` (72 checks, `RE7MP_ONLY_1023=1`): version and build, where the Enemies node is (inside Developer tools, in order, and not offered when Developer tools is shut), the joiner and host hint lines, mode 3 working with the panel never drawn, source guards (none of the execution-test or hair names are left; the team wipe, the "skip" path and `mia_head_parts` are), and old settings, markers and files from before. In the main harness the 9.18 execution-test blocks (about 214 lines) and the hair block (226 lines) are replaced by short "it is gone" blocks (62 and 74 lines). Full run: **6,934 checks, 0 failed** (6,935 before). Seven deliberate breakages (the Enemies node renamed, the joiner hint changed, `CFG.hair`, `app.Em3000Grapple` and `RE7.exec` named again, the "skip" path renamed, the team-wipe debug section renamed) were each caught by exactly the check meant for it. No C++ change.

**Not done / limits.** The exe is not rebuilt here (Windows). Things that look like testing tools but are still in the menu, not on your list: the Death section's three "Test:" buttons, the "Receive tests" buttons in *Items*, *Items: journal* (off by default) and *Story sync* (EXPERIMENTAL). They can move under Developer tools in one more pass.

## What changed in v10.22 (release **0.11**, the hotfix; protocol v6 unchanged; **script AND exe: rebuild re7mp.exe, it carries the script, `defaults.json` and the release number inside it**)

Your 22 November request: "lets clean up the debug ready for release and get the defaults right, I may have released v0.10 without clearing this up and getting it right, so lets bump to 0.11 for a quick hotfix and set the defaults right, lets get rid of the cutscene recorder and the spawn log for mias hair, lets leave the door sync on as default and remove the option to turn it off and get rid of its tab in the menu and lets put ghosts and move my enemies onto the hosts (experimental) as on by default and get rid of the experimental label. Lets merge the sync pickups tab and the items give and drop tab to just "items" as a universal tab". Everything below is unverified in the real game (the tests use a fake game).

**What changed**
- **1. The release is 0.11 (build 10.22).** `CFG.RELEASE = "0.11"` in the script and `kReleaseVersion = "0.11"` in `src/version.hpp` (a C++ test checks that the two agree; it passes). **Why the exe has to be rebuilt:** `re7mp.exe` carries the script, `defaults.json` and the models inside it (CMake embeds them), and the two players' scripts compare release numbers. A new script with the old exe would show "re7mp.exe is v0.10 but this script is v0.11" in the panel. Build the exe, and give both players that one.
- **2. The cutscene recorder is gone** (the panel section "Cutscene probe", `CFG.cs`, 314 lines). Gone with it: the call that fed it from the story hook, its once-a-frame update, the settings keys `cutscene_probe`, `cutscene_secs`, `cutscene_link`, `cutscene_stop_loco`, `cutscene_loco_grace` (an old settings file that still has them is read without them and written back without them), and the `health.cutscenes` section of the debug file. **Not touched:** the F10 clip recorder and the *Animation clips* section (they are the body's own recorder), and the *Story sync* with its `requestEvent` hook (the recorder only borrowed that hook; the story sync installs it for itself). Files it wrote earlier (`re7mp/cutscenes/` and `re7mp/clips/cs_*.json`) stay on disk and do nothing; delete them if you like.
- **3. The spawn log is gone** (the panel section "Spawn log: Mia's spawn, the methods and files, hair physics", `CFG.slog`, 1,123 lines). It hooked a few dozen of the game's spawn and load methods and looked at every object with a Motion component once a second, so removing it also removes those hooks and that scan. Gone with it: the keys `spawn_log` and `spawn_focus`, `health.spawn_log`, the file names `spawnlog`, `spawntext` and `spawnstate`, and the crash marker stage `"spawnlog"` (a `lastcall.json` left by an old session with that stage is now passed over silently). Old files `re7mp/spawnlog.json`, `spawn_log.txt`, `spawnstate.json` stay on disk, harmless. **Not touched:** *Mia: hair physics* in the "Other player's body" section (the experiment that copies her chain component; it ships unticked and has its own crash marker). Say if that one should go too.
- **4. Door sync is always on, with no box and no tab.** The section "Doors (shared with the other player)" is gone. The saved key `door_sync` is no longer written or read, so an old `door_sync: false` cannot switch the doors off. `CFG.door.on` is a constant `true`; the `if not D.on` branches in the door code are left in place (they can no longer be reached). `health.doors` stays in the debug file, because that is how a door that did not open is looked into.
- **5. Enemy sync mode 3 ("Ghosts, and move my own enemies onto the host's") is the default and has lost its "(EXPERIMENTAL)".** `enemy.mode` starts at 3. The label of the mode, the line "EXPERIMENTAL: this moves your own enemies about. Use a throwaway save." and the "(experimental)" in the section title ("Enemies (experimental)" is now "Enemies") are gone. **A new settings key, `enemy_mode2`:** the old key `enemy_mode` held 2 for every player (the old default was saved with the rest of the settings), so reading it would have left everybody on ghosts only. It is no longer read or written. Consequence to know: **everyone starts on mode 3 the first time they run 0.11, even a player who had picked ghosts only; from then on their own choice is remembered.** The loader no longer throws a saved 3 away at the start (it did, "the experimental mode is never switched on by itself"). `defaults.json` ships `"enemy_mode2": 3`. The other experiments in that section keep their own labels and stay as they were (the boss health, the Molded copies, the hand-over and the clip playback are all still off or optional as before). The exe's own box "Sync enemies and the Molded" (ticked by default) already set mode 3 when it ran; now the script agrees without it. Unticking that box still gives mode 2.
- **6. One "Items" section.** *Items: sync pickups* and *Items: give and drop* are one section, **Items**, where the pickups tab was (before *Enemies*): the pickups half under a "PICKUPS:" line, a separator, then the give and drop half under "GIVE AND DROP:". Every control is the same as before and keeps its saved key; each half is still drawn inside its own error guard, so one half failing cannot blank the other. The separate *Items: journal of pickups and inventory changes* section (off by default) was not part of the merge and is still there.
- **7. Tests that said "the default is mode 2".** Six old checks and the stand-in model worlds assumed the old default; they now choose mode 2 on purpose (the 10.15 pool check, the 10.16 car probe, the 10.19 garage car gate, the 9.31 / 10.21 enemy-model worlds). That is also a real consequence worth knowing: in mode 3 the joiner's own enemy IS the picture, so the stand-in model (the T pose) is never made over it (10.21), and the garage car sync (mode 3 only) looks for the car every 2 s whenever the story number cannot be read.
- **Shipped defaults:** `defaults.json` loses `cutscene_probe`, `cutscene_link`, `cutscene_secs` and `enemy_mode`, gains `enemy_mode2: 3`, build 10.22. `tools/build_defaults.py` now has `enemy_mode2: 3` in `RELEASE_OVERRIDES` and the old keys in `NEVER_SHIP`, so a rebuild from your own settings file cannot bring them back.

**What to check at home (both players, the new exe and script):** the panel title says v0.11 and "Release v0.11 (script build 10.22 ...)"; Insert > RE7 Multiplayer shows **Items** (one section) and no Doors section; *Enemies* says "Enemy sync" on the third entry without "EXPERIMENTAL"; a door opened by one player opens for the other; a Mia fight shows no second model inside her; the Developer tools section has no cutscene or spawn log entries. Send both debug files if anything looks wrong.

**Tests.** New file `tools/lua_smoke_1022.lua` (102 checks: version, the menu on both roles, every section opened is closed exactly once (the real ImGui crashes on a TreePop that does not match; the fake one never noticed, so the test counts), the controls of both Items halves, an old settings file with every removed key, an old `spawnlog` crash marker, the source text has no `CFG.slog` / `CFG.cs`, mode 3 default / kept / old key ignored / damaged values / host). In `tools/lua_smoke.lua`: the cutscene probe tests (about 430 lines) and the spawn log scenario (287 lines) are gone; the door tests now say "always on"; the shipped-defaults test checks `enemy_mode2`; the version strings are 0.11 / 10.22. Full run: **6,935 checks, 0 failed** (7,330 before; the removed features took about 500 checks with them). C++ tests: all pass (including the one that the script and `version.hpp` agree on 0.11). **Deliberate breakages:** 25 changes to the script (mode default back to 2, the old key read again, a saved 3 thrown away, the experimental label and warning back, the Items tab split or its headings dropped, the Doors tab or the door switch back, the old keys written again, a spawn log or cutscene section back in the debug file, release 0.10, build 10.21, doors off by default ...) were each caught by the new file; two more were run through the whole harness and caught there too (the door switch read again: four checks; `defaults.json` with `enemy_mode2` at 2: the shipped-defaults check); and a section that is never closed, and one that is closed twice, were caught by the new tree check. Nothing survived.

**Not done / limits.** The exe is not rebuilt here (Windows). Other things that look like testing tools but are still in the player's sections and were not part of your list: the *Enemies* section's probes and test buttons (garage car probe, actor probe, targeting log, "Write enemy details", "Trace enemy damage", "Test: hit the red box"), the "Receive tests" buttons in *Items*, *Execution test* in *Death*, the *Items: journal* section, *Story sync* (labelled EXPERIMENTAL) and *Mia: hair physics*. They can move under *Developer tools* in one more pass if you want.

## What changed in v10.21 (release **0.10**; protocol v6 unchanged; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json`; the exe stays**)

Your 22 November request, with a screenshot of the Mia fight in which a T-posed stand-in stood on top of the real Mia: "this t pose as the stand in for hosts enemies should be gotten rid of since it already hijacks the joiners game enemies to sync with host or whoever the enemy is targeting at the moment, since we can tell which gun does what damage we can sync the damage of each gun, the molded also have not got synced limb and head removal, killing one persons molded should kill both persons regardless of host or joiner status".

**What the screenshot shows.** In mode 3 the joiner's own Mia is moved onto the host's Mia, so she is the picture. The "Enemy models" feature (9.31) did not know that: it made a model of the host's `Em2000` as well, with no animation, so it stood there in its rest pose (the T) inside the real one. Nothing asked "does this game already have a real body of it?"

**What changed (all of it unverified in the real game)**
- **1. The T-pose stand-in is gone.** Two changes, so that it cannot come back by accident:
  - *Off by default.* The *Enemy models* box starts unticked. It is remembered under a new settings key, `pup_on2`; the old key `pup_on` held "true" for everybody (it was the default that got saved) and is no longer read, so nobody keeps the stand-ins switched on by an old file.
  - *Never over a real body.* New `enemy.real_body(name, now)`: true when mode 3 moved my own enemy of that name onto the host's within the last 1.5 s (`enemy.driven[name].moved_at`), or a Molded body of this game is the copy of it (`CFG.molded.copies`). `CFG.pup.pick` skips those enemies, and `draw_enemies` draws no red box over them either (the name label stays, so you still see what it is). An enemy my game has no body for (the host's enemy has not spawned here) is still the red box with its label.
  - The debug file's `health.enemy_models` has `real_body_there_so_no_model` (which enemies were skipped for this reason, and when), `remembered_choice` and `off_by_the_crash_marker`.
- **2. Damage by gun is on by default.** It was built in 9.31 (your table `CFG.wpn.DAMAGE`, 22 guns, times the gun scale 2.1) but switched off, so every shot sent the same flat number. Now one M19 shot sends 120 x 2.1 = 252 instead of 100 x 2.1 = 210, a magnum 1200 x 2.1, and so on; the host divides by the ratio it measured for that enemy, as before. Saved under `hit_by_gun2` (the old key held "false" for everybody and is not read). A gun that is not in the table (the knife, anything unnamed) falls back to the flat number and is counted: `health.enemy_hits.by_gun` has `sent_by_gun` (shots and the last damage per gun), `flat_number_shots`, `by_gun_on_but_no_known_gun_shots` and `last_gun`, and the panel shows "Last shot used: ... Sent by gun so far: ...". Headshot or weak-point multipliers are not known and are not sent; melee is not per-weapon.
- **3. A kill counts for both players (new message kind 21).** The host's death already killed the joiner's copy (the health pool, 10.15). New: if MY copy of the host's enemy dies here first (my own shot; for a Molded copy or an enemy mode 3 moved), I wait 0.7 s (the host's own death may be one snapshot away), then ask the host (`k|name|x|z`, with the host's own place for it) to kill its own. The host answers `a|name|ok|how` or `a|name|no|why`. Rules, each checked by a test: asked again after 2.5 s without an answer, four times at most; a plain refusal is final but "not now" (a cutscene there) is asked again; never a body that was already dead when first seen (an older save); never when the host's enemy is dead already; **never a boss** (Mia, Jack: kill writes during scripted fights crashed the game before; checked on both sides); nothing during a cutscene, a load or the seconds after (`CFG.guard`); a dead copy is no longer moved onto the host's and no longer plays the host's clips; the host refuses when it is not the host, the box is off, "hits from the other player" is off, the enemy is handed over to the joiner, a crash marker is set, more than 6 a second, or no such enemy. The host's kill is one hit far above the enemy's health (4 x its health + 1000, at least 3000) through the same `newFakeDamageInfo` + `addManualDamage` the joiner's shots already use, up to 3 times until `enemy_is_dead` says dead. A crash marker (`stage "kill"`) is written before it and cleared after; a session that ended inside one switches kill sharing off at the next start (tick the box to try again). Box: *Share kills* (remembered as `kill_sync`). Debug: `health.kill_sync` (counts, every copy watched with why it stopped, the last 14 lines).
- **4. Molded limbs and heads (new message kind 22) - PARTLY GUESSWORK.** The game keeps lost parts as `app.MoldedActionController.LostPartsUnit.Type` (Head 0, LeftArm 1, RightArm 2, LeftLeg 3, RightLeg 4, Body 5, Blade 6) with classes `app.EnemyLostPartsController`, `...LostPartsWork` and `Em4000/4100/4200LostPartsUserData`, but the dump I have lists the names of those types, not their members. So the script does not guess names. It reads the type definitions (one per half second, no live object needed) into **`re7mp/limb_probe.json`** (send me this file), and acts only through two exact shapes of method it can recognise on each Molded body's controller (`app.Em4000ActionController`, `app.MoldedActionController`, `...EnemyLostPartsController`):
  - *read:* a method with `Lost` in its name that takes one part type and returns a Boolean (an "is this part lost" question), asked for Head and the four limbs; or a list of `LostPartsWork` objects with an `IsLost`-like flag (the part comes from the object's own type field, not its place in the list).
  - *remove:* a method with `Lost` in its name that takes one part type and is not `is/has/get/can/check/on`; up to 3 candidates in order of how likely the name is (`request...` first), the next one only when the part did not read as lost 1.5 s after the call.
  - A part lost on one side is sent as `l|name|mask` (bit = 1 << type; cumulative, once per change and again every 10 s so a copy that was not there yet gets it); the other side removes the missing parts, waits up to 30 s for a body that is not there yet, never echoes what it was told, and skips a dead body, the Body and the Blade, a boss, a cutscene or a load. **With no way to READ the lost parts nothing is sent or removed**, and the debug file says plainly which methods it found: `health.limb_sync.found` lists, per controller type, the "is it lost" methods and the "make it lost" candidates it saw. If the game offers neither, you get the probe file and I wire the real names next time. A crash marker (`stage "limb"`) switches it off at the next start. Box: *Share Molded limbs and heads* (remembered as `limb_sync`). Debug: `health.limb_sync` (counts, what was found, what each body reads as lost, what was told and heard, what each part's removal did, the last 30 events).
- **5. Two older bugs the tests turned up (not part of your request).** (a) A crash marker from the making of an enemy model only switched the models off when nothing was remembered; with a remembered "on" (after the first run that was always) it was ignored, so a crash while making a model did not stop the next start from making one. The marker now wins for that session, and ticking the box clears it. (b) When the loader switched itself off after a crash, that "off" was never written to a settings file that already existed (the file's saved key was made after the marker had been applied), so the session after next had it on again. It is written now.
- **Debug file:** new `health.kill_sync` and `health.limb_sync`; `health.enemy_models.real_body_there_so_no_model`; `health.enemy_hits.by_gun`. New file `re7mp/limb_probe.json`. `CFG.BUILD = "10.21"`, `defaults.json` has build 10.21.

**What to send me next:** the debug file of both players after a fight (a Molded fight if you can; Mia's too, to see that no stand-in is drawn), and `re7mp/limb_probe.json` from either. In the debug file read: `health.enemy_models.real_body_there_so_no_model` (the skipped enemies), `health.enemy_hits.by_gun` (what each gun sent), `health.kill_sync` (did the asks get answered, and what did the host say), `health.limb_sync.found` and `.events` (did it find a way to read and to remove).

**Tests.** A new file, `tools/lua_smoke_1021.lua` (122 checks: both sides of the kill message, every refusal, the cutscene hold, the crash marker, the limb read and remove with four shapes of fake controller, the fallback candidate, no echo, a body that is not there yet, the probe file), the stand-in and per-gun checks in `tools/lua_smoke.lua`, a hand-over check in `tools/lua_smoke_1018.lua`; **7,330 checks in all, 0 failed** (7,141 before). Deliberate breaks of the new code (each must fail at least one check): 65 for kill and limb (62 caught by the new file, one more by the 10.18 file; two not caught on purpose: "nearest of two enemies with the same name" needs two live same-named enemies in the fake game, and the limb hand-over guard cannot be reached because the hand-over never takes a Molded), and 11 for the stand-in, loader and per-gun changes (10 caught; the 1.5 s freshness window of `enemy.real_body` is not, because the test world moves its enemies every frame). The first runs of the breaks found five gaps in the tests (the host with hits off, the host's own crash marker, a crash marker beating a remembered "on", the loader's "off" written to an existing file, ticking the box clearing the marker); they are closed.

**Not done / limits.** Knife and melee damage are not per weapon, headshot and weak-point multipliers are unknown, the limb methods are unverified (see 4), and a joiner's kill of an enemy the HOST runs through the hand-over is refused on purpose (the joiner runs those). Nothing in 10.16 to 10.21 has run in the real game.

## What changed in v10.20 (release **0.10**; protocol v6 unchanged; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json`; the exe stays**)

Your 22 November request: "look into what performance issues the mod may have", with the previous debug files, and the autosaves, which "can hitch the frame rate a little even though the mod doesn't use them". The files are the 20 November pair from build 10.15 (host 5,480 s, 311,441 frames; joiner 1,764 s, 282,767 frames), so nothing since 10.16 is in them.

**What the 10.15 files show**
- **The host runs about 57 frames a second and the script takes 4.7 ms of each frame (plus 0.7 ms in the game-phase tick); 4.45 % of its frames take over 16 ms, against 0.12 % on the joiner (160 frames a second, 1.5 ms).** The joiner's PC is faster, so the same work shows less.
- **The file bridge is about 12.5 % of every second on BOTH PCs** (`net send/read`: 679 s of 5,480 on the host, 222 s of 1,764 on the joiner). The script talks to re7mp.exe through small files: a pose write about 19 to 25 times a second, a pose read about 28 and an inbox read about 26 (host). That is about 70 file operations a second at about 1.7 ms each, the same on both PCs. The host's number looks three times worse because it draws a third of the frames. 45 % of the host's frames over 8 ms are in this part (17,602 of 39,515).
- **The host's inbox was in "active" mode 98.5 % of the time** (145,309 reads in 5,480 s, only 2,237 left out by the 10.15 throttle): messages come all the time there, and active mode read the inbox (up to 32 messages) on every pass.
- **The five slowest frames of the host (596, 454, 451, 438, 409 ms) are all in `saves+settings`.** That part has `read_saves` (the exe's file, every second), `saves.watch` (61 questions to the game, `isExistData` for slots 0 to 60, every 5 s, with or without a partner, even alone) and the Load-screen labels. Only 8 frames in 311,441 took over 8 ms there, so the calls are normally cheap and now and then stall, which is what a disk wait looks like (the save folder is where the game writes its autosave, where Steam Cloud looks and where `c:/program files (x86)` is watched by the antivirus). **Not proven:** the files hold no save lines, so a stall cannot be matched to an autosave by time.
- **The debug file is a regular hitch of its own:** 396 writes on the host, average 34.3 ms (building 6.9 ms, writing 27 to 41 ms for a 1.25 MB file) every 15 s, worst 400 ms (three of the twelve slowest frames: 400, 346 and 248 ms). The build also asked the game for all 61 slots on every write.
- **The enemy scene scan** (once a second, host only) has the worst single outlier of the script's own work (305 ms) and its part is over 8 ms in 5,440 frames; the snapshots (15 a second) are in the same part, so the file could not say which. `game-phase tick` has a 705 ms outlier (the creating of a model) and 5,479 frames over 8 ms; its six jobs were one number. `my model` (4,768 frames over 8 ms) and `menu/who/weapon/inventory` were also lumps.
- **What the files can NOT show: a hitch the game makes.** The recorder timed only the script. An autosave that stalls the game for 200 ms is a long wait between two frames with no script time in it, so it was invisible. That is the missing half of your autosave question.

**What changed (all of it unverified in the real game)**
- **1. The frame-gap recorder (`health.perf.frame_gaps`).** Every frame start is compared with the one before: `gap_ms` = the wall time between them, `ours_ms` = what the script used in the earlier frame (all its parts and the game-phase callbacks), `other_ms` = the rest (the game, the driver, other programs). Gaps of 60 ms and more are listed in time order (the last 30, `recent`) and the 12 biggest (`worst`), each with the part that was the script's slowest (`ours_top`), `loading` (the game says it is loading a scene), `menu` (a game menu open) and `saving`. Counts by size (50, 100, 250, 500, 1000 ms) and, for gaps over 100 ms, how many were mostly not the script (`over_100ms_mostly_not_the_script`) and how many were (`over_100ms_mostly_the_script`). **Reading it:** a long gap with a small `ours_ms` is the game, a long gap with a large `ours_ms` is the script and `ours_top` names the part.
- **2. The game's saving flag.** `app.SaveDataManager.get_NowSaving` (named in the 9.x type dump, never read before) is read on every frame; `health.perf.frame_gaps.saving_flag` lists `saving began` / `saving ended` with when and how long (`events`, the last 30), how often it began (`times_began`) and whether the flag works (`supported`; after five answers that are not true or false in a row it is given up and `supported` is false). It may be the wrong flag for the autosave or true only after the stall; if it never turns true, tick *9+ also trace save and load calls* and press 9 (the trace lines carry the time) and compare them with `recent`.
- **3. Finer parts, so the next file names the culprit instead of a lump.** `saves+settings` is now `saves: exe file`, `saves: slot watch`, `saves: load screen labels`, `settings write`; `menu/who/weapon/inventory` is `menu`, `who`, `weapon`, `inventory`; `my model (real)` is `my model: real.frame`, `my model: motion bank`, `my model: low camera + settle`; `doors` is split into `world modules` (the probes and the sync modules) and `doors`; the host's enemy part is `enemy host: scene scan (once a second)` and `enemy host: snapshots + file`; the game-phase tick has a lap for each of its six jobs (`phase: ...`, which also appear next to the whole `game-phase tick`). The table keeps 48 parts (was 24). The laps are not counted twice in `ours_ms`.
- **4. The saves, which is the part you asked about.**
  - **re7mp.exe's save file is read every 5 s, not every second** (every 15 s while nothing is shared and nobody is connected, every 2 s while the REFramework menu is open, so the panel is fresh when you look). A new "Sent your save to the other player" line in the chat can take up to 5 s (15 s the first time you tick *Share saves* in the exe). Nothing else needs it sooner: the Load-screen labels use the list that is already read.
  - **The game's save slots are asked for slots 0 to 20, not 0 to 60** (the game lists 15 at most; the exe never installs past 15): 21 questions a scan, not 61.
  - **Only while somebody is connected is the scan every 5 s; alone it is every 60 s** (only to forget a save you deleted). The first look after a partner appears only takes the picture, so a save you made BEFORE they came is not marked "[CO-OP with Bob]". A mark that is waiting for its 6 s keeps the quick look going even if the partner leaves.
  - **One scan serves everybody:** the panel (2 s, from a scan up to 2 s old) and the debug file (a scan up to 10 s old) no longer ask the game on their own. `health.coop_saves.slot_scans` says how many scans, how many questions, the average and the longest, how many answers came from the cache, how many times the exe's file was read, and lists every scan that took 30 ms or more with when it happened.
- **5. The inbox in active mode:** at least 0.07 s between reads (was 0: every pass), and active for 1 s after a message (was 2 s). About ten to fourteen reads a second on the host instead of 26; a message waits at most about 0.1 s more. Quiet mode (0.1 s) is as before. `health.bridge_reads.message_inbox_poll` shows the new numbers.
- **6. The debug file in play is written at most every 30 s** (was 15 s). With the REFramework menu open it is still every 2 s, and *Save the debug file now* writes it at once. After a crash up to 30 s of the file's lines are missing; the same lines are in REFramework's own log (`re2_framework_log.txt`), and `lastcall.json` is untouched. It is a number in `CFG.dbgf.play_s` if you want it shorter.
- **7. Build label 10.20** (script, `defaults.json`).

**What was left alone, and why.** The file bridge itself (about 70 operations a second) is the biggest cost, and the only large saving left is to not poll: re7mp.exe could put the number of its newest message into the pose file it already rewrites 25 times a second, so the script reads the inbox only when that number changes (the same latency, about 26 reads a second saved on the host, an exe and script change that would have to work with older versions). It is not built; say if you want it. The scene scan (once a second) and the snapshots are the host's enemy sync and the game-phase tick is the other player's body, the enemy models and their weapons: they are the features, so nothing was cut before the finer parts tell which of them is the cost.

**Tests.** `tools/lua_smoke_1020.lua` (run alone with `RE7MP_ONLY_1020=1`): 45 checks (the gap recorder with a pause outside the script and a slow write inside it, the trim to 30 and the sort of the 12 biggest, the saving flag with its edges, durations and give-up, the exe file's read rates idle / connected / menu open, the slot scans and the questions asked, a slow scan listed, the baseline when a partner appears, the inbox rate and a message arriving within 0.15 s, the debug file's rates, the laps of the game-phase tick). A mutation pass (24 deliberate breaks of the new rules) is caught by those checks. The full regression (7,141 checks) passes. The older checks run with test-only overrides that keep the old timings (`RE7MP_TEST_SAVES_S`, `RE7MP_TEST_ACTIVE_GAP`, `RE7MP_TEST_ACTIVE_S`); the new file uses the real ones. One older check changed on purpose: the panel's "the game lists this slot" still follows a slot within 2 s, now from a scan to slot 20.

**What to do at home.** Play a normal session on the host PC with this build, including one autosave (a checkpoint) and one fight, then send the debug file from BOTH PCs and `re7mp/car_sync.json` if you tried the garage. In the file: `health.perf.frame_gaps` (are the gaps over 100 ms the game's or the script's? is `saving` true at them?), `health.coop_saves.slot_scans` (are there slow scans?), and the part table (`net send/read` should be lower on the host, the finer parts name the next cost). If you still see a hitch at an autosave and `saving_flag.supported` is false or `saving` is never true, tick *9+ also trace save and load calls*, press 9 and send the lines too.

## What changed in v10.19a (release **0.10**; protocol v6 unchanged; **re7mp.exe only: rebuild it on Windows; the script and `defaults.json` stay at 10.19**)

A log someone posted from a Radmin VPN game: the joiner said `Started: connecting over TCP to 26.106.142.46:27015`, `TCP: connected to the host.`, and one second later `TCP connection lost (read failed); reconnecting...`, and the host's log said nothing at all.

- **What that log means.** "Connected" is the TCP handshake, so the VPN, the address and the port are fine (a blocked port or a wrong address would have timed out instead). The host then closed the connection at once. The host does that in exactly one case it checks itself: **the "Other player" box on the host is filled in and does not match the address the joiner connects from** (the connection is closed without a word; because the joiner had already sent data, Windows reports it as a reset, "read failed"). Over Radmin the joiner connects from its own 26.x.x.x address; a typed LAN address, a public IP or a typo in that box is turned away. A second possibility is something else listening on that port on the host (another program, or a second copy of re7mp.exe), and a firewall that resets after the handshake looks the same.
- **Nothing is silent any more** (`src/tcp_transport.cpp`, `src/transport.hpp`; it changes the log only, not what connects):
  - **The host says who it turned away**: `TCP: turned away a connection from 26.x.x.x: the 'Other player' box allows only 26.y.y.y. If 26.x.x.x is the other player, clear that box (it is optional) or type 26.x.x.x in it.` Once per address (again after 30 s), not once a second.
  - **The joiner says what the host sees**: `TCP: connected to the host (the host sees you as 26.x.x.x).` That is the address to type in the host's "Other player" box if it is filled in.
  - **The host says where a player came from**: `TCP: the other player connected (from 26.x.x.x).`
  - **"read failed" and "send failed" carry the OS error number**, with a plain meaning for the usual ones (10054 / ECONNRESET "the other side reset the connection", 10053 aborted, 10060 timed out, 10061 refused, no route, network down).
  - **A joiner that is dropped within 3 s of connecting twice in a row** gets one extra line that lists the three things to check on the host (the box, the mode and port, the firewall), again every 10 drops, so a long wait on the joining screen does not fill the log.
- **Tests.** `tests/core_tests.cpp` (the restricted-host case and the normal TCP case): the joiner log shows the address the host sees, the host log shows the turned-away address once (not on every retry) with the allowed address, the quick-drop hint appears, and the normal connect logs both addresses. All C++ tests pass. The Windows build itself could not be compiled here (the C++ in this change has no Windows-only code beyond the existing socket calls, `getsockname` and `inet_ntop`, which `ws2tcpip.h` provides).
- **What to do if you see it:** on the host, clear the "Other player" box (it is optional; empty means anyone who has the address and port can join) or type the address the joiner's log shows after "the host sees you as"; both players must be in the same Radmin network; only one re7mp.exe may host on that port.

## What changed in v10.19 (release **0.10**; protocol v6 unchanged; **the script AND re7mp.exe change: update `re7mp.lua`, `defaults.json` and rebuild the exe on Windows**)

Your 21 November questions: the garage car, "force both players in the car" ("make the person who got in first the driver and put the other character in the second seat"), and "leave sync Molded and enemies on as a tick in the exe, on by default; we are not going to release until this is synced and tested".

- **1. A box in re7mp.exe: "Sync enemies and the Molded", ticked by default.** New `src/enemy_sync.hpp` (a copy of the story box's pattern: `re7mp.ini` remembers it, `re7mp_enemysync.json` = `{enemy_sync, seq}` is written when Start is pressed and when the box changes) and `src/gui_win.cpp` (the box, `apply_enemy_sync`). The script reads the file every 2 s and applies it once per new `seq`, so a later change in the panel is not undone. **Ticked:** enemy mode 3, the copies' own AI switched off (`enemy.drive_ai`), the copies of the host's Molded on (unless the last session ended inside one: then they stay off and the panel says so). **Unticked:** mode 2, AI on, the copies let go, no Molded copies. Without the new exe the script behaves as in 10.18 (mode 2). The C++ side is tested with `tests/core_tests.cpp` (`test_enemy_sync`); the window code could not be compiled here.
- **2. The garage car (c03_1_Main 700 to 1099; ON by default; needs mode 3).** The car (`sm9104_EthanCar_Garage`) is not an enemy: it has a brain (`app.CarInGarage`), a state machine and three interact children, so each game used to play the whole fight alone. Now:
  - **Who drives.** Whoever gets in first (the car's state leaves `WaitPlayerGetInto`) claims the car (message kind 19, `c|state|seq`, repeated every 4 s). Host or joiner. If both get in in the same moment, the host keeps it.
  - **The other player gets in too (your idea).** The other game fires its own `Interact_GetInto.successInteract()`, so both state machines start together and the other game's Jack cannot take its car. That player is the *rider*. (A first-person view cannot show two bodies in one seat, so every ghost of the other player is drawn 0.7 m to the car's right while a car claim is on; the slider has a minus for the wrong side.)
  - **The puppet.** Once the rider is seated and the runner's car has gone past the get-in (or 3 s have passed), the rider's car is a puppet: brain off, state machine paused, the three interacts off, and its position and turn are put on the runner's car every frame just before rendering (0.2 s behind, between two real points). A rider that is far from the car (more than 8 m for 15 s), is down, or is in a quiet-check hold watches from outside with the same puppet. The rider is carried along with the car if the game does not carry the body.
  - **Jack.** The runner's Jack is the real one. Host runs: the joiner's copy follows the host's as every enemy does in mode 3. Joiner runs: the joiner sends its Jack in its state file and the host's Jack is put onto it (its AI switched off, put back at the end). Jack is a boss, so the hand-over and the pool never touch him.
  - **The state line.** The runner's game puts `#car,x,y,z,qx,qy,qz,qw,speed,windows,frontCrashes,rearCrashes,flags,state` FIRST in its enemy state text (a name that fails the enemy reader's pattern, so an older reader skips it; the size cut takes the end, never this line).
  - **Damage, once each.** Window bits, front / rear body crashes, the door and each tyre are mirrored on the rider's car with the game's own `breakWindow(n)`, `breakFrontBody()`, `breakRearBody()`, `breakOriginalLeftDoor()` and `blowOut<Which>()`; never during a quiet-check hold (dropped after 6 s).
  - **Run-over.** When Jack drives the runner's car and it goes over a player who watches from outside, that player gets one guarded hit (default 250, never lethal in one pass, 1.5 s apart).
  - **The end.** The runner tells the other game (`x|exploded` or `x|left`). On a wreck the rider's car is given back (state machine running), set to `AfterExplosion`, `breakAll()` is called and the rider gets out (`Interact_GetOut`); on `left` only the get-out. Everything the puppet switched off is switched on again. A 30 s cool-down stops a car that stayed in its old state from being claimed again.
  - **Panel (Enemies).** The box, one box per part (ride, ghost seat + offset, carry, brain, damage, run-over + amount), a **"Get me out of the car"** button and a **"Let go of the car now"** button. The story-step matching waits while a car claim is on.
  - **Debug.** `health.garage_car` and the journal `re7mp/car_sync.json` (every state change of my car, every claim, what was fired, counts, the last error, the last run-over check). An error inside the sync is written there and in the log instead of vanishing. A session that ended inside a car call (crumb stage `car`) switches the sync off at the next start.
- **3. Actor probe (reads only; off at every start).** Other scene objects drive themselves like the car. The probe lists the objects with an `app.MovementController` or a character controller that are not enemies or players (name, components, position, moved or not, state-machine state) to `re7mp/actor_probe.json`. Walk a chapter with it on and send the file.
- **4. Corrections.** The garage Jack is `Em3000` (the car's `Enemy` field names it), not `Em2000`: the README and an old findings item said otherwise.
- **Tests.** `tools/lua_smoke_1019.lua` (run alone with `RE7MP_ONLY_1019=1`): a fake garage car (brain, state machine, interact children, tyre flags), Jack as a scene enemy, two games side by side. 115 checks: the exe box (once per seq, ticked / unticked), gating, the claim and the tie, the forced ride, the puppet, the car put where the runner's is, the ghost seat, the carried rider, each damage once, run-overs, the end in order, a story that moves on, a game that ignores the state, a runner that goes quiet, a joiner that runs the car with Jack, matching frozen, crash marker, actor probe. A mutation pass (23 deliberate breaks: tie, gate, ride, puppet, damage once, end order, cool-down, Jack held, line first, seat, matching, run-over, exe box, carry, left, stale claim) is caught by the tests; one survivor is equivalent.
- **Nothing here has been seen in the real game.** The things to watch, in the order they can go wrong: (1) does `Interact_GetInto.successInteract()` seat the other player? (2) does the seat animation survive the state machine being paused? (3) do `set_Enabled(false)`, `set_Paused(true)` and the transform writes work from the script? (4) does `setCurrentState(String)` take (three signatures are tried)? (5) is the ghost seat on the right side? (6) is the rider carried by the game or by the script? (7) does the 0.2 s delay look smooth? Each is a line in the journal.
- **What to send back after a garage fight.** `re7mp/car_sync.json` and the debug file from BOTH PCs, `re7mp/actor_probe.json` if the probe was on, and, if the game closed, `lastcall.json`, `re2_framework_log.txt` and `reframework_crash.dmp` from the PC that closed. Rebuild the exe first (new `src/enemy_sync.hpp`, changed `src/gui_win.cpp`).

## What changed in v10.18 (release **0.10**; protocol v6 unchanged; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json`, the exe from 10.13 stays**)

Your 20 November questions about the game's flow state manager and your three ideas: "match the story points so a gimmick can no longer stop the other player", and "the person an enemy targets should host that enemy so the game logic handles it itself". **All three are in, all experimental, the risky parts off at every start, and nothing here has been seen in the real game.** The numbers and calls come from both mods' notes; the first debug files will show what the real game answers.

- **1. Story points: the probe (reads only; on at every start).** `app.GameFlowFsmManager` (a singleton) holds `Flows`, one `app.GameFlowActiveControl` per story part (keyed by the murmur hash of "<part>_Main": c01_Main 46ba27ae, c03_1_Main 9fb77189, c03_2_Main 2c60d2ac), whose numbered steps are the "story points". Once a second (every 5 s alone) the script reads each part's number (`getProgressiveNo(hash)`; the game answers with a `via.userdata.Variable`, read with whichever of `get_Value`, `get_Int` ... works, and the way that worked is written down), tells the other player every change (message 18: `n|chapter|hash|number|before` for a step forward, `r|...` for a fall, `s|chapter|hash:number,...` every 10 s), and shows both players' numbers side by side in the "Story sync" panel (mine, theirs, which is behind). It finds each part's steps by asking `hasState` (4 ways of asking are tried). The game's own types for the manager, the control and the number are written to `re7mp/flow_probe.json` once, with the steps found. Debug file: `health.story_flow`.
- **2. Story points: matching (EXPERIMENTAL; off at every start; a box, a "Catch up once" button and an "only write the number" box).** A part of mine that is BEHIND the other player's is taken forward, never back, one step at a time through the game's own `sendNo` (the call that RUNS a step: the other mod saw `sendNo(600)` play `c03e22_00` and tick an objective, where `setProgressiveNo` / `jumpProgressiveNo` only wrote the number and nothing happened), 1.5 s apart, using the numbers the other player went through (else the part's own steps, else the number itself), only in a quiet moment (not loading, no cutscene or event, not down, no menu, not 30 s after a FORCE cutscene, not 8 s after a load), each step checked 1.2 s later. A step the game ignores is asked for twice, then the part is left alone ("stuck"); with the second box only the number is written (it skips what the step would do). A number that FELL here (a checkpoint reload, a restart) is not pulled forward again until the other player's number moves. Debug file: `health.story_match` (steps, verified, not taken, stuck, why it waited, the history). **My reading, not confirmed**: a level trigger named `500_Event_...` only acts when its part is at step 500, so a game that is behind never fires it; the probe and matching are how to find out.
- **3a. Who is the enemy after? (reads only; on at every start).** Once a second, for the ten nearest enemies of this game: who its AI is targeting (`app.EnemyThinkBase.get_Target`: me / nobody / someone else), how far it is from me and from the other player, and how often the hand-over rule below WOULD have given it to the joiner. Debug file: `health.enemy_targets` (per enemy). This is the data the final rule should be made from.
- **3b.
