The enemy hand-over (EXPERIMENTAL; off at every start; BOTH players tick it; Enemy sync mode 3 on both).** The host's enemies only ever go for the host: the joiner is a ghost with no status component and no vision beacon there (the other mod: a ghost target was accepted, then cleared within a second, and Jack stood still). So instead of a fake target, the enemy is run by the player it should go for (the other mod's design, message 20): the HOST offers an ordinary enemy to the joiner when the joiner is at least 3 m nearer (within 30 m) for 1 s, and the host's own AI is not on the host at close range (6 m); the JOINER checks its own copy (the same name, within 8 m of where the host's is, alive, awake), lets it go (its AI components back on, no longer put onto the host's enemy) and sends that enemy's state 12 times a second in the host's own text through the same state file (re7mp.exe relays it in both directions, so nothing changed there); the HOST stops sending that enemy, switches the AI of its own body off (the same components mode 3 "switch off their AI" switches off), puts the body where the joiner's is, and its health follows the joiner's number (the owner's number is the pool); a hit the host lands on the body is passed on and lands on the joiner's enemy. It goes back to the host when the host is clearly nearer again, no state arrives for 3 s, the joiner says it lost it or went down, a load starts or either box is switched off, and then stays with the host for 30 s. A death on the joiner's side is finished on the host's body the way the pool finishes copies. **Bosses and Molded bodies stay with the host** (the crash history, and a Molded has no name that is the same in both games), as does any enemy that shares its name with another. Debug file: `health.enemy_handover` (offered, taken, refused and why, taken back and why, states, hits, the last lines).
- **Tests.** `tools/lua_smoke_1018.lua` (run alone with `RE7MP_ONLY_1018=1`): a fake story flow, a fake enemy with a think component, and a host and a joiner game side by side with the messages and the state file carried across by hand. Mutation checks on the new rules were run (the margin, the host's body and snapshot, the AI switched back on, hits passed on, the staleness timeout).
- **What to send back after a session.** The debug file (`health.story_flow`, `health.story_match`, `health.enemy_targets`, `health.enemy_handover`) and `re7mp/flow_probe.json` from BOTH PCs; if the game closed: `lastcall.json` (new stages `flow`, `tgt`, `own`).
- **Not done / honest limits.**
  - **Nothing has been seen in the real game.** Unknown: whether the number can be read the way the notes say, whether `hasState` and `sendNo` can be called from here, whether a trigger really waits for its step, whether `get_Target` reads the AI's target, and how the host's puppet body behaves with its AI components off.
  - **The host's body of an enemy the joiner runs does not play the joiner's animations** (they play on the joiner's copy only; the other mod's version had the same limit), so it may slide in a pose on the host's screen.
  - **While the joiner runs an enemy, that enemy cannot hurt the host's player** (its AI works in the joiner's game); the host takes it back when it is clearly nearer. With both players about equally near it stays where it was (the 3 m margin).
  - **Molded bodies and bosses are not handed over**; a boss's targeting is only logged.
  - **The matching skips what the steps would do** when only the number is written; it can leave a gimmick unplayed.
  - Everything from 10.17 and 10.16 and the open items (the item drop, Steam, host performance, the garage car) stand.

## What changed in v10.17 (release **0.10**; protocol v6 unchanged; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json`, the exe from 10.13 stays**)

Your 20 November remark: "I suspect theres a seperate animation bank that includes cutscenes since those animations dont load". **You were right**, and the two debug files of that day (the host's and the joiner's) show how, for both Ethan and Mia. **Everything here is unverified in the real game** (the harness fakes the game's answers: 6,796 checks passed, 0 failed; the new rules were each broken on purpose to see a check fail: 7 breaks, all caught).

- **Ethan in a cutscene (the fix).** While a cutscene runs, Ethan's layer 0 plays clips from the event's own motion list, a separate file such as `Animation/Ingame/c03/c03e20_00/motlist/c03e20_00_pl0000.motlist`. Since 10.14 that list has been added to the other body as bank 30 (60 adds in the host's file and 15 in the joiner's, **0 failures**), but the script played an entry only when its name started with `pl0000`, and a cutscene's entry is called `c03e20_00_pl0000_000`. So each cutscene in both files (c01e00_00, c01e04_00, c03e20_00, c03e21_00, c03e22_00) was refused with "is not a character's clip ... not played" and the body kept its idle for the whole scene (`scripted_moves.counts.held` 4,227 in the host's file, `unmappable` 35 of 59 moves). Now an entry is the character's own clip, played as it is, when its name starts with `pl0000` (as before), **carries the actor's tag** (`..._pl0000_000`), **ends in `_PL`** (the player's side of a clip made for two, e.g. `em2000_Common_TakeAx_Stand_PL`, which the Mia fight c01e04_00 uses) or **comes from a list file that ends in `_pl0000.motlist`** (the character's own cutscene list). A prop's or a weapon's own clip (`sm0316_Interact_ChainCut`, `wp1100_Interact_ChainCut`) and the enemy's side of a clip made for two (`..._EM`) still do not play; they still play only as the character's clip of the same name, or not at all. `scripted_moves.counts.own_other` counts the new ones and the first 16 are named in `scripted_moves.lines` ("plays as it is (the actor's tag pl0000 is in its name)"). Unverified: whether a cutscene's clip played on the other body sits where it should (its root may carry the cutscene's own coordinates) and how far it is behind the other game's cutscene (the clip starts when the message arrives).
- **Mia's banks (diagnostics, not a fix yet).** In the joiner's file Mia (Em2000) plays clips from banks 1 and 3 (`3/20` 3,061 times on the host: a 1,680-frame clip) that the joiner's copy does not have (11 clips missing, `plays_missing` 185), and the host answered "bank not found" for them 20 times: the three quick tries (5 s apart) for the bank's path all failed, and the placeholder `Em2100` of the same file lists bank 0 as `Animation/Enemy/em2000/motlist/em2000_c01e00.motlist` and bank 1 as `.../Gimmick/em2000_Gimmick_ch01.motlist`, i.e. exactly the chapter-event and gimmick lists. A cutscene's or gimmick's bank is only on the enemy while the event runs, so the quick tries can all fall before it is there. Now, after the three quick tries the host tries **six more times, 20 s apart, while the bank's clips are still being seen** (`PATH_LATE_TRIES`; `paths_found_late` counts the ones found late; the bank's entry in `health.enemy_clips.banks` says "after N tries") and, from the third failed try, records **what the enemy's Motion does hold** (`banks.<enemy>.<bank>.search`: how many dynamic, active and indexed banks, and for each row the kind, index, bank id and path: at most 40 rows, reads only). I did not copy the sibling's paths (Em2100 for Em2000): the bank numbers are not the same in both (Em2000's bank 0 is `em2000.motlist`, Em2100's is `em2000_c01e00.motlist`), so a copy could play the wrong animation. The joiner's file also said "no animation layer on my copy: clips are not played on it (needs the box 'Also switch off their AI')": enemy clips are only played on the joiner's copy while that box is ticked.
- Debug file: `health.motion_banks.scripted_moves.counts.own_other`, `health.enemy_clips.counts.paths_found_late`, `health.enemy_clips.banks.<enemy>.<bank>.search`. `CFG.BUILD = "10.17"`, `defaults.json` build 10.17 (clips and settings are the 10.15 ones). New settings: none.
- **Not done / honest limits.**
  - **Mia's cutscene clips are still not on the joiner's copy**: this build only searches longer and records what is there; it will say in the next file where those banks live (or that the live enemy never holds them).
  - **Other actors' cutscene lists** (`..._em2000.motlist` of an event) are not used: only Ethan's body is driven from an event's list.
  - **The cutscene clip starts when the message arrives** (the message has no frame), so the other body can be a little behind the other game's cutscene; whether the two start together depends on the story sync.
  - Everything from 10.16 (crash defences, the garage probe) and the open items (the item drop, Steam, host performance) stand.

## What changed in v10.16 (release **0.10**; protocol v6 unchanged; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json`, the exe from 10.13 stays**)

Your 20 November report (two debug files, the ChatGPT notes on the garage car): "crash after mia fight, crash when fighting jack, some other things". **Neither debug file contains a crash crumb** (the debug file is written every 15 s, so the last seconds before a crash are lost), so the cause of the two crashes is **not proven**. What the files do show is the script writing to bosses and making and destroying weapon models around exactly those fights, and what Molded Together's notes say about the same moments (scripted damage on a boss during a cutscene: "VMContext was already corrupted", c0000005 about 18 s later). 10.16 removes those things. **Everything here is unverified in the real game** (the harness fakes the game's answers: 6,777 checks passed, 0 failed; the quick file `tools/lua_smoke_1015.lua` now has 419 of them, 77 more than in 10.15, and every new rule was broken on purpose to see its check fail: 26 deliberate changes, all caught; one of them, "the probe never stops", changes nothing because a second guard stops it by an error).

- **The Mia crash: the health pool no longer touches a boss.** In your joiner's file the pool wrote the host's health into the joiner's own Mia/Jack copy during the cutscene c01e04_00 (host 700 of 2000, the copy's own maximum 1000), raised one to 350 after the game's own script had brought it down, and when the host's boss died it hit the copy three times with 3000 damage, then "Enemy model gone" and the game closed. Now: a boss (the families em2000, em2100, em3000, em3100, em3200, em3300, em3600, em6000, em8000, em8100, em8500, em8900, or any maximum of 2000 or more) is **left alone by default** (the bar still shows the host's number on both screens); a new box "EXPERIMENTAL: the pool also writes the host's health into my copy of a BOSS" (off at every start, not remembered) allows **lowering** only (never raising, never when the two maxima differ by more than 1 %: two games that disagree on a boss's maximum are in different phases of the fight), and **never a damage call on a boss**: when the host's boss dies, mine is left to the game's own story (the journal says so). On the host, a hit from the joiner on a boss is refused with "not now: <why>" while the quiet check below says so. Counters in `health.boss_pool` (`boss_not_written`, `boss_max_differs`, `boss_raise_skipped`, `boss_finish_skipped`, `host_boss_hits_refused`, `held_not_quiet`); a crash marker for the pool (`pool` stage) switches it off at the next start.
- **A quiet check (`health.guard`) that the pool, the weapon model and the boss hits wait for.** Nothing is written, hit, made or changed while the game is loading, an event has just started (the story hook sees it before the event list does: 4 s), a cutscene is playing (`GameManager._GameEventMan.RunningEventControllers` has entries AND `MenuManager.isPlayingEvent` is not false: a background event such as c01e01_01, which stays "running" through the whole Mia fight, is not a cutscene) or one ended less than 4 s ago. It only reads (at most four times a second). If the game will not say, the check lets things through as before and `health.guard.source` says "unreadable: ...", so that is visible in the next file.
- **The Jack crash: the weapon model is calmer.** In your files the sender sent things that are not weapons (an antiseptic bottle, a Props/ puzzle object) and the receiver made 74 weapon objects and destroyed 73 in one session, including the chainsaw (`wp0060`) of the Jack fight. Now: only a `wp####` object, or an `app.Item` whose model is a `Weapon/` file, is a weapon (`not_a_weapon` counts the rest); **the chainsaw is never sent or made** (`SKIP`, `skipped_named`, `skipped_last`; the sender and the receiver both refuse it, so an older sender is also ignored); the player's children are not walked and nothing is made during a cutscene, a load or the 4 s after one (`scans_held_not_quiet`, `held_not_quiet`, the status says "waiting: a cutscene is playing"); a copy is let go (forgotten, never destroyed) when the game starts loading; at most 12 copies a minute (`churn_held`; a weapon that flips in and out of the hand no longer makes and destroys objects for ever).
- **The garage car probe (reads only; the first step of the ChatGPT notes).** The other mod's notes say "Garage fight: CarInGarage not synced; a friend driving shows as their character moving at seat height", and nothing records what the type is. New box "Garage car probe (reads only)" (off at every start, not remembered): when ticked it writes `re7mp/car_probe.json` at once with the type definition of `app.CarInGarage` (every field with its type and whether it is static, every method with its parameter count and return type, up to the first `System.`/`via.Component` parent), and, once an object of that type exists in the scene (the garage), **three readings** (now, +5 s, +15 s: plain fields, i.e. numbers, booleans, text and vectors; an object field only says it is set; the game object's name and position; its component types) and **a list of the fields that changed** between the first and the last reading (the candidates for "where the car is" and "who drives it"). It looks at the scene once every 2 s while it waits, then stops; "Probe again" starts afresh. It never writes to the game and sends nothing. `health.car_probe`. **No car sync was attempted** (the notes say not to touch `sm9104_FrontGlass_outCar` and `sm9353_ethancardoor_rigidbody` before the type is known).
- **Already there for the other two notes.** `EventActionController.CurrentTask > get_eventParam > eventAction > get_motionJackFsm > getActiveMotionBank(0)` is already read by the motion-bank module (10.1; `re7mp/motion_banks.json`). The names of the events that start are in the journal when the "Story sync" box is ticked (the line "story: I started the cutscene c03e02_00 ..."): tick it for the garage fight.
- Debug file: new sections `health.guard`, `health.car_probe`; `health.boss_pool` and `health.weapon_models` have the new counters. `CFG.BUILD = "10.16"`, `defaults.json` has build 10.16 (the clips and settings are the 10.15 ones). New settings: none (both new boxes start off and are never saved).
- **Not done / honest limits.**
  - **The crash causes are not proven.** If the game still closes at the end of the Mia fight or in the Jack fight, send, from the PC that closed: `reframework/data/re7mp/lastcall.json`, `re2_framework_log.txt` (it keeps every `[re7mp]` journal line and the exception address) and `reframework_crash.dmp` if there is one. That names what the script was doing in the last second.
  - **A boss on the joiner is no longer kept in step** unless the new box is ticked: the joiner's copy of Mia or Jack can be at a different health from the host's. The boss bar still shows the host's number on both screens.
  - **The quiet check may never be quiet** if the game's own event list is read wrongly (for example if a background event always counts): `health.guard` (`source`, `counts.cutscene`, `last_not_quiet`) shows it.
  - **Weapon models are still one still mesh**, and the chainsaw is not shown at all.
  - **Host performance** (your host file: 4.7 ms a frame on average, hitches of 400 to 700 ms) and the `renamed_duplicates` count of 3,040 in the same file were noted, not investigated.
  - **The item drop** you called broken in November: still not specified (a herb or ammo that did nothing, an item that did not appear for your friend, a box or an enemy drop?). Nothing changed.
  - **Steam** (10.13) is still unverified.

## What changed in v10.15 (release **0.10**; protocol v6 unchanged; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json`, the exe from 10.13 stays**)

Your 19 November requests: "we should work on showing the weapon models now, I cant test right now but all the knowledge and research from molded together and our own research, lets make sure the molded enemies are synced too from the research, we should also see what we can optimise but its not priority", and, while that was being built, "both players boss health pool should be the same too, just to make sure they sync". You could not test, so everything was built from the project notes (Molded Together's `NOTES`, `coop_enemies.lua` and `coop_dummy.lua` of their repository, our own `re7-findings.md`) and checked against the test harness. **Everything here is unverified in the real game** (the harness fakes the game's answers: the last full run passed 6,688 checks and failed one, the harness's own expectation of the build number in `defaults.json` (it still wanted 10.14), which is corrected; 342 of the checks are new, in `tools/lua_smoke_1015.lua`, and every new piece was also broken on purpose, 20 times, to see that its check then fails).

- **The other player's weapon on their body (new message kind 17).** The body of the other player held nothing. Now the game that holds a weapon tells the other one which it is and the other one makes a copy from the game's own files. *Sender* (looked at twice a second while the other player is here): the drawn child of my player called `wp....` (or an `app.Item` object) that has a mesh and a material; its two file paths and where it sits on its joint (position, rotation, the joint's name, `R_Wep` for Ethan) go as `w|<mesh>|<material or =>|x|y|z|qw|qx|qy|qz|<joint>` when they change (at most once a second) and every 8 s; `w|-` once when the hand is empty. The knife counts only after half a second in the hand (a gun coming out leaves it drawn for a moment) and only when nothing else is drawn. *Receiver:* every field is checked (paths `[A-Za-z0-9_-./]` and 90 characters at most, no `..`, no leading `/`; numbers finite, the offset within 3 m, the rotation normalised), then in the body's game phase ONE object with a `via.render.Mesh` is made, the two files are loaded by path through the loader the body already uses, and when the mesh is ready (10 s at most) it is hung on the body's `R_Wep` joint (else `R_Hand`, else `R_Wrist`) with the sender's grip. It comes down when the weapon changes, the body is made again, or the box goes off; a weapon that fails 3 times is given up. The game's own weapon object is never copied or attached: only a mesh made from files. Safety: marker stage `wmesh` (a session that ended inside it switches this off at the next start and the journal says so), a box "Weapon: show the weapon in their hand" (remembered as `weapon_models`). `health.weapon_models` (what I hold and why not, what they hold, the copy, counts, the last 24 lines).
- **One health pool, and the boss bar (your 16:04 note).** The host's game runs the enemies, so the host's health number is the pool: every hit the joiner makes already went to the host (kind 9) and lowers it there. What was not the same: in mode 3 ("move my own enemies onto the host's") the joiner's own copy of the enemy kept a health number of its own, so it could outlive the host's and a boss showed two different numbers. Now, for every copy that is moved onto a host enemy (at most four writes a second per copy): mine higher than the host's, mine is set to the host's at once (`setHealth(health, max)`, the call the other mod uses); mine lower (my own shot landed first), the host's number is put back only after the difference has stood for 1.5 s, so a hit that was just sent is not undone before the host has applied it; a different maximum (another difficulty), the same SHARE of the pool; never below 1 (`setHealth(0)` marks an enemy dead without the death); when the host's enemy has been dead for 1 s mine is finished by one hit far above its health through the game's damage call, up to 3 times, and only within 20 s of the last time it was put on the host's enemy, so another enemy of the same name in the joiner's own scene is left alone. The snapshot carries the enemy's maximum as a **14th field** (the joiner reads 13 or 14: an older host has no maximum, and nothing is scaled then). A **boss bar** (name, health / maximum, share) at the top of BOTH screens, from the host's reading, so the two numbers can be seen to be the same; a boss is one of the families em2000, em2100, em3000, em3100, em3200, em3300, em3600, em6000, em8000, em8100, em8500, em8900 or anything with a maximum of 2000 or more; a dead boss loses its bar. Two boxes (remembered: `pool_follow`, `pool_bar`). `health.boss_pool`.
- **The Molded, on the host's side (spawn-point names).** Molded Together found why the Molded were the one family nobody could see: a Molded body sits in the pool (`/MoldedGenerator/MoldedPool`), so a walk over the top of the scene never meets it, and the five Em4000 of Chapter 3's "Moldeads" folder have no save component, so "folder path / name" gave them all one id. Now the game's spawn points (`app.EnemySpawnInfo`, read about once a second the way their scan does) are read: a point whose body is an Em#### object gives an enemy named `Em4000_<12 hex digits of the point's GUID>`, the same in both games. That wire name keys the joiner's enemies, models, boxes and hits. A body the game put to sleep (`get_Update` false: no player near its zone) is not sent; at most 14 from the spawn points; two scene-root objects with one name (any enemy) are told apart by a suffix from their address, so one no longer overwrites the other on the joiner (a parked placeholder with a live enemy's name used to hide the live one). Reading only. `health.molded_spawn_points`.
- **The Molded on the joiner: copies (EXPERIMENTAL, off at every start, never saved).** The ghosts (red boxes, enemy models) only show where the host's Molded are. A new box makes the joiner's OWN game bring out the same Molded (its spawn point has the same GUID) and move that real body onto the host's, the way Molded Together does; it works in mode 2 and mode 3 and lets go in mode 1. Taken from their notes (v0.47b to v0.49), with every limit I know of: at most 8 copies; the spawn is asked with `requestSpawn(spawnInfo, 0)` ONLY (the three-argument form raised c0000005 for them), every 2 s for 20 s because it refuses while the spot is in view; the body is taken out of its pool (`set_Parent(nil)`: while pooled, a position set from outside only changes its local transform and the world matrix stops following for good) and moved with `MovementController.warp(position, rotation)` (not again while it stands still, under 1 cm within a quarter of a second); the game's sleep is undone (UpdateSelf and DrawSelf on, the mesh switched on again every second) only while the spawn point still has THIS body; its AI is off (`MotionFsm` disabled, `EnemyOrder.requestThinkOff`; no component is ever switched on blindly: some start off on purpose); the host's clip is played on layer 0 by the same call the enemy clips use (a failing call waits a second before it is tried again); when the host's Molded dies the copy gets up to 3 hits far above its health, credited to my player; a copy whose position could not be set 5 times in a row is let go and that spawn point is left alone for 60 s; it is let go too when the host stops sending it, when its data is older than 3 s, when the box or the mode goes off, or when the scene changes (a body I woke goes back to sleep and into its pool). The ghost of a Molded that has a copy is not drawn; its invisible box stays, so shots at it still go to the host as before. Safety: marker stage `molded` (a session that ended inside it switches the box off at the next start; the journal says "the last session ended while the script was moving a copy of the host's Molded in this game"; ticking the box by hand tries again). `health.molded_copies`.
- **Optimisation (not the priority, so only what the numbers asked for).** Your 10.13 debug file says what a frame costs: 1.5 ms on average over 143,867 frames (3,648 over 8 ms, 116 over 16 ms, 20 over 33 ms), of which "net send/read" 0.7 ms (110 s in all, the slowest 126 ms, 1,263 frames over 8 ms), "enemy update" 0.4 ms, "menu/who/weapon/inventory" 0.2 ms, everything else under 0.1 ms. So the script is light and the cost is file reading (the pose file 33 times a second, the message inbox 33 times a second, the pose written 25 times a second). To see what else could go, the test harness got a profile (`RE7MP_PROFILE=1`: game calls per second by method, the script lines that make them, file operations per second). Done: **the message inbox is read every 0.1 s while nothing arrives** (about 10 reads a second instead of 33) **and as often as before for 2 s after a message** (`CFG.bridge.msg`; the first message after a quiet spell waits at most 0.1 s; `health.bridge_reads.message_inbox_poll` counts the reads and the reads left out). In my own Molded code the profile found three costs that I took out before they shipped: the host's frame number read every frame per copy (now once a second), the scene key read through 3 to 4 native calls every frame (now every 0.25 s) and a warp every frame for a body that stands still (now skipped). **Not done, on purpose:** keeping managed objects across frames and spreading the scene-root walks over several frames would save a little more, but a managed object the game has freed is an access violation and a walk that spans frames can meet a changed scene; for a gain that cannot be measured here that is a bad trade, and your next file will say whether anything is worth it (`health.perf` is unchanged).
- Debug file: new sections `health.weapon_models`, `health.boss_pool`, `health.molded_spawn_points`, `health.molded_copies`, `health.bridge_reads.message_inbox_poll`. `CFG.BUILD = "10.15"`, `defaults.json` rebuilt. New: message kind 17, the 14th snapshot field, the saved settings `pool_follow`, `pool_bar` and `weapon_models`, the crash stages `wmesh` and `molded`. The harness got `tools/lua_smoke_1015.lua` (the new checks; `RE7MP_ONLY_1015=1` runs only that file, a few seconds), the profile, and `RE7MP_TEST_QUIET_S` (the older scenarios keep the old inbox rate).
- **Found by the checks while this was built.** (1) The weapon sender's "nothing in the hand" message every 8 s filled the 32 places of the outbox and pushed a real message out of it (the revive check failed): "nothing" is now sent once, and only after a weapon was sent. (2) A line of the journal that says a copy "would not die" was hidden by the journal's own rate limit: it is now forced. (3) A boss that was dead kept its bar (only "alive" flags counted). (4) The weapon status said "on the hand" after the box was switched off. (5) Switching the Molded box on and off left nothing in the journal. (6) A failing `changeMotion` or `warp` would have been retried every frame for ever: now a one-second back-off, and five failures in a row let the copy go with a 60 s refusal.
- **Not done / honest limits.**
  - **None of this has been seen in the game.** The weapon copy needs the game to load a weapon's mesh and material by path and the body to have the hand joint; the Molded copies need the game to accept the spawn request, the pool exit and the warp. Each one says in the debug file where it stopped (`status`, `lines`, `counts`).
  - **The weapon is one still mesh.** A weapon with several parts (a scope, a pump, a lit flashlight), a muzzle flash, a slide that moves, a reload that moves the magazine are not shown, and a weapon that hangs deeper than two levels under the player is not found (`weapon_models.why_not` says so). Nothing is sent for an empty hand. A different body model (Mia, Clancy) may have no `R_Wep`: the next joints are tried and the file lists the hand joints it has.
  - **The Molded copies do not attack here.** Their AI is off; what happens when a Molded hits the joiner is unchanged from 10.14. Only the Molded whose spawn point is in the joiner's scene can have a copy (the same area loaded); the others stay ghosts.
  - **The boss pool is the host's number.** If the joiner's game is not in mode 3 the pool is only shown (the bar), not written to a copy.
  - **The item drop** (you said it is broken): still not touched. The only drop in your 10.13 file is Mia's Driver's Licence, a discardable KEY item that the script refuses to put on the ground on purpose since 10.12. I need to know which drop you mean (a herb or ammo that did nothing, an item that did not appear for your friend, a box or an enemy drop).
  - **Steam is still unproven** (10.13's hellos and app id 480): the log lines starting `Steam:` from both PCs are the next thing to look at.

## What changed in v10.14 (release **0.10**; protocol v6 unchanged; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json`, the exe from 10.13 stays**)

Your 19 November request: "we should build the animations and motions that they have (Molded Together), it has already been synced". I read their code (`coop_dummy.lua`: `event_jack`, `jack_real`, `add_ghost_banks`, `run_slot`, the `mbank` share) next to ours and built what was missing. **Everything here is unverified in the real game** (the checks are against the test harness, 6313 of them, none failing; the game's own answers to these calls could not be tried).

- **Scripted moves (the van door, a ladder, the sheet metal, the kick on a body bag, the low beams).** The game takes over the player's animator and plays clips from the event's OWN motion list on layer 0, in place of Ethan's bank 0: the pair our `mot` event sent for that was a number of a prop's list, which means nothing on the other body. Now the list is read (player > `app.EventActionController` (`get_isActive`) > `CurrentTask` > `get_eventParam` > `<eventAction>k__BackingField` > `get_motionJackFsm` > `getActiveMotionBank(0)` > `get_MotionList` > `get_ResourcePath`, and the bank's id) and goes to the other player first, then the clip layer 0 plays from it (new message kind **16**: `j|<clip>|<path>`, `j|-` when it is over). The other body adds the list as its **bank 30** (a `DynamicMotionBank` that overwrites the id, then `setupMotionBank`) and plays the clip. An entry named `pl...` plays as it is; one that is not (a ladder's `LadderUpL`, the prop's tree nodes) plays as the character's clip of the same name in that list (`pl0000_Hands_Ladder-Up_L`: names compared in lower case without `pl0000_`, `Hands_` and punctuation). An entry that maps to nothing is not played (an unbound clip is the T pose), and while their move lasts the pair and labels they send as `mot` are not read as Ethan clips. While I am in a move myself, my layer-0 pair is not sent as `mot` and not learned.
- **The banks the game adds to a body at run time** (ids 3 to 63: a grab, a fight move) go out as `b|<id>|<overwrite>|<path>` when the list changes and every 15 s, and are added to the other body the same way (the count is reset to what the body started with and all are added again: the other mod found a list swapped into a used slot is not taken up). A small action of such a bank (`act`, bank 3 to 31) now goes out and plays once its bank is on the body; until then it waits instead of being marked as failed for 10 s.
- **A second small action** (the other mod's `action2`, "usually the other arm"): the second upper-layer clip is sent as the new `actb` event and played on the body's third layer with its own mask and blend mode; ending it fades only its layer.
- **Their block plays on the arms.** The block (`mol`, the whole-body clip of 10.4, which stopped the legs) is now ALSO sent as a small action with the mask its layer has, and when the arm layer can play it the body's legs keep their walk. A block with no joint mask, a clip the body cannot play, or the small actions switched off: the whole-body clip, as before (`health.game_motion.small_action.their_block_by_the_arms`).
- **Safety.** Every game call of the sharing is traced (marker stage `jack`): a session that ended inside it switches the sharing off at the next start and the journal says where (the rest of the animation sync is not touched). A box in the panel ("Scripted moves", not remembered) switches it off at any time. At most 60 bank changes a session. Paths are checked (`[%w_./-]`, no `..`, `motlist` in the name).
- Debug file: `health.motion_banks.scripted_moves` (my list and clip, theirs, the banks on the body, counts of mapped / held / unmappable entries, bank changes, the last 40 lines), `health.motion_banks.small_actions.second`, `health.game_motion.small_action.second`. `CFG.BUILD = "10.14"`, `defaults.json` rebuilt. New: message kind 16, the `actb` event; no new saved settings.
- **Not done / honest limits.**
  - **The weapon walk and jog (2020 / 2021 on the arm layer while they move)** of the other mod are not built: our `mot` pair already carries the weapon set's Move tree (2002), which is where Ethan's own walk with a weapon is.
  - **Which entries the game really names `pl...`** is unknown: the first time you climb a ladder or open the van door with the other player, `health.motion_banks.scripted_moves.lines` says what was mapped and what was not.
  - **The item drop** (you said it is broken): the only drop in your 10.13 file is Mia's Driver's Licence, a discardable KEY item, which the script refuses to put on the ground on purpose since 10.12 (`createDropItemInstance` makes a bare item object for key items and the game fell over on it). Nothing was changed for it; I need to know which drop you mean (a herb or ammo that did nothing, an item that did not appear for your friend, a box or an enemy drop).

## What changed in v10.13 (release **0.10**; protocol v6 unchanged; **a new re7mp.exe AND a new script: update both**; `defaults.json` rebuilt; **the exe is about 22 MB bigger: it carries the models**)

Your 19 November notes: the cutscene markers in the Mia basement, the models inside the exe, and "Steam says connected (100) but sends no data". **Everything here is unverified in the real game** (the Windows-only parts of the exe cannot be built on the machine this was made on: the first thing to do is build it and look at the log).

- **Steam: the link starts itself, and the log says where it stops.** "Relay ready (100)" only says Steam's own network is up. The session with your friend does not exist until somebody SENDS something to them, and the exe sent nothing until the script wrote its first pose (with the game not yet in a save, never). From Valve's own source (`csteamnetworkingmessages.cpp`): `AcceptSessionWithUser` is a no-op when no session exists, a signal for a program with no listener is dropped without a word, and only a send creates the connection. So each side now sends a small hello (`S7HI`, 8 bytes, eaten by the link and never shown to the engine) as soon as it starts, every second until it hears the other, and a quiet one every 4 s while connected; the first hello that arrives is answered at once. The log now carries what Steam says about the session (`GetSessionConnectionInfo`: none / connecting / finding a route / connected / closed by the other side / failed here, the end reason and Steam's own text, the relay data centre), whenever it changes, and after 20 s without a word one hint line says what to check (and, from the session's state, which of the two likely causes it is). The session is closed when you press Stop, so the next Start is clean.
- **Steam: the exe is no longer "Resident Evil 7" to Steam.** It started Steam as app 418370, the same program as the running game on the same account, so Steam could hand the other player's session request to the game's own connection, which ignores it. It now runs as app **480** (Spacewar, free for every account). Put `steam_app_id=418370` in `re7mp.ini` to get the old behaviour back. While the exe runs with Steam, your Steam status says "Playing Spacewar" (it used to say Resident Evil 7). One Steam connection is now started once and kept for the whole run (the friends menu and the link used to start and shut Steam down in turn, with `FreeLibrary` in between).
- **The models are inside the exe.** AfroJackX's "Third Person Full Model Placeholders RT (2022)" (Nexus RE7 mod 86) is packed into `re7mp.exe` (resource 103, `modpack/third-person-full-models.zip`, made by `tools/pack_models.py`). *Install / update mod* installs them from the exe itself: nothing to download, no folder to keep beside the exe (a `modpack` folder beside it, or the author's zip in Downloads, are still used when the exe carries none). The credit is in the log, in `CREDITS.txt` (inside the zip) and in the README; the licence points are unchanged (RE7 only, free, nothing sold, no donation points, not converted to another game). **An update now downloads those 22 MB with the exe.**
- **The exe has an icon** (`data/re7mp_icon.ico`, resource 1): in Explorer, the title bar and the taskbar.
- **Cutscene markers (`Pos_<cutscene>_<actor>`, e.g. `Pos_c01e00_30_em2000` for Mia in the basement).** They are fixed objects of the area, so both games have them at the same place and there is nothing to sync about WHERE they are; what can differ is whether the area is loaded when FORCE plays a cutscene for you. So: the markers of a cutscene that starts in your game are looked up and told to the other player (message kind 15, before the cutscene message); before FORCE plays a cutscene here, the markers the other player named are looked for in this game, and if some are missing the play waits (up to 6 s, checking every second) for the area to load, then plays anyway and says so. `health.story.markers` in the debug file lists, per cutscene, what each game found (actors and places), what was missing or moved, and how the wait ended; **a new button in the story panel lists every `Pos_` object of the scene with its place** (stand in the room, press it, send the debug file: this is how to see the Mia basement's markers). A box in the panel switches the check off. One scene walk (at most 0.25 s, at most 20 000 objects) per cutscene, kept for 20 s.
- **Models that were not rebuilt (Mia, Clancy, Pete).** The 17 November file shows Mia's body with no materials for 12+ s and no verdict at all: after a body comes up, the check waited up to 14 s for "parts still loading" even when the MAIN body itself had no materials, and Clancy's, Pete's and the Em6200 body took 17 s to be rebuilt for the same reason (every one was fine after the rebuild). The main body's materials are now waited for 5 s (`model_settle.body_materials_wait_s`); hands and other parts keep their 14 s.
- `CFG.BUILD = "10.13"`, `defaults.json` rebuilt. New: message kind 15, the `steam_app_id` ini key, the story panel's marker box and button.
- **Not done / honest limits.**
  - **Steam is still unproven.** If it still sends no data, the log lines starting `Steam:` from both PCs now say whether Steam found a route, which relay, and why a session failed. The next step then would be a REFramework plugin inside the game itself (no second program on the account at all).
  - **The duplicate T-posing enemy on the joiner** (hide the inactive model, tie both health pools, target both players), **molded enemies**, **damage per gun and ammo type**, **the garage fight's targeting and shared health** and **the boss fight that leaves the joiner's AI dead**: not touched. They need the other player in the room to test, and the `CarInGarage` notes (`Notes_for_claude.txt`) are an investigation, not a fix.
  - **The cutscene markers are looked up, not moved.** If the Mia basement cutscene still misplaces her after the wait, the file now says whether her marker was there.

## What changed in v10.12 (release **0.10**; protocol v6 unchanged; **a new script AND a new re7mp.exe: update both** (the exe change is small: an old exe still runs with the new script); `defaults.json` rebuilt)

Your 15 November notes on 10.11 (the list "broken") and "the enemy sync could be better". **Everything here is unverified in the real game** (the checks are against the test harness; the exe's Windows part cannot be compiled where this was written).

- **Enemy sync: a timeline instead of a chase.** The joiner used to move each enemy toward the newest snapshot (`a = 1 - exp(-12 dt)`): at 10 snapshots a second that is a step and a slow-down before every one. Now every snapshot is a point on a timeline and the enemies are drawn a short, adaptive time **behind the host** (about 0.17 to 0.25 s: twice the measured snapshot gap plus a little), always **between two real positions**, with the facing interpolated too (the other mod's "playback delay" idea from the project notes). Late data is carried on at the last speed for at most 0.2 s, a jump of 8 m or more (a spawn, a warp) is shown at once and not slid through. The host puts **its clock** in the snapshot (`T<seconds>` as the first entry; a 10.11 joiner skips it, a 10.11 host sends none and the arrival times are used), and the host sends **15 snapshots a second while it has 8 enemies or fewer** (10 with more: the reading is the cost). The debug file has `health.enemy_timeline` (delay, gap, host clock seen, frames interpolated / extrapolated / held, jumps, restarts).
- **Run or walk.** "When another player is running, the walk animation plays". The clips you recorded say the walk is **2.54 m/s** and the run **4.02 m/s**, and the script switched at **2.4**, below the recorded walk, so which one played depended on camera noise. Now the player says it in the flags (**bit 13, `POSE_RUN` 8192**: the exe already passes all 16 bits; my own speed over 0.2 s steps, with a lower bar to stay running than to start, never while crouched) and the receiver runs when that bit is set **or** when its own estimate of their speed is above the bar (an older build on the other side). The bars are the middle of your two recorded speeds (3.28 / 2.88). The debug file has `health.run_or_walk` (the bars, my speed, theirs, the bit, switches); if running still plays walk, send it with the clip names from `game_motion.by_state`.
- **Crawling and the cut-off legs sink into the floor.** The body stood at (camera height - 1.6 m), right for standing and (with the clip's own hips) for a crouch; crawling or lying puts the camera 0.4 to 0.8 m above the floor, so the body ended up about a metre in the ground. The sender knows its own floor (the player object's y): while its camera is lower than **0.9 m** above it (and on the player, not a cutscene camera) it sends the new **`low`** event (centimetres, `0` when it ends, repeated every 1.5 s while it lasts) and the other body is stood on **that** floor. Not used for Ethan's friend. `health.low_body`.
- **Key item crash when dropped in the main house.** `createDropItemInstance` makes a proper pickup only for the consumables; for a key item (or a weapon) it makes a bare item object, and the game fell over on it (your 10.2 file shows exactly such a drop: `MailMia`, a "discardable key item"). The ground route now refuses anything that is not known to be a consumable: by the category the game gave when the item was seen (focused, discarded or picked up here: ammo, healing, materials and "other" pass; key items, files, maps and weapons do not), and by name for the key-like names (key, fuse, cutter, crank, valve, handle, bolt, card, file, map, note, mail, licence ...). What is refused is left to the game's own discard, nothing is sent, and the debug file says so (`health.items.ground.refused_not_consumable`, `last_refused`).
- **EntranceHallKey is shared** (only one of you needs it: the door opens in both games): when one of you picks it up it is taken from the other's game like a herb. Only the keys named in `items.SHARE_NAMES` (this one); every other key item still stays in each game.
- **"A message (kind 7 / 8) was never confirmed by the other player" (exe).** The exe's 15 s retry clock started when the script handed the message over, so health, version and event messages queued **before the link was up** (Steam relay still connecting, a save being copied, the other game loading) were reported as failed although nobody could have confirmed them yet. The clock now runs **only while the other player is reachable** (a packet from them in the last 6 s); unreachable messages wait (3 minutes at most) and the log says "(the link to them was down)" when they were given up for that reason. Needs the new exe; the script is unchanged for this.
- **Doors (from the first part of this build):** doors that stayed half-open (a pushed door short of full now opens fully on the `open` message), angles every **0.1 s** and from **2 degrees**, the settled angle is always sent, the other game glides faster and no longer pulls back (`CFG.door`); `health.doors`.
- **Ethan's hands without textures (from the first part of this build):** the first look at a new body no longer counts "still streaming" as a problem (it took the next material file at once and kept the wrong body material), and a saved wrong hand material from 10.11 is forgotten once.
- `CFG.BUILD = "10.12"`, `defaults.json` rebuilt. No new settings.
- **Not done / honest limits.**
  - **The shotgun that is duplicated** (and "one person's shotgun can hold the held door open so the other can grab it"): needs a debug file from a session where one of you picks the shotgun up (`health.item_sync.ring` lists every pickup with what was done about it; the 10.11 file has no pickup at all). Weapons are taken from the other game by design (whoever picks up first), so a copy that stays means the removal did not find it or the pickup was not seen.
  - **No animations for the vans or the wood beams:** scripted moves (the van door, the beams) are described in `claude/re7-motion-bank.md` (motion jack) but not built.
  - **Steam does not connect two players:** I cannot reproduce it here and the exe log is not in the files. The first things to send are the exe log lines starting with `Steam:` from both PCs (signed in as ..., the relay's state, "connected" or "nothing from the other player").
  - Joe (the hand that moves back at the inventory, crouch and block as one) still needs his debug file after a block, a crouch and the inventory.

## What changed in v10.11 (release **0.10**; same protocol v6; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json`, the exe from 10.10 stays**)

Your 11:15 and 11:29 notes: a 10 fps dip every 10 seconds, Mia's left side, and Joe. **Unverified in the real game.**

- **The dip every 10 seconds.** Your 09:13 debug file already shows it: the slowest frames sit exactly 10 s apart (225.5 s, 245.5 s, 265.5 s, 295.5 s, 325.5 s ...) and every time the slow part is "menu/who/weapon/inventory" at 27 to 34 ms. That is the "Play as" module: every 10 s it forced a **walk of the whole scene** (up to 20,000 objects, a name read for each) to look for the player object, and the player-object search walked it again every 60 s (every 5 s when two player objects were in the scene). Now the remembered player object is checked first with one position read: while it is within 3.5 m of the camera (RE7 is first person, the camera is the player's eyes) it is kept and **the scene is not walked at all**. A walk happens only when the object is lost, or is not where the camera is (a swapped object, a cutscene camera), at most every 30 s, doubling (60, 120 ... 300 s) while it keeps finding the same object. The debug file says how often it walked: `health.who.player_search` (`scene_walks`, `kept_without_a_walk`, `last_walk_ms`, `slowest_walk_ms`, `distance_to_camera_m`, `next_walk_wait_s`). If you still see a dip every 10 s after this, send me the debug file: the slowest-frames list then names the part.
- **Mia's left side.** 10.10 hid the pack's hand layer (`pl2020_Lhand`) because the game's own `pl2020` was meant to be her left hand. From `models.json`: `pl2020` is her left ARM with the codex socket (69 joints + `Codex_s`) and no hand of its own, so the result was no hand and a codex. The layer is shown again (the arm with the codex from `pl2020`, the hand from the layer); tell me if she has two hands or is still short of one.
- `CFG.BUILD = "10.11"`, `defaults.json` rebuilt. No new settings.
- **Joe: not changed, needs your files.** "His hand moves back when it does the inventory" and "it does not crouch and block separately": the debug file you sent earlier (a Joe session) shows the idle state playing the game's `Hands_StandDamage_Loop` and no entry at all for guard or inventory, so I cannot tell from it what Joe played. After a session with Joe doing a block, a crouch and the inventory, send `debug.json` (its `game_motion.by_state` and `health.friend.body` lines name the clip and where it came from for each state).

## What changed in v10.10 (release **0.10**; same protocol v6; **a new script AND a new re7mp.exe: update both**; the Steam choice is in the new exe, an old exe still runs with the new script)

Your 9:11 to 9:56 notes of 15 November: Mia's arms, the friend's shot rate, Joe's hands, the crouch that sinks, the ground height, the frame dips every 4 to 6 s, small animations (the van door) and the Steam invite tab. **Everything here is unverified in the real game** (the checks below are against the test harness; the exe's Windows part cannot be compiled where this was written).

- **Steam invites (new tab in the exe).** A third connection choice beside "TCP: I host" / "TCP: I join": **Steam invites**. You type your friend's Steam ID (17 digits, the short account number, a steamcommunity profile address, or a whole invite line), or press **Pick a friend...** (your Steam friends, those playing RE7 first), and press Start; **Copy my invite** puts a line with your own Steam ID on the clipboard. The link is Valve's relay (`ISteamNetworkingMessages`, the same thing the other developer's mod uses): no port, no VPN. The lower Steam ID hosts (both sides work it out; the engine re-checks it with Steam's own answer), both sides accept the other's session at start and again every 2 s until something arrives, only the named friend's packets are used, "connected / nothing for 10 s / back" and the relay's state are written to the log. The exe loads the game's own `steam_api64.dll` by name (no SDK needed to build) and says in the log which function is missing, if one is. The TCP choices are unchanged. **Open question, needs your PC:** whether a second process (re7mp.exe) can start Steam for RE7 while RE7 runs. The plan, the other mod's recipe and the decisions are in the project (`claude/re7-steam-invite-plan.md`). New files: `src/steam_api.hpp`, `src/steam_transport.hpp/.cpp`, `src/steam_dyn.hpp`, `src/steam_dyn_win.cpp` (Windows exe only); `re7mp.ini` keys `transport=steam`, `steam_peer`.
- **The friend's feet are on the floor.** The crouch that "works for a second or two and then goes into the ground": no fixed number can be right, so the friend now **measures its own feet** (the lowest toe joint while it is crouched against the standing reading) and stands the body up until the toe is as high above the floor as when it stood; the slider is a fine tune on top (switch: `friend_ground_feet`, numbers in `friend.json` / `debug.json` under `crouch.feet`).
- **Ground height.** The floor under you is read from your own player object (its position is where the feet are) and used only when the camera is a believable height above it; the old camera-minus-guess is the fallback. Jumps and the camera bob no longer move the floor.
- **The friend shoots no faster than every 300 ms** (slider 100 to 2000 ms, `friend_shot_gap_ms`), whatever the gun.
- **Mia's arms.** The right arm was the game's `pl2010` (arm and a piece of chest); the left side was only the codex hand with the chest layer. The left is now the game's own **`pl2020_hand`** (the arm that goes with the right one; named so because the pack's hair has the same file stem); optional, left out alone if it does not load.
- **Joe's hands.** Joe's folder (`ch9`) holds his body, head and face textures but no hand file. The guessed hand files of the other developer's mod are tried too (his list, his order); Ethan's hands are used for a side only when none of that side's guesses loaded; Joe's real hand files are **learned** from the Model collection (F11) once seen and used from then on.
- **No more model checks that cost frames.** A body or enemy model gets **one verdict** (fine, or given up on) and is not read again until it is made again; the checks that recurred every 4 to 6 s are gone.
- **Small actions (the van door, a wall, the codex).** The other mod found Ethan's own Motion plays them on layers 1 to 7 with a joint mask that keeps the legs on the walk. The script now sends the first moving clip of 1000 or more on those layers (bank 0 only) as an **`act`** event with its layer, mask and blend, and the other body plays it on a second layer over its walk; a switch "Share small actions" in the panel (`act_layers`) and a log of the last 20 are in the debug file.
- **Healing clips** (2790 and the like) are in none of Ethan's weapon sets; a clip of 1000 or more that the set does not have is looked for in the item set (200) before the hands.
- **Motion bank report** (project: `claude/re7-motion-bank.md`): what the bank holds (8 layers, masks, weapon sets, which clips are what), what is sent, what is not and the next steps (scripted moves such as the van door and ladder through the motion jack, an arms-only block, the weapon walk and jog).
- `CFG.BUILD = "10.10"`, `defaults.json` rebuilt. Settings keys: `friend_ground_feet`, `friend_shot_gap_ms`, `act_layers`.
- **Not done / honest limits.** The Steam link is tested only with a mock of Steam; "the inventory is not pre-packaged yet" is not changed because it can mean the inventory animation (the shipped clip is the one you recorded) or the friend's starting items: tell me which; scripted moves (van door, ladder, kick) and an arms-only block are described in the motion bank report but not built.

## What changed in v10.9 (release **0.10**; same protocol v6; **a new script AND a new re7mp.exe: update both** (the old exe still runs with the new script; the window fix and the save log wording are in the new exe))

Your 8 am test list and the notes after it: the shooting distance, the block distance, the revive after a game over, the crouch sliders, the friend's floating crouch, Mia's chest, Pete's first load, Joe's hands, the cut-off writing in the exe, the door fps note and a plan for the Steam invite. **Everything here is unverified in the real game** (the checks below are against the test harness).

- **Shooting distance 5 m.** Ethan's friend shoots enemies within **5 m of you** (was 10). It is a slider now (2 to 25 m, "It shoots enemies this close to YOU", settings key `friend_shoot_m`). I read "attack distance" as this, the distance at which it starts shooting; the **enemy's reach to hurt the friend (1.6 m) is a separate number and was not changed**.
- **The friend blocks only within 1 m of an enemy** (it blocked from 5 m). It lets go at 1.4 m (so it does not flicker); a slider in the Ethan's friend box ("It blocks only when an enemy is this close to IT", 0.5 to 5 m, key `friend_guard_m`). The enemy's reach to hurt it is still 1.6 m, so an enemy 1.0 to 1.6 m away can now hurt it without it blocking: tell me if you want those two numbers tied together. With a gun and ammo it shoots instead of blocking, as before.
- **After a game over Ethan's friend revives and teleports to you.** The game's own game-over signal arms it; when the reload that follows has finished (the loading gate was shut and has been open for 2 s; if no load is seen it goes out 45 s after the game over) the script sends the exe a new command, **`revive`** (kind 11): the friend gets up at **full health** (whether it was down, hurt or fine) and is **put next to you** (the same snap as "Move bot to me": a down bot stays where it fell, one closer than 40 m would only walk). It then sends `up`, which an exe from before 10.9 obeys (it gets up where it is: no teleport without the new exe). A switch in the Ethan's friend box ("After a game over it gets up at full health and comes to me", on, key `friend_revive_after_over`), a **button to try it without dying** ("Ethan's friend: revive it and bring it to me", next to "make it get up"), and the debug file's `health.friend.after_game_over` (waiting, load seen, game overs seen, revives sent, last). It does nothing when the other player is a real player.
- **Crouch sliders go down to -2 m** (negative = lower the body) in both places. The loader accepts -2 to +0.4 (+0.6 on the friend's), a saved value out of range is ignored.
- **The friend's crouch no longer floats, and gets down at once.** Cause, from the code and your saved settings: the friend's camera is pinned at the standing height above its floor, so nothing sinks when it crouches, but the body was still stood up by the crouch compensation (0.5 m + the slider): with the slider at 0.0 it floated 0.5 m. The friend now has **its own crouch slider** (`friend_crouch_lift`, default 0.0, -2 to +0.6; the one in Developer tools > Real body is for a real player's body, whose camera does sink and is unchanged). Its crouch pose also eases in at 16 per second instead of 9 (about 0.15 s to be down; the 10.8 build took about 0.33 s plus the clip blend). The debug file measures it: `health.friend.body.crouch.body_down_after_s` and `body_lift_m`.
- **Mia's chest.** Her pack body has the chest in the `pl2023` part, in the layer **`pl2000_VR`** (the other developer's note: "without pl2023 her chest and left hand were missing"). The 10.4 rule "hide ..._VR layers on a body that has its own hands" hid it. Layers named `..._VR` that are not VR hands (`pl2000_VR`, and Joe's `pl9002_Leg_VR_m`, his legs) are left alone now, and a saved "pl2000_VR hidden" from 10.8 is dropped once. This is my reading of the files and the other mod's note, not something I have seen: if the hole is still there, tick/untick the box "VR hands layer: pl2000_VR" in Developer tools > Real body, and send the debug file: the Face lines now list **every layer of every part with on/off** (`Face: pl2023 layers: ...`) and which layers were changed.
- **Pete (Em9200) failed once, then loaded.** His mesh took longer than 10 s to stream in the first time (`MeshReady=false ... after 10 s`), and the retry lock (25 s) refused the next try ("no model of Pete Walken is in this scene"); by the third try the game had it loaded. The wait for a body's mesh is **30 s** now (the settle's rebuild wait 35 s) and the retry lock is 6 s.
- **Joe's hands.** The guessed hand files (`ch9/.../pl9010.mesh`, `pl9020.mesh`) **do not exist** (both "not loaded after 10 s" in your file, which also cost 15 s and a rebuild), and the pack has none. Joe now **borrows Ethan's hands** (`pl0010`, `pl0020`, the game's own files, which load at once); they are optional (if they do not load only they are left out). Joe's own hand files are still unknown.
- **The exe window** is 100 px wider and 16 px taller; the note next to the "Appear as:" drop-down has the whole row and two lines, the status lines the whole width, the save line two lines (nothing is cut off any more). Needs a rebuild of the exe.
- **Doors (the fps dip).** Your debug file says the door code itself costs little (the doors part of the frame profile: 0.0 ms average, 1.0 ms worst; one angle message in the whole session; only the first angle of a push goes to the journal). What it cannot see is the `setInteract` hook, which runs inside the game's own call; it returns at once when you are alone now and its time is counted (`health.doors.setInteract_hook_ms_total`, calls and skips in `counts`). As you suggested: angles are sent **every 0.35 s and only when the door moved 5 degrees** (was 0.2 s and 3), the other game swings the door smoothly toward each reported angle, and asks the game only when it moved a quarter of a degree. The biggest frames in your file (45 to 82 ms) are `net send/read` and the enemy scene walk, not doors: that is the next thing to look at if the dips stay.
- **The wrong save.** The exe sent `data013Slot.bin` at 07:57:10; your folder shows **the game wrote exactly that file at 07:57:00** and `data014Slot.bin` was last written on 11 Nov, so the exe sent what the game saved. Either the game's slot "14" is the file `013` or the save went to 13. The exe's log line now says "= Load screen row 13", so you can compare it with the row you saved in (tell me which, and I will correct the mapping).
- **Steam invite: a plan and the groundwork.** The plan is in the project (`claude/re7-steam-invite-plan.md`): the game's own `steam_api64.dll` exports everything needed (friend invite with a connect string, relayed player-to-player messages), the exe would load it, both routes (Steam and direct) in one invite line, either can win. In this build only the **invite line** exists (`src/invite.hpp`: build, parse, reject junk, 200 characters at most; 150+ checks in the C++ suite). Nothing talks to Steam yet: step 1 is a small probe on your PC (can a second process initialise Steam for RE7 while it runs).
- `CFG.BUILD = "10.9"`, `defaults.json` rebuilt. Settings keys: `friend_shoot_m`, `friend_guard_m`, `friend_crouch_lift`, `friend_revive_after_over`, `vr_chest_10_9`.
- Tests: the Lua smoke test (all checks pass; new ones for the shooting and block distances and their clamps and sliders, the crouch slider, the layer rule for Mia's chest and Joe's legs with the one-time migration, the slow mesh wait, Joe's borrowed hands, the door angle rate and the hook counters) and the C++ suite with the new invite checks and the `revive` command (all pass). A settings key that is not in the settings change signature is never saved on its own: the new ones are in it, and a test caught the two I had missed.
- **Not done / honest limits.** Nothing here is verified in the game. Mia's chest and the door dip are best guesses from the files; the Steam invite does not exist yet beyond the invite line; the wrong-save question needs your answer (which row the Load screen shows for the save you made at 07:57).

## What changed in v10.8 (release **0.10**; same protocol v6; **a new re7mp.exe AND a new script: update both** (an old exe still runs with the new script; only the friend's exact self-heal needs the new exe))

Ethan's friend **fights**: it crouches when you crouch, shoots the enemies near you with **its own guns and ammo**, tells you when it is out, heals itself and takes hits. Clancy's head and hands, the crouch height, the crouched block and the inventory clip are fixed again, and the window loses its second REFramework row. **None of it is verified in the real game.** It comes from your notes of 14 Nov 23:35 and your 10.7 files.

- **Clancy's head and hands (the black head and hands in your screenshot).** Cause, from `model_status.json` and `debug.json`: the pack's head (`pl3002`) showed "no materials" at the first look, because everything was still loading, the next material file was taken at once (his **body's**, which has other materials) and that wrong choice was written to `model_status.json`, so every later start used it. Now a pack file gets a **second look** before another is tried, the body's file is never a candidate for the head, the next one is the game's own `pl3050.mdf2` (his head's), and a body file kept for the head or the hands by 10.7 is **ignored and forgotten**. The hands (`pl3010`, `pl3020`, the game's files) take the game's **`em3010.mdf2` / `em3020.mdf2`** first (the files the game uses for them).
- **Crouch height.** The body stood too low (his knees in the floor). The extra lift is **0.20 m** now (it was 0.08; the slider goes to 0.40). A saved 0.08 (the old default) is not kept as a choice. **Where the slider is:** Developer tools > "Real body (experiment: a second Ethan of my own)" > "Crouch: stand the body this much higher (m; if the crouch sinks into the floor)", and the same slider is in the "Ethan's friend" box. It is the same setting.
- **Crouch, crouch while moving, block, crouched block and inventory.** They are recognised on you; what plays on the other body now: crouch, crouch walk and the inventory use **the saved clip recorded from your Ethan** (the bank has no clip for them; the generic Idle/Move node of layer 0 plays whatever the state), a block is the game's guard clip on the overlay layer (as in 10.4), and a **crouched block or crouched inventory** is the crouch clip with the **arms of the saved block / inventory clip** over it (`health.clips.arms_over_the_crouch`). **New: `health.game_motion.by_state`** says, for every state (crouch, crouch_walk, guard, inventory...), what played last and where it came from (the game's clip with its id, or the saved clip, or no clip) and how often, so "not found" can be read off the file.
- **Ethan's friend crouches when you crouch** (so you can both hide from the same thing, though it does not know it is hiding).
- **Ethan's friend fights** (`CFG.friend.fight`; switches and numbers in the "Ethan's friend" box, remembered):
  - **Its own weapons and ammo.** Weapons and ammo are **not shared any more**: a gun or ammo you give it (F7, within 3 m) leaves your inventory and is kept by it (`friend.json`). It shoots a gun with that gun's own kind of ammo (HandgunBullet for a handgun, ShotgunBullet for a shotgun, ...; by the item's name, or the game's category). Every shot takes one round out of **its** inventory.
  - **Target: the enemy nearest to YOU** within 10 m of you and 25 m of the friend. **Line of sight is ignored** (your instruction; the ray casts are unreliable anyway). Dead and parked enemies do not count, and a dead enemy is not shot again.
  - **Damage by the gun and the ammo it has:** the gun's table damage (`CFG.wpn.DAMAGE`; a gun that is not in it gets 100 / 90 / 50 / 1200 for handgun / shotgun / machine gun / magnum) times the same scale the other player's shots use (the Weapons section's "Gun damage scale", 2.1), handed to the game's own damage call (the host's `HIT.apply`, with the correction for what the game really takes off). It uses the carried gun with the most damage per second and keeps it until its ammo is gone. One shot every 0.55 s (handgun), 1.1 s (shotgun), 0.14 s (machine gun), 1.3 s (magnum), a reload after every magazine (15, 6, 30, 6 rounds; 1.7 to 2.6 s).
  - **It says when it is out of ammo** ("I'm out of ammo!" at the top left, every 25 s while an enemy is near), warns at 5 rounds left, and says it has "nothing to shoot with" when it carries no gun.
  - **Stance:** gun out, aiming and the shot pose, facing the enemy it shoots at. With a gun and ammo it **shoots instead of blocking**; it blocks (as in 10.7) when it cannot shoot.
  - **It heals itself** from its own inventory when **its own** health is at or below a level (default 40 %, slider 10 to 80 %): the weakest item that lifts it over the level, 2.4 s with the heal pose, the item leaves its inventory when the health is given. Your health comes first (it does not heal itself while it is helping you). With nothing to use it says so.
  - **Damage taken: simulated.** The game's enemy AI does not know the friend exists, so I cannot make an enemy aim at it. An enemy within 1.6 m of the friend takes **8 %** of its health off every 1.2 s (a slider, 0 = never; 40 % of that while it blocks). The hit goes to the exe as the "friendly fire" event it already took (9.33); at 1 health it goes down and you get it up the usual way (F8 within 3 m, or a heal). A friend that is down does not shoot or heal itself.
  - **Where to see it:** the panel ("WHAT IT DOES FOR ITSELF": state, target, the gun in hand, rounds in the magazine, ammo per kind, its health, shots / hits / damage dealt / kills / ammo used / reloads / out of ammo / heals / hits taken), `friend: ...` lines in the log (takes up its gun, each shot with the damage and the enemy's health before and after, running low, out of ammo, uses a Herb on itself, ... reached it: it takes N damage) and `health.friend.fights` in the debug file. Two test buttons: put a handgun and 30 bullets into its inventory, and take its guns and ammo away.
  - Settings keys: `friend_fights`, `friend_heals_itself`, `friend_self_below`, `friend_hurt_pct`.
- **The exe.** (1) The **"REFramework:" row under the updater and its Install button are gone** (the "Install / update mod" button does the same, asks before it replaces a REFramework, and its download progress is now a line in the log at every 10 %); the log box moved up and the window is 30 px shorter. (2) A new command for the friend, `hp:<n>` (kind 11), sets its health exactly (2 to 1000): the self-heal needs it, because "up" only gave back all of it. An exe from before 10.8 ignores it; the script then says so (`health.friend.fights.exe_older_than_10_8`), and uses "up" instead when the heal would have been nearly all of it.
- `CFG.BUILD = "10.8"`, `defaults.json` rebuilt. Tests: the Lua smoke test (5769 checks), the new scenario 32 (crouching with you, shots and ammo accounting, out of ammo and low ammo, the right kind of ammo, no gun, nothing near you, a real player, the switch, the self-heal and the exact health, the exe answering or not, hits taken, down, the stance), new Clancy material tests (a slow head gets a second look, pl3050 next, the body's file is never taken, em3010 / em3020 first, a poisoned kept file is ignored), the crouched-arms test, the 10.5 gift tests rewritten for ammo that is accepted now, and the C++ suite (the new `hp:` command).
- **Not done / honest limits.** Enemies cannot really target the friend (the damage is simulated by distance). It still does not back away from you (the exe only follows, a change in the exe's bot logic) and does not hand items back. The gun and ammo kinds are matched by the items' names (real weapon item names have not been seen in a file yet: if a gun is not recognised, `health.friend.fights` lists what it carries and what it took for a gun). The Joe hands and the GameObjectsDisplay crash are as in 10.7.

## What changed in v10.7 (release **0.10**; same protocol v6; **the script only: update `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json`; no exe change** (the exe of 10.5 stays))

Ethan's friend **acts for itself**, the menu is cleaned up, and the model pack no longer throws Clancy out because of a hand. **None of it is verified in the real game.** It comes from your notes of 14 Nov 22:25 and your 10.5 files (the Project doc `re7-findings.md` has the reading of them).

- **Crouch, crouch walk, inventory and a crouched block.** They were "recognised on you but not played on the other body": the generic Idle/Move node was played instead of the state-specific clips (and a block or the inventory outranked the crouch). Now the recorded clips (made from the real Ethan) are played for them (`CFG.gmot.OWN_CLIP`: crouch, crouch_walk, inventory), the label for "crouched while blocking or in the inventory" is the crouch one (`CFG.clip.choose`), and Ethan's `Hands_Crouch_Loop` / `Hands_CrouchDamage_Loop` / `Hands_WalkCrouch_Loop` are on the standard list of clips the script knows by name. Ids were read from your `motion_banks.json` (0/12 Crouch_Loop, 0/13 CrouchDamage_Loop, 0/40-43 WalkCrouch, 0/2300 Guard_Loop, 0/2790 Recovery).
- **Ethan's friend acts for itself (the new default of "Ethan's friend does": "Acts for itself").** `CFG.friend.body`. re7mp.exe still only moves it (it is told "state:copy"); everything the body shows comes from the script:
  - **Your pose is not copied** (your crouch, gun, aim, shot, block and inventory bits from the exe are dropped; "Copies me" is still in the list). It shows the heal pose while it works on you, **blocks while an enemy is within 5 m** (it lets go 1 s after the enemy is 6.5 m away or gone), and otherwise walks, runs or stands by how fast it really moves. It faces the enemy it blocks.
  - **It faces the way it last moved** (it kept your camera's direction before, so the model looked outwards all the time). Standing still it keeps the last direction; before it has moved at all it faces you.
  - **Grounded on your trail.** The exe puts it at YOUR height, so on stairs or any step it hung in the air. The script keeps a trail of the places you walked (a point every 0.25 m, the floor under your camera; 500 points = 125 m; a jump of 20 m or more, a load, starts a new trail) and sets the friend's height from the nearest point of it (within 2 m, else the nearest within 6 m, else your floor). It is the technique of the other co-op mod (its bot follows a recorded path, no ray casts: they are not reliable in this game; the idea is from its files, the code is ours).
  - **Enemies it sees:** the enemy list of the enemy sync, or, when that is not running, a scan of its own once a second (`health.friend.body.enemy_scans`); parked, dead and placeholder objects do not count. Only enemies within about 6.5 m are looked at in detail.
  - **Where to see it:** the panel (Its body: BLOCKING / not blocking, the nearest enemy, the floor height and how many heights came from the trail), `health: Ethan's friend blocks: Em2000 is 3.0 m from it` and `lowers its guard` in the log, and `health.friend.body` in the debug file.
  - **Not done yet (10.8):** it does not fight, has no ammo of its own, does not heal itself, and enemies do not target it or hurt it. It also does not back away from you: the exe only follows.
- **Menu.** Retired: **Start together**, **Co-op saves**, **Find item hooks (tracer)** and **Model probe**. Their code is still in the script; a hidden switch brings them back (`"show_retired_panels": true` in `re7mp/settings.json`; it is kept when the settings are saved). **"Send me this"** is gone as a top-level box: the debug files are one compact box at the top of **Developer tools** ("Debug files for the developer"), with the save and clear buttons first and the numbered buttons in three groups (Game and items, Enemies, Player and events); every button keeps its label.
- **Clancy and Mia from the natives folder (the pack).** Your 10.5 file shows Mia from the pack (`made_from_the_pack: pl2000`) but **Clancy fell back to the game's own models**: his hands (`pl3010`, `pl3020`) are the game's files, their materials were not found in the two rebuilds the check allows, and one hand failing made the whole pack fail. Now (1) the material file that worked for his hands in your tests (the body's own `pl3001.mdf2`) is tried first while the pack is installed, and (2) **a part that is not from the pack is optional**: if it still fails after the rebuilds (or its mesh never loads), only that part is left out (`health.model_pack.parts_left_out`) and the pack's body stays. "Forget the kept material files" also brings the left-out hands back for another try.
- **Joe's hands.** The pack has none and the game's file names are not known (the other developer tried `pl9000/pl9010/pl9010` and `pl9000/pl9000/pl9011` and friends, all empty). Two **new guesses** that follow Ethan's pattern (`pl0000/pl0000/pl0010`) are asked for: `ch9/Character/Player/pl9000/pl9000/pl9010.mesh` and `pl9020.mesh`, as optional parts: if they exist they show, if not they are taken away (after 10 s) and Joe stays from the pack. **If they do not exist, the real file names have to be collected**: play End of Zoe as Joe and press F11 (Collect model data); `re7mp/models.json` then lists his hand parts.
- **GameObjectsDisplay.** REFramework's own Game Objects Display is not part of this mod (nothing in the script calls it or draws labels for objects). The only crash in your logs (14 Nov 13:46) is in REFramework's own code after the Object Explorer's dump, not in a script call. No log of a crash with Game Objects Display on was in your files, so nothing was changed for it; see the Project doc.
- `CFG.BUILD = "10.7"`, `defaults.json` rebuilt. Tests: the Lua smoke test (5637 checks), new scenarios 30 (the friend's body: pose not copied, block near Jack and let go, which enemy and clip, facing, the stairs and the trail, the trail reset and its limit, the heal clip) and 31 (the menu: the four sections hidden and brought back by the switch, the debug box inside Developer tools) and the pack tests f2b-f2d (a broken hand is left out and the pack stays, Joe's guessed hands that load and that do not, not asked for again at every make). The drop-down tests of 9.38 follow the new order of the list ("Acts for itself" first). No C++ was changed, so the C++ suite was not run.

## What changed in v10.6 (release **0.10**; same protocol v6; **the script only: update `re7mp.lua`; no exe change** (the exe of 10.5 stays))

Ethan's friend **helps you**: it gets you up when you are down and heals you when your health is low. **None of it is verified in the real game.** All of it is in the script (`CFG.friend.ai`, next to the health code), because the script knows your health, how far the friend is and what the friend carries; re7mp.exe still only walks the friend after you (2 m away; 1.6 m in "Beside you"; "Stays put" stays put, so it only helps when it happens to be within reach).

- **Revive.** You are down (downed mode) and the friend has been within the revive distance (3 m) for the revive time (3 s): you get up with the usual "health after being revived" (30 %). No item is used (the wait is the price, as with F8). It shows its use pose while it works on you, the screen says "Ethan's friend: helping you up 1.2 / 3 s", and walking out of reach stops it (it starts again from 0 when it is back). It uses the same two sliders as F8 (revive distance, seconds to stay).
- **Heal.** Your health is at or below the level (default **40 %**, a slider from 10 to 80 %) for 1.2 s, the friend is within the revive distance and it carries a healing item: it uses one on you. The use takes 2.4 s (its heal pose shows, "Ethan's friend: using a Herb on you 1.0 / 2.4 s"); at the end your health goes up and **only then** the item leaves **its own** inventory (`re7mp/friend.json`), so walking out of reach, going down or healing yourself meanwhile spends nothing. It picks the weakest item that lifts you back over the level, else the strongest it carries. A second heal waits 3 s. With no healing item it says so once ("give it a Herb or First Aid Med").
- **How much an item gives back** (percent of your maximum health; an estimate from RE7 guides, not read from the game): Herb 25 %, First Aid Med (any other healing item) 33 %, Strong First Aid Med 100 %. An item counts as a healing item by the game's category (3, when this script has seen the item) or, if not seen, by its name (herb, remedy, first aid, medic); Chem Fluid is not one. Revive first, then heal: a revived player is at 30 %, which is under the default level, so the friend then uses a Herb on you if it has one.
- **Switches (Ethan's friend section of the panel, remembered):** "Ethan's friend helps me", "It gets me up when I am down", "It uses a healing item ... when my health is low", the level slider, and a test button that puts your health 10 % under the level. The friend does nothing for a real player on the network, while it is down itself (the panel's "make it go down"), or when the box is unticked. Without downed mode it still heals you.
