- **What the mod is doing, where to see it:** the panel section (Now / Last / counts), `health: Ethan's friend: ...` lines in the log (is helping you up / stopped helping you up: it is N m away / is using a Herb on you / stopped using the Herb: ... (nothing spent) / used a Herb on you: health A -> B of C (it has N left) / carries no healing item), and `health.friend.helps` in the debug file (switches, level, distance, status, what it carries and what each gives, counts, the last heal). New settings keys: `friend_helps`, `friend_revive`, `friend_heal`, `friend_heal_below`.
- `CFG.BUILD = "10.6"`, `defaults.json` rebuilt. Tests: the Lua smoke test, new scenario 29 (revive after 3 s with no item, too far and a short visit, revive then heal, heal timing and the item leaving the inventory only at the end, the cooldown, walking out of reach mid-use, the weakest-item choice, nothing to use, the switches and the level and their saving, a real player never helped, the friend down itself, downed mode off), then the whole suite once (5408 checks, 0 failed). No C++ was changed, so the C++ tests were not run again.
- **Not built yet:** the friend does not shoot or hurt enemies, does not hand items back and does not walk to you from "Stays put". Still to talk through: how it fights (an attack the game accepts, ammo from your inventory).

## What changed in v10.5 (release **0.10**; same protocol v6; **new re7mp.exe AND a new script: update both**)

The loopback bot becomes **Ethan's friend**, an AI helper you play the game with alone. This build is the first step: the name, how it moves, what it looks like, and the rule for weapons and ammo. **None of it is verified in the real game.** The window code (`gui_win.cpp`) cannot be compiled where this was written, so the exe needs a build on your side; the engine, the tests and the script were run.

- **Renamed.** The window says "Ethan's friend (play alone)" instead of "Loopback test (fake player)", the engine's player is named **Ethan's friend** (it shows over the friend, in the logs and in the label of a co-op save it echoes: "CO-OP Ethan's friend ..."), and the script's panel and log say so too. The script accepts **both names**, so a new script with an old exe, or the reverse, still treats it as the friend. Debug file keys changed with it: `who.loopback_bot_*` is `who.friend_*`, `motion_banks.other_layers.fed_to_the_loopback_bot` is `fed_to_the_friend`. (A real player who types "Ethan's friend" as their name would be treated as the friend by the script: don't.)
- **Orbit is gone.** The friend **follows you** by default (a walk when close, a run when far, it stops about 2 m away and is put next to you when you are far); **Stays put** and **Beside you** stay, and the button is now "Move friend to me". An old `re7mp.ini` that says `bot=orbit` means follow. The script's one-state animation tests (Walk, Run, Gun walking ...) still circle a spot at that pace; they are test aids, not a mode.
- **"Appear as" is the friend's look.** In the friend mode the drop-down above Start (its label changes to "Appear as:") picks what the **friend** looks like and does not touch yours; in network mode it is "Play as" for you, as before. The exe tells the script by itself (`char`), so the script's panel box for it is no longer needed (it still works, and whichever was picked last counts). Each mode remembers its own pick (`play_as` and `friend_as` in `re7mp.ini`). The list now has **Chris, Zoe and Joe** as well (the full models in the pack must be installed; without them the script keeps Ethan). The first entry in the friend mode is "Default (the script's own body)": it says nothing about its character. New CLI flag: `--bot-as <key>`.
- **Separate from the online part.** Nothing in this build sends friend data over the network or runs while a real player is connected: the exe's friend is `Mode::Loopback` on 127.0.0.1, the script's friend code is `CFG.friend` and acts only when the other player is named Ethan's friend (`CFG.friend.present()`), and its inventory has its own file (`re7mp/friend.json`).
- **Weapons and ammo are shared.** The friend has none of its own: it uses yours. A weapon, a stack weapon or ammo you offer it (F7 or the panel's Give buttons) is refused, nothing is sent and you keep it, and the screen says why. The kind comes from the game's own item category, as in the item sync; a kind that cannot be read is not refused. **Not built yet:** the friend does not shoot, so no ammo is used and no enemy is hurt.
- **Its own inventory.** Everything else you give it leaves your inventory when it confirms (the same 3 m rule and the same 'last one' rule as for a player) and is kept by it in `re7mp/friend.json`, so a restart does not lose it. The panel has a new section, "Ethan's friend (an AI helper you play with alone: no network)", that lists what it carries, what it took and what was refused as shared; `health.friend` in the debug file has the same. **Not built yet:** it does not use items, hand anything back or pick things up.
- `CFG.BUILD = "10.5"`, `defaults.json` rebuilt. The test suites: the Lua smoke test (new scenario 28: ammo refused, an ordinary item taken and listed, the old name, a real player unaffected, the inventory read back) and the C++ tests (the friend's look at the start and while running, the new characters, Follow as the default, the orbit tests rewritten).
- **Next, to talk through:** how the friend fights (it needs a way to hurt enemies that the game accepts, and to take its ammo from your inventory), when it uses a heal, whether it can hand items back, and how it picks things up.

## What changed in v10.4 (release **0.10**; same protocol v6; script only: update `re7mp.lua` on both computers; no exe change)

From your test of 10.3 (blocking did not show, some clips played and some did not, the rig stood idle while the clip id changed, Mia was only half the pack, Clancy's VR hands, a slow start). **None of this is verified in the real game; the debug file says what it did.**

- **Blocking and other overlay clips.** The game plays a block (`Hands_Guard_Loop`), a heal (`Hands_Recovery`, `Hands_Injection`), a melee and a reload on **layers 1 and 2**, while layer 0 stays on the locomotion tree node (0/0 Idle). That is why the clip id changed but the body looked idle. The sender (`CFG.mbank`) now reads layers 1 to 5 as well, follows which of them moves, and sends the clip that matters as a new event `mol` (`<bank>_<id>_<label>`, `0_0_none` when it ends; sent on change, repeated every 1.5 s while it plays, never faster than 0.15 s). The receiver plays that clip on its body first (`how = "their layer clip"`), then falls back to the clip of that name or a recorded clip. The guard and heal flags of the position packets also come from the layer now. Every (bank, id) pair seen on those layers is listed in `health.motion_banks.other_layers.every_pair_seen_on_those_layers`, so a clip that is not covered yet can be added from your file.
- **The rig is checked before the clips run.** A body whose rig lacks the joints the clips drive (22 names, at least 14 needed) is left to the recorded clips and its state says so (`this body's rig does not fit`); it no longer counts as a failure of the whole thing. This is for the new models, whose rigs differ from Ethan's.
- **No arms-out pose, no 26 scans.** The new Motion is switched **off** after it is set up and on with the first clip. The active banks repeat the ids 0 to 2 (one set per weapon type); each id is listed once, and a body made again does not list them again (`scans_skipped`). If a disabled Motion does not bind, it is switched on again and the layer is rebuilt before the test fails.
- **Faster.** Retry timers: first retry 4 s to 3 s, later retries 2 s to 1 s; the model check every 5 s to 3 s (quiet 10 s to 8 s, grace 4 s to 3 s, fast 2 s to 1.5 s); the self-test plays the walk clip for 1 s instead of 2 s.
- **Mia.** The head of the full models (`pl2002`, `pl2102`) is layered like the old one: her corrupted skin layers are switched off and her own on (`CFG.body.face.LAYERED` now has `em2050`, `pl2002`, `pl2102`), and every part of a body is read, so a layer you tick in the panel is applied on any of them. The panel boxes show the layers of every part. Whether the pack's body part `pl2001` is used instead of the old Mia body is shown in `health.model_pack`; if she still looks like Evil Mia, send `models.json` and the panel layer list.
- **Chris, Zoe and Joe from the pack.** Added to `CFG.ids.LIST`, `CFG.pack.ENTRY` and `CFG.who.LIST`: Chris Redfield (Not a Hero, `pl1000`, files under `coop/ch8/...`), Zoe Baker (Daughters, `pl3400`, body `pl3401` and parts `pl3402`, `pl3410`, `pl3420`), and Joe Baker (End of Zoe, `pl9000`, files under `coop/ch9/...`). The pack's playable Baker is Joe (End of Zoe); you wrote Jack. Jack Baker the enemy (`em3000`) is a different model the mod already has; if you meant him as a playable body, tell me which files. Chris and Joe have no hand files in the table on purpose (the names are not confirmed). They need the pack (`modpack.json`) like the other full models; without it the body stays Ethan and the panel says the character is not available. Zoe is lifted 4 cm (`CFG.body.LIFT`); the other new bodies may need the same, so look at their feet. **The "Play as" list in re7mp.exe is not updated (the exe was not rebuilt); pick them in the in-game panel.**
- **Clancy's VR hands.** A material layer whose name is `..._VR`, `_VR_...` or `VR_...` is switched off while the body has hands of its own (a part named `pl0010`/`pl0020`, `pl3010`/`pl3020`...), and restored when the rule is unticked. New box "Hide the VR hands layer (a material named ..._VR) on a body that has its own hands, e.g. Clancy's" (remembered; `vr_layers_off`); the layer boxes now say "VR hands layer: <name>" or "Head layer: <name>". The names are guessed (`pl3000_VR`); the panel lists the real ones, and a ticked box for a name that is different also works.
- **Loopback bot.** It now feeds its own motion events back to the receiver, so the motion path runs when you test alone.
- `CFG.BUILD = "10.4"`; new in the debug file: `health.motion_banks.other_layers`, `health.game_motion.body.rig`, `health.game_motion.counts.their_layer` / `scans_skipped`, `health.game_motion.state = "this body's rig does not fit"`.
- **Notes for the next build:** `TreeLayer:get_HighestWeightMotionNode()` returns the node a tree layer plays; its members are not dumped yet and it is not used. Fight moves, ladders and grabs come from dynamic banks (ids 3 to 31) and are not shared yet.

## What changed in v10.3 (release **0.10**; script only: update `re7mp.lua` on both computers)

- **The note about discarding is at the top of the screen.** While the inventory is open and another player is there, the two gold lines ("Discarding an item here DROPS it on the ground for the other player too ..." and "To hand an item over instead ... press [F7]") are drawn at the top, in the middle, instead of the bottom. Same box as before ("Show the [F7] give hint ..."). The `[F7] give ...` line stays top left.
- `CFG.BUILD = "10.3"`; the changes of 10.2 (the game's own animations) are unchanged.
- **Notes from the other developer's mod on the motion banks** (their Claude's description of their code, 2026-11-14; not run by me): Ethan's `.motbank` has 29 banks (active ids 0-2 are the ones his sets use), dynamic banks the game adds at run time are ids 3-31, the other mod's own slot is 30; a clip is always the pair (bank id, clip id) read from layer 0 (`get_MotionBankID`, `get_MotionID`) with frame and speed; `getMotionInfo(bank, id)` is checked before playing so a missing clip is skipped instead of T-posing. That is what `CFG.gmot` does too (it lists the active banks of the body's Motion in the debug file, `health.game_motion.clip_names`); dynamic banks (fight moves, ladder, grabs) are not read or shared by us yet.

## What changed in v10.2 (release **0.10**; same protocol v6; **the script only: update `re7mp.lua` on both computers; no exe change**)

Your 2026-11-14 14:26 note: *"all animations should come from the motion bank"*. Until 10.1 the other player's body was posed by clips **I recorded** (joint angles of Ethan walking, running ...). Now the game's own clips play on it (`CFG.gmot`, `re7mp.lua`):

1. **How.** The body (a `via.GameObject` with a mesh) gets a `via.motion.Motion` component of its own. It is given the **bank asset and the joint map of your own player's Motion** (the way the other developer's ghost is animated; Ethan's are kept as soon as you have played him), a hand-built `via.motion.TreeLayer` on slot 0, and layer 0 is told which bank and clip to play with the six-parameter `changeMotion` (the call the enemy clips already use).
2. **Which clip**, in this order: (a) **the pair the other player's Ethan plays right now** (the `mot` event; it now carries their weapon set too, `"<bank>_<clip>_<label>.<set>"`, and the body asks for the same set with `set_TargetBankType` before playing it; used only when your Motion has that clip); (b) **by what they are doing** (the label the recorded-clip player already chooses): the clip of that *name* in the bank: `Hands_StandDamage_Loop` (idle), `Hands_Walk_Loop`, `Hands_Jog_Loop` (run), `Hands_WalkCrouch_Loop` (crouch walk, and held still for crouch), walk and run speeded up or slowed down to how fast they really move; (c) a pair the 10.1 detector learned for that label; (d) **nothing found: the recorded clip plays for that label** and the body's Motion is switched off meanwhile.
3. **Every step is separate** (mesh ready -> Motion component -> bank asset and joint map -> layer -> wait for the banks to load -> list the clips by name -> **self-test**), one native call each, each with a crash marker (`lastcall.json`, stage `gmot`). The **self-test** plays the walk clip for 2 s: the layer's frame must advance **and** the body's joints must move; if not, it fails.
4. **A failure never leaves the body without animation.** Any failed step, a layer that freezes three times, a `changeMotion` that keeps failing, or a crash inside it in the last session: the recorded clips animate the body again, and the top left says once: *"Motion: The game's own animations could not be used (why). The recorded ones are used instead."* (nothing is said when it works). A crash inside it switches it off at the next start.
5. **The box** in "Other player's body: ..." : *Animate it with the game's own clips (the motion bank) instead of my recorded ones* (default ticked; remembered in `re7mp/game_motion.json`), with one line saying what it is doing now. Unticking and ticking again tries again.
6. **Debug file** `health.game_motion`: the state and why, where the bank asset and joint map came from (their paths and types), each step's result (counts of layers, active banks, joints), the self-test numbers, the pair playing now and how it was chosen, counts (their clip / by name / learned / hand-backs / missing pairs), **every clip name of the bank** (up to 400, for the next build's name table), the pairs of theirs your banks do not have, and the last 80 lines of what it did (the same lines go to the journal for the first moves).
7. **Version:** `CFG.BUILD = "10.2"`; `kReleaseVersion` stays "0.10".

**Tests:** the Lua smoke suite has a new block with a fake game whose body Motion answers like the game's (setup in steps, bank listing, self-test passing, joints that do not move, a layer that does not advance, a refused component, a failing `changeMotion`, their pair with a weapon set, a pair that is not in the banks, a label with no game clip, a crash marker, the file and the box, no source Motion yet, a body made again).

**Not verified (needs your game; nothing here could be tried without it):** that `createComponent("via.motion.Motion")` on a bare object works, that the bank asset and joint map of your player's Motion can be given to it, that the Motion updates without the game's own `app.MotionManager` and animates the joints of this body (the self-test is there to say), the clip names (taken from the other developer's notes), that a weapon set's banks load after `set_TargetBankType`, and that a Motion switched off lets the recorded clips write the joints. **If it fails, send the debug file: `game_motion.lines` says which step.**

## What changed in v10.1 (release **0.10**; same protocol v6; **new re7mp.exe AND a new script: update both on both computers**)

Version 0.9 is released, so the release label is now **0.10** and builds are numbered 10.x. Your 2026-11-14 13:35 note, in its parts:

1. **The other player's character comes from the full models** (Nexus RE7 mod 86, "Third Person Full Model Placeholders RT (2022)" by AfroJackX), head included, for Ethan, Mia and Clancy (and their other forms the pack has), instead of the game's own character files. re7mp.exe writes a small flag file (`reframework\data\re7mp\modpack.json`) when the pack is installed under `natives\stm\coop`; the script asks for the pack's files by their `coop/Character/Player/...` paths only when that file is there. The hands the pack does not have come from the game as before. If a pack model will not load (the mesh never arrives, or the body cannot be made), that character goes back to the game's own model for the rest of the session and the top left says so. Without the pack nothing changes. Ethan's copy from the scene is still the first choice when the scene has him (the 9.35 rule: Ethan from files had no textures); the pack is used for him when it is installed and the scene has no copy to take. **Unverified in the real game**: whether the pack's models load at these paths, and with their textures.
2. **A check 4 seconds after someone switched character or loaded in** (`CFG.settle`): the body is read the way the every-5-seconds check reads it (mesh, materials, every texture slot, the head part); fine: nothing is said; broken: it is made again (a part with no materials gets its next material file first), looked at again 2 seconds after the new body is up, made again once more if still broken, and only if it is **still** broken you get a line at the top left ("The other player's body (...) is still broken after 2 rebuilds: ..."). A part that is still loading is waited for (14 s, longer than the body module's own 10 s) before it counts. While it runs the every-5-seconds check leaves the body alone, and a rebuild that check does by itself is not counted as a new start (otherwise a body that stays broken would be rebuilt and announced again and again: found in the first test run). Everything is in the debug file under `model_settle` and `model_pack`.
3. **A motion bank detector for Ethan** (`CFG.mbank`): while you play Ethan it reads his layer-0 bank / clip pair four times a second, asks the game for the clip's **name** once per pair (`getMotionInfo` + `MotionName`, the same call the enemy clips use), turns plain words in the name into one of the clip labels (reload, melee, guard, heal, shoot, aim, crouch, walk, run, idle), and also notes what you were doing while the pair played (your pose flags and speed). The table (pair, name, how often, what you were doing) is kept in `re7mp\motion_banks.json` and is in the debug file under `motion_banks`. The pair goes to the other player as a `mot` event (`<bank>_<clip>_<label>`, when it changes and every 1.5 s). On the receiving side a fresh label (under 3.5 s old) is used **only for what the pose flags cannot say: a reload and a melee swing**; walking, running, crouching, aiming and the rest stay with the flags and the speed (they are measured; a name is a guess; the debug file counts how often a name disagrees with what you were doing). It plays the recorded joint clips on the other player's body, as before: the avatar body has no Motion component, so playing the game's own clips on it is a later step (needs a Motion component on the body and the bank loaded; not attempted without a way to test it). A crash inside the detector is remembered (`mbank` crumb) and switches it off at the next start. It only reads.
4. **The loopback bot's options work.** Cause: the bot's *act* defaulted to "copy me", and in that act the exe ignored the Orbit / Stays put / Follows radios (only Follow had its own code), so the other two did nothing. Now the radios decide the position when the act is Copy or the old timer (Orbit, Stays put, Follows you, and a new **Beside you** radio: it stands next to you and moves as you move); a one-state act (walk, run, ...) still places the bot itself, as its name says. The script's drop-downs ("The loopback bot is / does") now also write a journal line when they change.
5. **Start no longer checks the Lua script.** The script and its data are installed and checked by the **Install / update mod** button and by the run after an update, nothing else; Start only says in the log if the script is missing. (The updater's line says the same.)
6. **The debug file says which REFramework wrote it** (`health.reframework`: tag, commit, commits past the tag, build date, missing calls) and the journal has a line at load. See the README, "Which REFramework".
7. **Version:** `CFG.RELEASE = "0.10"`, `CFG.BUILD = "10.1"`, `kReleaseVersion = "0.10"`.

**Tests:** the Lua smoke suite (5100+ checks) has new blocks for the pack paths (flag file or not, failure falls back), the 4 s / 2 s check (fine body silent, a body fixed by one rebuild silent, a body that stays broken rebuilt twice then announced once), the received `mot` event (reload plays, stale goes back, walk label ignored, junk counted) and the detector (read, name, label, send rate, kept table, crash marker, not Ethan). The C++ suite has the bot radios with the copy act and the pack flag file.

**Not verified (needs your game):** the window code (`gui_win.cpp`) is not compiled where this was written; the pack paths and textures; the motion-name rules (a name like `...Reload...` is assumed); the 4-second timing on a real load.

## What changed in v9.45 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **new re7mp.exe; the script changes only its build label**)

Your 2026-11-14 12:50 note: the installer and the auto updater should be more streamlined: the updater should install the new Lua script when it installs the new version, and the install should be one function that checks the REFramework version, asks whether to install the one that is needed, and then puts the script and what the mod needs in the right places. And Nexus mod 86 (AfroJackX) should be pre-packaged and installed with the exe, with credit. Then, at 13:09: the mod's permissions say the assets can be used without credit.

**What changed**
1. **One install function** (`install_mod`, `src/installer.cpp`): (1) checks the REFramework in the RE7 folder against the version the mod is tested with (`v1.5.9.1`): fine, missing, an older or newer one this program put there, or a `dinput8.dll` this program didn't install (its version can't be read, so it asks); (2) if it is not right it asks "install REFramework v1.5.9.1 now?" and on yes downloads, verifies and installs it (an existing dll is kept as `dinput8.dll.bak`); on no it carries on and says the mod will not run until it is; (3) installs `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json` and says "build 9.44 -> 9.45" or "up to date"; (4) sets up the full character models if the author's zip is at hand (below). The **Start** button, the **Install / update mod** button and the first start after an update all call it.
2. **The updater brings the script.** The release check now fetches the script and defaults that belong to the release: `lua/re7mp.lua` and `data/re7mp/defaults.json` inside the release zip, or release files named `re7mp.lua` and `defaults.json`. The script's `RELEASE` label must equal the release's version, or it is not used and the log says why. The program is swapped first; only then is the script written, so they never disagree. If the release carries no script, the new program installs its own copy the first time it starts.
3. **After an update the new program runs the whole install by itself** (so a REFramework version the new program needs is checked too). On every other start an **existing** install is quietly kept current (script and defaults only; nothing is installed for the first time, nothing is downloaded).
4. **The full character models (Nexus RE7 mod 86, "Third Person Full Model Placeholders RT (2022)" by AfroJackX) ship with re7mp.** I first read only the upload and modification lines of the mod's Permissions page; the full box (re-read 2026-11-14 13:10) says asset use is allowed without permission or credit. Not allowed: uploading the file itself, use in anything sold or earning donation points, and converting it to other games. So the assets (22 meshes, 22 materials, 5 textures, 41 MB, unchanged) are in `modpack\third-person-full-models\natives\...` next to re7mp.exe, with a `CREDITS.txt`, not as the author's archive; the author is credited anyway (log, README, notices). The install finds that folder (or, failing that, the author's zip in your Downloads), puts the meshes and materials under `natives\stm\coop` (so your own first-person body is not replaced; the layout the other developer's installer uses), the textures where they belong, and switches on REFramework's Loose File Loader (`re2_fw_config.txt`). Every file is compared by checksum first, so a second run writes nothing. Without either the log says where to get them and the mod runs as before. The script does not read these files yet, they are for RE7 only (never RE8), and the pack has models, materials and textures only: no animations.
5. **Tests:** 130+ new checks (the real shipped pack is installed into a temp folder and compared byte for byte) in the C++ suite (REFramework verdicts, yes/no/offline, script and data written once and reported, the update package from a zip and from separate files, a script from another release refused, the swap failing, the model pack layout, the loose-file switch). The window code (`gui_win.cpp`) is a thin caller and was **not compiled here**.

**Not changed:** the Lua script and the protocol (only `CFG.BUILD`), your settings, the REFramework row's manual Install / Reinstall buttons.

## What changed in v9.44 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers; no exe change**)

Your 2026-11-14 02:08 note: key items like the fuse and the bolt cutters are taken from both games, so a pickup that is taken and then consumed or used by one player would break the other's game. Then, at 02:12: "if it ships with only sharing consumables and guns, then leave it on". So item sync **stays on, but now shares only consumables and weapons.** **Unverified in the real game:** it is checked against a fake game; the category is read from the real record the game's own dump shows (`app.Item.get_ItemData()` / `_ItemData`, `app.ItemData.Category`, enum `app.Item.ItemCategoryType`), which is how the first real run will confirm it.

**What changed**
1. **Only these are shared:** weapon (1), ammo / shell (2), heal / drug (3), material (7, the chemicals and the like, consumed in crafting) and stack weapon (8, throwables). **Never shared:** key item (4), file (5), map (6), usable key item (9), discardable key item (10), supply box (11), other item (0), and anything whose category can't be read (nothing is shared that can't be classified).
2. **Both sides check.** The player who picks an item up sends it only if it is on the list. The player who receives the message looks at the item lying in their own world (its own record) before removing it, so an older script on the other computer that sends everything can't take a key item out of this game either.
3. **A weapon goes to whoever picks it up first, even in "both players get it" mode:** it is removed on the other screen, so one of you can have the double barrel and the other the second shotgun when they find it. Heals and ammo still stay on both screens in "both" mode.
4. **A new box, "Share only consumables and weapons"** (on; remembered as `item_safe_only`, and shipped on). Unticking it shares everything as before, with a warning line in the panel. The old sharing mechanics (the 3 m rule for gifts, the ground route) are untouched.
5. **Debug file:** `health.item_sync` gains `only_consumables_and_weapons`, `not_shared_key_items_and_the_like`, `category_unreadable`, `weapons_taken_for_the_other_player` and `categories_seen` (item id -> category name), and each pickup ring line says why an item was not sent.

**Not changed:** a settings file from an older build keeps its own choice (sync ticked stays ticked, now with the filter); the shipped `defaults.json` has `item_sync` on and `item_safe_only` on (a new shipped key: 54 settings).

**Tests:** Lua Scenario 26 (a new player: on and filtered; a heal / ammo / weapon / material sent and a key item / usable key item / file / other item / unreadable category not sent; the counts and category names in the debug file; unticking the filter shares everything with a warning and is remembered; 'take' mode removes a heal and leaves a key item and an unreadable item; 'both' mode keeps a heal, takes a weapon and keeps a key item; filter off + 'both' is as it was; an older settings file keeps sync with the filter; the panel text). The older item checks (mechanics) run with the filter off.

**Still open:** that the real game's `get_ItemData` / `Category` read works as the dump shows (the first pickup lines in the log say `category` or "can't be read"), which item ids are which category (the debug file now collects them), and everything that needs a second player.

## What changed in v9.43 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers; no exe change**)

Your request: "a log that detects when Mia spawns, then check which methods the game calls to spawn her, as well as which files it loads. That way you'll know for sure if something is missing" (for the Mia hair, and for finding hair physics on characters and enemies). **Unverified in the real game:** the whole module is checked against a fake game; which classes and methods it finds, and what Mia's real objects hold, is what your first run will tell. It only reads: nothing is changed in the game.

**What it is:** a new panel node "Spawn log: Mia's spawn, the methods and files, hair physics" with a box (ships unticked, remembered as `spawn_log`) and a box for the words to watch (`spawn_focus`, start: `mia;pl2000;pl2100;em2000;em2200;em2300`).

**What it records**
1. **The methods the game calls.** Three seconds after it is ticked, the script looks through the manager classes this game has (`app.EnemyManager`, `app.CharacterManager`, `app.PlayerManager`, `app.EnemySpawner`, `app.EventManager`, `app.GameManager`, `via.Prefab`, `via.ResourceManager`, and `via.Scene` / `via.GameObject` for create / instantiate names only) and hooks the methods whose names say spawn, create, instantiate, generate, appear, summon, regist, request, load, setup or start, at most 140, five a frame. The first two readable arguments of every call (a name, a file path, a prefab's path) go into a ring of the last 600 calls. A method that fires more than 150 times a second is counted, not logged ("too busy to log").
2. **When she spawns.** Once a second the script looks at every object with a Motion component. A character matching the words that was not there a second ago (or was already there when the log started: said so) starts an **episode**: when, chapter, position, what the game called in the 20 s before (grouped by method: how often, first and last second relative to her spawn, the argument), every call or file that **mentions her** in the last 3 minutes, every file the game was asked for in the 90 s before, and the same for the 25 s after. The scene is left alone while the game is loading.
3. **What she is made of.** Her inventory, five objects a frame: every object under her, its components, and the files they hold: mesh and material, the `.motlist` list of her Motion, and every resource / path getter of her components. Physics components (anything with chain, cloth, hair, spring in the type name) are listed on their own with every number and flag they answer, and with the **method list of their type** in the debug file (`physics_types[...].methods`), which is what the next build needs to set a chain up from real names.
4. **What my copy lacks.** The same inventory of my copy of her (the lab body made from her, or her enemy model), and the difference: objects, component kinds (physics first), files and physics components that the real one has and the copy has not. With no copy standing the log says so; the panel's "Compare the last spawn with my copy" reads it again once one stands.
5. **A census of hair physics.** Every 5 s: which character or enemy carries which physics component (`via.motion.Chain`, `via.motion.ChainCollision`, `via.motion.ChainGroup`, `via.dynamics.GpuCloth`, `via.dynamics.Cloth`, `via.motion.SpringBone`, `app.ChainClothSelector`), on which object, kept between sessions; types this game does not have are said so; characters seen with no physics at all are listed.

**Where it goes:** `re7mp/spawnlog.json` (everything, including her full object list), `re7mp/spawn_log.txt` (the same lines as text; needs `fs.write`, else the lines are in the JSON), and the debug file as `health.spawn_log`. **Send me `spawnlog.json` after you have seen her spawn.**

**Safety:** every read of a component and every hook is announced in `lastcall.json` first (stage `spawnlog`). A session that ended inside one never makes that call again (`re7mp/spawnstate.json`; the panel has a button to forget them). Nothing is hooked while the box is unticked.

**How to use it:** tick the box, play to a place where Mia appears (the flashback, the cutscenes, the fight), let 25 s pass, then send `re7mp/spawnlog.json` and the debug file. To compare with the copy, make the body from her first (Body lab: model `Pl2000` or `Em2000`) and press "Compare the last spawn with my copy".

**Tests:** Lua Scenario 25 (hooks found by name and not the getters, a static method's argument, a prefab's path, the busy-method count, her spawn with the chapter, the calls before it with the name she was spawned with, the files asked for before, her objects / mesh / material / motion lists / chain file / numbers read, the copy difference with objects, files, physics and component kinds, the text log, her leaving, the loading gate, the box off, ticking it in the panel with other words, a hook that ended a session, a component read that ended a session, the census with the missing types).

**Still open:** everything the real game will say about which classes exist; the door gate's skip count and the enemy bank 0 / clip 0 reads from 9.41/9.42; everything that needs a second player.

## What changed in v9.42 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers AND rebuild the exe: the save folder is chosen by Steam account, and the two exes tell each other which account they use**)

Your idea from the 9.41 test: use both players' Steam save-profile ids, the way the other developer's mod knows its players, **without anything going through Steam**. **Unverified in the real game:** the folder choice and the account message are checked with fake Steam folders and two exes on one PC; nothing about a re-signed save loading in the real game has changed (it is still untried).

**What was true before**
- The exe wrote saves into `userdata/<id>/418370/remote/win64_save`, re-signed for the receiver (the account block is copied from any file the receiver's own game wrote, no key needed; unchanged), but it *chose* the folder by "newest file wins", and `re7mp.ini` kept that choice for ever. On a PC with two Steam accounts (yours: 759758082 in use, 1055164497 old) the wrong one is easy to end up on.

**What changed**
1. **The folder is the signed-in account's.** RE7 loads saves from the account Steam is signed in as, so that is the folder to watch. The window reads the account from the registry value Steam keeps up to date (`HKCU\Software\Valve\Steam\ActiveProcess\ActiveUser`; a plain read, nothing is sent to Steam and nothing of Steam's is changed). Steam not running: the account marked `MostRecent` in `<steam>\config\loginusers.vdf` (a plain file read). Neither known: the old rule, and the log says it was a guess. Signed in but that account has **no RE7 folder yet**: no folder at all, and the log says to start RE7 once and reach the main menu (watching another account's folder would be worse).
2. **A stale folder is replaced.** On start-up and again when you press Start, a folder in the box that is a `userdata/<id>/...` path of *another* account than the signed-in one is replaced, and the log line says which account it was and which it is now. A path that is not a userdata folder (typed on purpose) is never touched; neither is the right one.
3. **The two exes tell each other their account (new exe-to-exe message kind 65001, `acct|1|<Steam32 id>`).** Sent once the other player is reachable, again after a break and every minute. The receiving exe consumes it (it never reaches the script; an older exe hands it to its script, which has no handler and ignores it, so there is no wire change and no protocol bump). The id is the folder's name, so a folder that is not a `userdata\<id>` path says nothing. It is a Steam account number, which is not secret, but it is sent: mention it if that matters to anyone you play with.
4. **Both accounts are named everywhere:** the exe's log ("Told the other player's re7mp.exe that this PC uses Steam account X", "The other player's PC uses Steam account Y"), the manifest (`account`, `partner_account`, and `partner_account` on each co-op save: the sender's for a received copy, the receiver's for one you sent), the panel (*Co-op saves*: "Steam accounts: your saves are in account X's folder; the other player's account is Y."), the load-screen scan and the debug file (`coop_saves.account`, `.partner_account`, `.accounts`, and per entry). The same account on both sides is called out (the loopback bot answers with this PC's account, so you can see it solo).

**What it does not do**
- It cannot make a save load for an account that has *never* run RE7: the account block is encrypted with the game's key and the tool has no key (AutostrongSharp's source, MIT, does not contain RE7's; it loads it from a profile file). The receiver still needs one file the game wrote on their account, which any player who got to the main menu once has (`data00-1.bin`).
- The slot number inside the encrypted body is unchanged and a re-signed save has still never been loaded in the real game.

**Tests:** C++ `test_save_accounts` (the message text and its refusals, the account out of a path, `loginusers.vdf`, the folder choice with two accounts including the one that was wrong before, the replacement rules, the manifest fields and a 9.41 manifest without them, two exes swapping accounts with nothing reaching either script, a folder without an account, the loopback bot) and Lua Scenario 24 (the panel line in all four states, the debug file, nonsense values). Full runs: Lua 4492 checks in all (673 new, most of them one "no UI error" check per panel line), none failing; the C++ suite passes in full.

**Still open:** the door gate's high skip count in your 9.41 debug file (3,937 of 5,777 polls), why six of seven enemy types read bank 0 / clip 0, and everything that needs a second player in the real game.

## What changed in v9.41 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers; no exe change**)

The second piece brought over from the other developer's co-op mod ("Molded Together", MIT, see `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`), rewritten into this script's own messages, notes and tests: **enemy animation, with the bank fetch.** **Unverified in the real game:** everything below ran against the fake game only, and the new box ships **off**.

**What was already true**
- The host has sent every enemy's animation (bank, clip number, frame) along with its position since 9.x, and the joiner never used it. Your 9.38 debug file says the F3 animation probe was never used (`enemy_motions`: "Not used yet", 0 runs), so this build had no animation ids of its own for enemies. The only ones known come from their notes (see `re7-findings.md`).

**What changed**
1. **A table of every bank and clip the game shows on an enemy (always on, costs next to nothing).** The host records each (bank, clip) it reads on each enemy, with how often and the largest frame seen, and finds the `.motlist` path of each bank (the Motion's dynamic banks first, then its active banks, then the probe's index route; three tries a bank, never faster than twice a second). It is in the debug file as `health.enemy_clips` and kept between sessions in `re7mp/enemy_clips.json`. **This is how the enemies' animation ids get known from the game itself:** play, then send that file.
2. **The joiner can play the host's clip on its own enemy (new box, off, remembered as `enemy_clips`).** Panel > Enemies > *Enemy sync* mode 3 > *Also switch off their AI* > **EXPERIMENTAL: play the host's enemy animations on my enemies**. Their calls: the enemy's own `MotionFsm` is switched off (and put back to what it was), and layer 0's six-parameter `changeMotion(bank, clip, frame, 8.0, 2, 0)` plays the clip (floats for the numbers). The frame is put right only when it has drifted by more than 8 frames, at most once a second.
3. **The bank fetch (new message kind 14).** A clip whose bank my copy has not loaded would leave it in a T-pose (their Mia, in every grab and when Ethan blocked her chainsaw). `getMotionInfo(bank, clip, info)` says whether the copy has it; when it does not, the copy keeps its last move and asks the host: `req|<enemy>|<bank>|<ch>` (at most 4 times a bank, 2 s apart). The host answers `bank|<enemy>|<bank>|<overwrite>|<path>|<ch>` (or `none|...|<why>|<ch>`) whether or not its own box is ticked, and the copy adds the bank (a `DynamicMotionBank` with the path's resource, `set_OverwriteBankID`, `set_BankID`, appended, then `setupMotionBank`, without which an added bank stayed inactive) and plays the clip. Paths are checked before use (`.motlist`, letters, digits, `_ . / -`, no `..`, 130 characters at most); at most 12 banks are added a session; a bank that fails is not tried again.
4. **It lets go:** box off, mode or AI box changed, the host quiet for 3 s, its enemy dead or parked, the game loading (the 9.40 gate), or an enemy being saved (their finding: a saved enemy keeps its think state, so `app.EnemySave.prepareToSave` puts the animation controller back and pauses the clips for 4 s). Three errors on one enemy switch its clips off for the session. A call that cannot tell whether the copy has a clip lets it play, counted as "unsure" (their rule).
5. **The panel** shows the box, why it is idle (`off`, `needs Enemy sync mode 3`, `needs the box 'Also switch off their AI'`, `this game is the host`, `no enemy data from the host`, `the game is loading`, `the game is saving`, or `playing`), the counts and the last message.

**Debug file:** `health.enemy_clips` (state, counters, what is playing, the table of clips with `copy_has_it`, the table of bank paths, the last 30 messages with their results, the last error).

**Tests:** 276 new Lua checks, 3819 in all, none failing (a fake Em2000 whose Motion answers `getMotionInfo`, the dynamic and active banks, `setupMotionBank` and the six-parameter `changeMotion`; its `MotionFsm` is a component of its own): the host's table and file, answers and refusals, nonsense messages, a request while loading or from another chapter, playing and the drift rule, a missing bank asked for 4 times and then added, refused paths, a resource that cannot be made, every way of letting go, three errors, a `getMotionInfo` that fails.

**Still open:** everything about it in the real game (whether `changeMotion` on a scene enemy with the AI box ticked looks right, whether the animation controller stays off, the real paths of the banks); what the model copies of enemies (the "Enemy models" boxes, which have no Motion component) need to animate: their copy of Mia got a motion bank asset, a joint map and a hand-made layer (`set_Resource(nil)` first) before it walked, which is the next piece; the grab and execution moves.

## What changed in v9.40 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers; no exe change**)

The first feature brought over from the other developer's co-op mod ("Molded Together", MIT, see `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`), one piece per build, rewritten into this script's own messages, notes and tests: **doors**. **Unverified in the real game:** everything below ran against the mock game only. Their notes say their door sync was tested live (two two-player sessions, up to the Chapter 3 yard); this script's version of it has not been.

**What changed**
1. **Doors are shared (new message kind 13).** When one player unlocks, opens, closes or pushes a door, the same happens to that door in the other game. Opening uses the six-argument `requestAutoOpen` with the door's own speed (their notes: the one-argument call did nothing on 87 of 106 remote opens of the latched main-house doors), closing uses `requestAutoClose`, a push is sent as its angle (at most 5 a second, only when it moved 3 degrees) and the other game glides the door to it with `requestPush`. A door that stays locked after the unlock is unlocked again (up to 3 times, a second apart). A door that did not start opening after 0.7 s is snapped open.
2. **A door that swings shut by itself is not sent as a close** (their notes: sending it slammed the door on the other player as they walked through). A close is sent when my player used the door (a hook on `DoorPush.setInteract`), or when it is a door I opened. The self-close belongs to the game of whoever opened the door: the other copy waits for its close; a door I opened does not shut while the other player stands in the doorway; their close waits while I stand in the doorway, and happens when I step out.
3. **The loading gate (`CFG.busy`).** While the game loads a scene, jumps chapter or swaps the player, nothing is called on a door (their 2026-11-10 session: about 250 exceptions a second inside the game, then a hang or a crash). The gate is shut during a load and for a second after, gives up after 3 minutes, and the doors are read again from scratch afterwards (what changed meanwhile is not sent as news). An unlock that arrives during a load, or for a door that is not loaded yet, is kept and applied when the door is there. Other messages for a door that is not there are dropped and counted.
4. **Doors cost nothing in solo play:** with nobody connected no door is scanned. Connected, the scene is scanned 4 times a second for `app.DoorPush`; the debug file says what that costs (`health.doors.poll_avg_ms`, `poll_worst_ms`).
5. **A message from another chapter is ignored** (each door message carries the chapter number).
6. **The panel has a *Doors (shared with the other player)* section:** a box to switch it off (remembered as `door_sync`, on by default), the counts, the last message and the last six sent or received with what became of each. **The debug file has `health.doors`:** every counter (sent, got, applied, not loaded here, other chapter, dropped while loading, retries, snaps, errors), the last 40 messages with their result, the doors known with their state, and `loading_gate` (how often it shut, for how long, why).

**Messages (kind 13, the pieces joined with `|`; the last piece is the chapter):** `open|<id>|<ch>`, `close|<id>|<ch>`, `unlock|<id>|<ch>`, `angle|<id>|<degrees>|<side>|<ch>`. The id is the door's `SaveGUID`, the same in both games.

**Tests:** 288 new Lua checks, 3543 in all, none failing (a fake `app.DoorPush` with the states, the six-argument open, a latched door, a door that needs two unlocks, an array without `get_elements`, a scan that fails, the loading flags, the chapter, the switch, solo play, nonsense messages). The exe and the C++ tests are unchanged. The first full run found that the door scan ran in solo play (three older "no scan with nobody connected" checks failed); fixed before the build.

**Still open:** everything about it in the real game (the door hook's argument slots, the cost of the scan, the glide of a pushed door over this script's message channel instead of their 30 Hz relay); the van door at the start is not a `DoorPush` and is not covered; drawers, switches and one-off interactions (the van, fridge, pot) are the next pieces, then the animated bodies for scripted interactions.

## What changed in v9.39 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers; no exe change**)

It answers your 2026-11-12 09:56 and 09:57 notes, your 9.38 files (debug file, framework log, `model_status.json`) and theo_'s debug file. **Unverified in the real game:** everything below ran against the mock game only (Lua checks and the C++ suite; the exe is unchanged, so an exe built from 9.38 is fine).

**What your 9.38 files showed**
- **The frozen Mia is very likely FORCE itself.** The framework log has an `Internal game exception ... app.GameManager.requestEvent ... System.ArgumentNullException` at 09:32:59, 09:36:45 and 09:41:14, one for each of the three times FORCE called `requestEvent` (`game_said_no: 3`). **3 of 3 FORCE calls threw inside the game.** (The 9.37 session's two "the game answered false" look the same, but that log was overwritten and cannot be checked.) Until 9.39 the script read the exception's `false` as "the game said no". An exception in the middle of `requestEvent` leaves the cutscene half started (the actors are placed, the timeline never runs), which is the Mia standing frozen in the basement in your 08:50 screenshot. The recorder, the move and the put-back were not the cause. (Which argument is null is not known: the game passes a real end callback itself, this script passes none; the log does not say.)
- **Clancy's black hands.** `pl3010` / `pl3020` got materials with the 4th candidate, **Ethan's** hand file (`pl0000/pl0000/pl0010.mdf2`, `pl0020.mdf2`). A file that exists but is the wrong one has materials and textures too, so the model check was content, called it "works" and kept it in `model_status.json`. The right file for Clancy's hands is still unknown: the scene's hands read 2 materials, Ethan's file has 1, and the collector has never been able to read a material file path from a scene object (`mdf` is empty for every model).
- **Mia's textures.** Neither file shows a fault: theo_'s game made your Mia (`Pl2000`: `pl2001` body, `pl2010`, `pl2020`, `pl2002`, the scene's `Em2000Head` with 2 of its 7 layers switched, and the chain hair from file) between 297 s and 427 s with no finding from the model check, and the material file of every Mia part was its own (nothing from 9.38's candidate list touched her). What did go wrong in that file: the chain hair `em2070` was "not loaded after 10 s" and taken away, so she had no hair. The check only counts materials and texture slots; whether the texture in a slot is the right one is not something it can see. Your next file will say more (below).
- Not a hang: the framework log has no gap over 2 s in the session (the longest, 5.3 s, is the game restarting at 09:50). The 09:39 move for `c01e01_00` (24 m) did not hold ("the game moved you again", 21 m from the place), so no cutscene was played and nothing was put back.
- The `re7mp_state_in.json` parse errors are still logged every 50 ms (the exe writes the file in place; empty when read). Not changed.

**What changed**
1. **FORCE is its own box now and switches itself off at the first "no".** re7mp.exe's *Sync story cutscenes* box turns on story sync and both moves only; FORCE has to be ticked in the panel (label says it is broken so far). The first answer of *no* turns it off again and says why in the journal, the panel and `health.story.force.switched_itself_off_because`. A move for a refused cutscene is still taken back. The hook now counts the game's own end callbacks apart from this script's calls (`game_passes_an_end_callback.type_when_set` names the delegate's type when the game passes one) so the next file says what a working call has to pass.
2. **Clancy's hands: no more other-character material files by themselves.** The script's own candidates are: the mesh's own name, the folder's name, `<parent>/<name>/<name>.mdf2`, then the **other files of the same model** (Clancy's body file has 3 materials, one may be the hands'; the other hand's; the lower body's). Ethan's and Mia's files are only reached with a button. **Other player's body** panel: every part made from a file is listed with the material file in use; **Next material file** (that part, the body is made again), **Keep this material file** (kept in `model_status.json` as `material_files_picked`, used first at every start), **Forget the kept material files**. A 9.38 `model_status.json` that kept Ethan's hand file is cleaned at the start. Reading `health.loader.material_tries` and `health.model_check.last_read_of_each_body` after you have cycled through tells which file is Clancy's.
3. **A part still not loaded after 5 s is made again once, by itself, also on the other player's real body** (Mia's chain hair; the enemy models have had this since 9.37). Mia's own-head experiment keeps its 10 s and its fallback. `health.body_lab.parts_made_again`.
4. **Crouch: the body stands 8 cm higher** at a full crouch (`CFG.body.crouch_extra`, a slider *Crouch: stand the body this much higher* in the body lab, 0 to 0.4 m, saved as `crouch_extra`). It is the lift you see on the other player's body, so the player who sees the crouch sinking into the floor is the one who sets it.
5. **For the next Mia / texture report:** `health.model_check.last_read_of_each_body` keeps the last reading of every body the other player was shown as (the row used to vanish with the body), and each reading now carries the material names (`materials=true/3 [name,name,name]`).

**Tests:** the Lua checks (new: the exe's box leaves FORCE off; a "no" switches it off and a second call is not made until it is ticked again; the script never asks for Ethan's or Mia's hand file by itself and does try the model's own files; the three buttons; a stale Ethan file in an old `model_status.json` is dropped; the pick is used at the next start; a slow part is made again once and joins) and the C++ suite (nothing in it changed).

**Still open:** which material file is Clancy's hands'; what makes `requestEvent` throw (FORCE stays a manual experiment); whether the move for a cutscene should exist at all (the game moves you back); the Mia texture report; Mia's hair physics; the exe's non-atomic writes of `re7mp_in.json` / `re7mp_state_in.json`; the crash cause; the 18 uncollected models; enemy sync.

## What changed in v9.38 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers AND rebuild the exe: the loopback bot copies you and can show any state you pick**)

It answers your 2026-11-12 08:50 and 08:51 notes and the files you gave access to (the framework log included). **Unverified in the real game:** everything below ran against the mock game only (3236 Lua checks, the C++ suite with a new engine-level test of the bot's states; the window code in `gui_win.cpp` is unchanged and still compiles only on your side).

**What your 9.37 files showed**
- **Mia froze.** The framework log has it at 08:39:25: theo_ started `c01e00_01` at (15.8, -6.6, 12.5); you stood at (15.8, -0.0, 11.3), so the script "moved you 7 m to theo_'s place" (6.6 m down, into the basement); 1.5 s later you were still there; FORCE then called `requestEvent` and the game answered **false** ("the game did not start c01e00_01 here"). The same at 08:43 for `c01e00_00`. So in your game the cutscene never played, and you were standing in its room with Mia waiting for it. **The cutscene recorder cannot have caused it:** it was off the whole session (`cutscenes.on` false, 0 cutscenes seen).
- **Clancy's hands** (the broken, untextured pair): `pl3010` and `pl3020` read `materials=false/0` for the whole 1177 s of the session and were found 120 times. The material file the script guesses from the mesh's name does not exist for them (Mia's hands work with the same guess). Andre's model (`em6200`, head `em6250`) is not in the check rows: if his hands are wrong too, the next file will say.
- Items dropping and being picked up by each other: noted as confirmed.

**What changed**
1. **The loopback bot copies you, and a drop-down picks what it does.** Panel, *Who am I? > The loopback bot does* (shown when the other player is the bot). Default **Copies me**: it stands 1.6 m beside you, moves exactly as you move, and shows your pose and gun bits (crouch, block, heal, gun out, aim, shoot) and your look direction. The others show one state each so it can be looked at for as long as wanted: *The old timer, Idle, Walk, Run, Crouch, Crouch walk, Block, Heal, Gun out, Gun out walking, Gun out running, Aim, Shoot (a shot every 1.5 s), Down*. Walk, run and the walking ones circle a fixed spot at 1.4 / 4.5 / 0.9 m/s (the script calls under 2.4 a walk); the others stand. It is sent as kind 11 `state:<key>` and repeated every 10 s; *Down* sends `down`, and leaving it sends `up`. **Needs the new exe** (an older exe ignores the command and the bot keeps its timer). Debug file: `who.loopback_bot_does`.
2. **The cutscene recorder is unlinked from cutscenes.** *Cutscene probe* has a new box **Linked to cutscenes** (off at every start): off, a cutscene start is only counted (`cutscenes.cutscenes_not_recorded_because_unlinked`) and nothing is recorded; a new button **Record now** records by hand (the same files; a recording by hand is not stopped by you standing still; F10 or the time ends it). Tick the box to get the old behaviour back.
3. **A move the game refuses is taken back.** When story sync moved you for a cutscene and FORCE's `requestEvent` then fails or answers false, you are put back where you were (within 30 s of the move). Box under FORCE, on at the start, remembered (`story_back`; `story.force.put_back_when_refused`). To find out whether the move or FORCE is the problem, untick one of the boxes in the panel.
4. **Hands with no materials get another material file.** A part made from its file that has no materials after 10 s tries the next candidate: its own name, then the folder's name (`pl3000/pl3000/pl3000.mdf2`), then `<parent>/<name>/<name>.mdf2`, then the same name under Ethan's and Mia's folders. The body or part is made again, the file that gives materials is kept in `model_status.json` (`material_files`) and used at the next start. `health.loader.material_files_that_worked` / `material_tries`, `health.model_check.other_material_file_tries`. This is a guess at the file name: if none works the debug file lists what was tried.
5. **Text:** the item hint and the panel line now say *click an item to select it, then press F7*; the status lines say "Selected (clicked)".

**Tests:** 3236 Lua checks (new: the hands' material file tried, kept and read back at the next start; the unlinked probe and *Record now*; the bot drop-down; the put-back after a refusal, with the box on and off) and the C++ suite (new: every bot state, copy, walk and run speeds measured on the running engine).

**Still open:** whether the put-back is enough for the frozen Mia, the crash cause, Clancy's lattice textures, Mia's hair physics, the exe's non-atomic writes of `re7mp_in.json` / `re7mp_state_in.json`, the 20 s `model probe` frame, the 18 uncollected models, enemy sync (not tested yet).

## What changed in v9.37 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers AND rebuild the exe: new characters in "Play as" and a bot that follows you**)

It answers your 2026-11-12 07:42 notes and the 9.36 debug file you gave access to. **Unverified in the real game:** everything below ran against the mock game only (3212 Lua checks, the C++ suite; the window code in `gui_win.cpp` could not be compiled here and only had a careful read).

**What your 9.36 debug file showed (enemy models)**
- The check works: 457 looks, 15 findings, and the other player's Clancy body was found with `pl3010: MaterialReady false` and made again. The "no hair / no head the first time, fine after a rebuild" you saw is the **part**: in the log Marguerite's hair (`em3150`) was made at 473.7 s and had not joined when you pressed "Make it again" 5.4 s later; the second time it joined in the same frame (the game had its file cached by then). The check did not get to act because it needs 8 s plus two findings 5 s apart, and you moved on after 5 to 9 s.
- Em4000 (Molded) and Em5400 (Insects) showed "MaterialReady is false" in the model table. That was the first look at a model that was still loading (Em5400 was replaced after 9 s), not a fault: the table kept the first look for ever.
- 18 models are "FAILED: no files are known" (Em2200, Em2800, Em3600-3900, Em4100, Em4200, Em5300, Em6000, Em7300, Em8100, Em8500, Em8900, Em9600-9900): they have never been collected, so there is no mesh path to load. They are listed in `health.model_table.still_to_collect`; each needs a scan in the scene where it appears. That is how "some models don't have bodies" is written down.
- Your frame times: the script's own work averaged 1.7 ms a frame over 229,303 frames; the slowest frames were 737 ms at 28.6 s (`saves+settings`, the first save at startup), 331 ms at 75 s (making your own body) and, every 20 s, a 50 ms `model probe` frame. The debug file write cost 13 ms every 15 s. Nothing in the script explains a constant hitch.

**Enemy models (what changed)**
1. **A part that has not loaded after 5 s is made again, once, by itself** (same resource, so what the game already streamed is kept). It is what "Make it again" did for you.
2. **A model made in the last 30 s is checked every 2 s** (was 5 / 10 s) and the grace is 4 s (was 8). Two findings in a row (so about 6 to 8 s) fix it.
3. **Fix 1 is per part now:** when only parts are wrong (missing, MaterialReady false, untextured) just those parts are made again and the body stays; fix 2 (and any body problem) makes the whole model again. Still 2 fixes per enemy and scene.
4. **The model table only records a problem after the grace** (a model still loading shows nothing).

**Playable characters**
5. **Pete Walken (em9200) and Andre Stickland (em6200)** are characters now: in the exe's "Play as" box, in the panel's "Show me to the other player as" and "The loopback bot is" boxes, in the other player's character message (`pete`, `andre`), and as the other player's real body (made from their files, with their head part). An older script on the other computer does not know the two words and shows you as Ethan. They are not characters the game lets anyone play, so "Automatic" never picks them.

**Loopback bot**
6. **"Follows you"**, a third choice next to "Orbits you" and "Stays put" (also `re7mp_cli --bot-follow`): the bot walks after you (a walk when close, up to 5 m/s when far), stops 2 m away, is put next to you when you are more than 40 m away (a new area), and "Move bot to me" puts it next to you. It still copies your rotation. The pretend enemy circles the bot, so with the bot following, the enemy stays around you too: good for watching animations.

**Enemy animation probe (F3 / button 12)**
7. **Yes: stand near the enemies and press F3.** It now watches 45 s (was 30) and every animation it sees carries how fast the object moved while it played, how far from you it was, and a guess (standing / walk / run / "close and not moving: an attack, a grab or a reaction?"). One press where you first stand still, then walk away, then let it come at you, is enough to label idle, walk and attack. Send `motlists.json` and the debug file.

**The loopback bot as a single-player helper** is planned, not built (see `re7-findings.md`, 9.37).

**Shipped defaults** (`defaults.json` rebuilt: 52 settings, 16 clips, build 9.37). No new saved setting.

## What changed in v9.36 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers; no exe change**)

It answers your 2026-11-12 06:46 notes and the data folder you gave access to. **Unverified in the real game:** everything below ran against the mock game only (3204 Lua checks, the C++ suite).

**The hitching and the crash**
1. **The debug file is no longer written for every item-trail line.** Your 9.35 debug file is about 380 KB (657 KB on disk) and was built and written whole on the render thread (a) every 2 s whenever any journal line had been added (the loopback bot's pose loop, the item journal and the weapon lines add one every second or two) and (b) at once for each of the 221 item-trail lines of that session. That is the cause of the hitching I found by reading the code; nothing timed it. Now the file is written at most every **15 s while you play** and every 2 s while the REFramework menu or a game menu is open, and the gap grows with what the last write cost (100 times as long: a 100 ms write waits 10 s). **Save the debug file now** (Send me this) writes it at once. Every line is still in REFramework's own log the moment it is said and the item trail still has its own small file, so a crash loses nothing that matters.
2. **A frame-time recorder** (`health.perf` in the debug file): per part of the frame loop the total, the average and the slowest time, how many frames took over 4, 8, 16, 33 and 66 ms, the 12 slowest frames with the part that was slowest in each, and what writing the debug file itself costs (building it and saving it). If it still hitches, the next file says where.
3. **The crash itself** (c0000005 in a game worker thread, nothing of the script in progress, a different address from the earlier ones): same family as before; loading models from files is still the unproven lead. Not fixed, only the hitching.

**Enemy models**
4. **Enemies no longer stand there headless.** A model made from files (Jack from the loopback bot) got no parts at all since 9.32. Now the box stays until the game has loaded the body's mesh, then its head and parts are made, and each joins only when its own mesh has loaded **and** its skeleton shares at least 4 joint names with the body (otherwise it is taken away and the file says why). A body that has not loaded after 10 s is dropped (the box stays, tried again later); a part that has not loaded after 10 s is taken away and the body goes on without it.
5. **The every-5-seconds model check** (box under Enemy models, on): every 5 s (every 10 s while no enemy is near on either screen; the loopback bot's enemies do not count) it looks at the other player's body and every enemy model, one per frame: MeshReady, MaterialReady, the materials, **every texture slot** (an empty slot is the untextured look; Clancy's hands) and the parts. A finding must be seen twice in a row and after 8 s. Fix 1: add the missing parts; fix 2: make the model again; then it is left alone (2 fixes per enemy and scene) and the debug file says what is wrong (`health.model_check`). The other player's body is made again once. A call into the game is announced in the crash file first (stage `verify`); a session that ended inside a check starts with it off.
6. **Loopback enemy picker** (Send me this, **13.**): choose any of the 36 models (combo, previous / next, "Make it again"); the bot's circling enemy is drawn as that model (a Lua-only remap: the exe still sends it as Em3000). The list says, for each model, how many of its meshes are in `models.json`, whether its files are known, what happened when it was made (made / failed and why / parts) and what the check found, and **NOT COLLECTED yet** for the ones you still need to collect. It is in the debug file as `health.model_table` and kept between sessions in `re7mp/model_status.json`.
7. **Mia's body**: a mesh that has not loaded in time marks her by-file body bad for 25 s now, not for the whole session (a slow load is not held against her).

**Other**
8. **Inventory note**: while an inventory-like menu is open with the other player there and the ground route on, a two-line note says discarding drops the item on the ground for the other player (and F7 hands one over). It follows the existing give-hint box.
9. **Mia's hair physics** is still the unticked experiment: the box was never ticked in your files, so there is nothing to read. Tick it with a corrupted Mia in the scene and press *Read her hair components*.

**Shipped defaults** (`defaults.json` rebuilt: 52 settings, 16 clips, build 9.36). The loader, picker and check add no saved setting (the give-hint box carries the inventory note).

## What changed in v9.35 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers and rebuild the exe: the loopback bot's circling enemy is Jack now, and the exe carries the script**)

It answers your 2026-11-11 18:46 message (three screenshots) and your 18:50 follow-up ("get rid of the tape watcher feature altogether"). **Unverified in the real game:** everything below ran against the mock game only (3073 Lua checks, the C++ suite).

**Removed**
1. **The tape watcher is gone.** It polled the player object five times a second, collected models during a tape and told the other player when a tape started. Gone with it: the panel section "VHS tapes", the `tape` section of the debug file, the `tape` event (one from an older script is ignored) and the settings keys `tape_on` / `tape_hide` (an old settings file with them loads fine; they are never shipped). What stays: the "which character am I playing" module (the player object's name), which story sync still asks whether I am in somebody else's scene. One side effect: the other player's stand-in is no longer held back while they are in a tape.

**Shipped defaults** (`data/re7mp/defaults.json` rebuilt: 52 settings, 16 clips; your own settings still win key by key, so press **Reset to defaults** once to take them)
2. **The item journal and its object scan ship OFF** (`item_journal`, `item_scan`): their hooks on three item calls could add lag for an average player. With the journal off the hooks are not even installed; the box in the panel still turns it on for a tester. (The item-data collector for the debug file is a separate thing and is still on.)
3. **Loading models from their files ships ON** (`load_by_path`), for Mia's playable body, Clancy and the enemy models. **Ethan is never made from files** (the script refuses; see 6).
4. **Giving the LAST one of a kind ships ON** (`give_last`). The crafting-way removal (`reduceItem`, third argument false) worked in the real game; the way that crashed it 4 times is gone. An inventory the script cannot read is still refused.
5. **"The right Mia settings"** from your screenshots: the layers of the corrupted Mia's head by name (`em2050_Skin_Mia` on, `em2050_Skin_Evil` and `em2050_Skin_Blend` off, `Eye_Shadow`, `em2050_Eye_Blend`, `em2050_Hair` and `eye_Tear` on), her own `pl2050` head off (it drew black), the two side-lock hair meshes (`Em2000LeftHair`, `Em2000RightHair`, flat white shards without their cloth) hidden, and the new head parts on (see 8). Hair physics ships unticked (see 9). `cutscene_probe`, `face_me`, `occlude` and `menu_hide` stay off as before.

**Ethan**
6. **He is copied from the scene, never made from his files.** The 9.34 test: a body made from his files "spawned with no textures", a copy of the scene's own Ethan "seems to consistently work" (it shares the textures the game has already loaded). When he is not in the scene (Mia's flashback, a tape, the opening) nothing is made and the scene is looked at again after 1, 2, 3 ... 10 s (it was 3, 6, 12, 24, 30 s); the panel says why he is not drawn.
7. **His copy is built while the new-game timer is still counting down** (and while "Show other player" is off), so it is already there when the timer ends. I read "drawn when the timer ... shows up" as "built then"; a copy of the scene is made at once. A body that has to be made from FILES (Mia's, Clancy's) waits until the other player is drawn, because a parked body may not get its textures (Clancy's hands and shirt were see-through).

**Mia**
8. **Her playable body (Pl2000) gets a head and hair.** The playable characters have no head that is drawn (`pl2050` is the game's shadow head), so a Pl2000 body stood there headless. The corrupted Mia's head (`Em2000Head`, em2050, with the layers above) and chain hair (`Em2000ChainHair`, em2070) are put on it as parts: the scene's own objects when the scene has them (they share the loaded textures), else made from their files. **Skeleton check:** a part follows the body only through the joint names they share (7 in the 9.34 data: Chest, Neck, Head and the shoulders), so a part that shares fewer than 4 is destroyed again and the body goes on without it; the debug file (`body_lab.mia_head_parts`) lists the counts per part and a log of what happened. Box in the panel: "Mia: put the corrupted Mia's head and chain hair on her playable body" (ships ticked). New button "Use Mia (Pl2000)".
9. **Hair physics (EXPERIMENTAL, ships unticked).** Her chain hair has a native `via.motion.Chain`. When the scene has a corrupted Mia, one is created on the copy and every readable property of the original's (numbers, booleans, text, resource references) is copied onto it; what the original holds is written to the debug file (`health.hair.probes`) whether or not the copy works, so the next build can use the real names. Only reads and one `createComponent` plus `set_` calls, each announced in `lastcall.json` first; a session that ended inside one switches it off at the next start and skips that call from then on (`hairstate.json`). **It may not work, it needs a corrupted Mia in the scene, and the side locks (GpuCloth) are not attempted.** The button "Read her hair components" reads them without changing anything.

**Loopback bot**
10. **The pretend enemy that circles you is Jack (`Em3000`)** instead of a made-up id, so with *Enemy models* on (and the loader) you can watch his model load in. The standing red box is still `Em9998`. **Animation is not done:** Jack stands in his rest pose; playing enemy animations needs the `.motlist` work first.

**Checks:** **3073** Lua checks (3018 at 9.34: the tape tests went, 75 new ones for Mia's head and hair, Ethan, the defaults and the reset), the C++ suite (the bot's circling enemy is `Em3000`), the syntax of the exe's window code (it cannot be built where I work).

**Not done / not known:** the enemy animations; hair physics for the side locks; Clancy's playable body has no head either (not addressed); whether the Mia layers look right on the real body; whether a head made from files gets its textures.

## What changed in v9.34 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers; the exe's code is the same as 9.33, but it carries the script, so rebuild it or copy `lua/re7mp.lua` into the game's `reframework\autorun` folder by hand**)

It answers your 2026-11-11 18:10 message ("change button 12 for f3"). **Unverified in the real game:** the key press and the line on screen only ran against the mock game.

1. **F3 does what button 12 ("Enemy animations (.motlist)") did.** Stand near enemies with the menu shut and press F3: it lists the banks and starts the 30 s watch; press F3 again and it stops and saves. Holding the key does not repeat it. The button stays in *Send me this* (labelled `[F3]` now) and does the same.
2. **A line at the top left shows it** ("ENEMY ANIMATIONS 12 / 30 s (F3 stops and saves) ..." with what each object plays), and for 8 s after each press or end it says what happened (done / nothing to watch / failed), because the panel is shut while you play.
3. **The debug file says whether the key reached the script:** `health.enemy_motions.key_presses` (it stays 0 if the game or another overlay ate F3). The stop reason names what pressed it: `stopped by F3` or `stopped by the button`.

Nothing else changed: F3 was not used by the script, the exe or (as far as I know) the game, and the shipped defaults are the same as 9.33 apart from their build label.

## What changed in v9.33 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers and rebuild the exe: the window has a new box and the loopback bot learned two things**)

It answers your 2026-11-11 16:12 message (the screenshot of Mia's black head, "leave the drop route as the default", "force the cutscene to the other person", "enemy animations", "get the defaults right for v0.9"), your 16:14 report (the game crashed while walking around; friendly fire does nothing on the loopback bot), the loopback request (Clancy's model on the bot) and the reset button. **Everything below is unverified in the real game:** the script ran against the mock game (3007 checks), the exe's core against its C++ suite, and the new window code only got a syntax check (the Windows window cannot be built on the machine I work on).

**Items**
1. **The ground ("drop") route is the default, remembered, and the only way to give a kind you do not hold.** The 3 m giving rule stays. A gift of a kind the other player already has still just makes their stack bigger (that worked before).
2. **The old way of making an item is gone** (`createItemInstance` + `addItem`, and the item lab built around it). It crashed the game every time it produced an object. Nothing is created for an inventory any more; the debug file no longer reports an `addItem` answer.

**Cutscenes (story sync)**
3. **One box in re7mp.exe, under "Share saves": "Sync story cutscenes (experimental): a cutscene plays for both of you".** It starts unticked (experimental) and is remembered by the exe. Ticked, the script turns on story sync, FORCE (the cutscene the other player starts is played here too) **and both moves** (the joiner to the host and the host to the joiner) at once, on whichever side has the exe ticked; **whoever hits a cutscene first, host or joiner, the other one is brought to the place and gets the cutscene.** Both players need it ticked. Unticked, all four are off. The script's own boxes for them are still in the panel (the exe's box sets them every couple of seconds; it travels through `re7mp_story.json`, `{story_sync, seq}`, written when you press Start and when you tick it). The script never saves these four switches.
4. **Watch only.** The panel lists the cutscenes seen so far, each with a *watch only* box (saved by cutscene id). When it is ticked on either player's side, the other player is still brought to the place but the cutscene is **not** played for them, so they can stand by and watch the first player go through it. The sender adds `|w` to the message (an older script ignores it).
5. **The top-left callouts ("I started the cutscene ...") are a debug option now, off.** Both players would otherwise get a prompt for every cutscene. The notes still go to the debug file and the panel's log.
6. **The slow walk to the cutscene point that you saw is, as far as I can tell, the game's own start of the event** (FORCE calls `requestEvent` with the other player's transform). I did not change it; the two moves above are the script's own jump to the other player's place before that.

**Mia's head**
7. **Her own head (`pl2050`) drew black** (your screenshot; it is her "shadow head", as Ethan's `pl0050` is). That box now starts **unticked** and the scene's head is used again.
