That head is one mesh with several layers (`em2050_Skin_Evil`, `_Skin_Blend`, `_Skin_Mia`, `Eye_Blend`, `Eye_Shadow`, `Hair`, `eye_Tear`): the script now switches them **by name** with `setMaterialsEnable` (the corrupted skin and the blend off, her own skin on). A box for the whole thing, a tick box per layer, and the layers' states before and after are in the debug file (`health.body`). A crash while it is switching is remembered (crumb stage `face`): the next start says so and has the layer switching off (the box can be ticked again). Unverified: I do not know that the game's layer names do what their names suggest.

**Loopback bot (testing)**
8. **Friendly fire works on it** (it did nothing before: the bot never took a hit). The bot has its own health now: each hit (an `e|ff|<damage>` event, 1 to 600) takes health off, never below 1, and the new health goes back at once; at 1 it goes down; a heal next to it gets it up. (The exe change.)
9. **It can say which character it is** (the box "The loopback bot is" in the panel, under *Play as*: nothing, Ethan, Mia or Clancy), so Mia's and Clancy's bodies can be tried alone (Clancy's comes from his files: tick *Load models that are not in this scene from their files* first; it ships off). (The exe change; `kind 11 "char:<key>"`.)

**Enemy animations**
10. **New button 12 in "Send me this": "Enemy animations (.motlist)".** Stand near enemies (or the player), press it, then fight or run for 30 s. Read-only. For the player and up to 8 enemies within 80 m it asks the engine's `via.motion.Motion` for its motion banks (`getMotionBank`, `getMotionListPathForTool` = the `.motlist` file path), their sizes, and then every 0.25 s which bank and motion id each layer plays; it writes the paths to **`re7mp/motlists.json`** (every path ever seen, kept between sessions) and the rest to `health.enemy_motions` in the debug file. It cannot tell the names of the animations yet; the paths and the ids are the start. It does nothing until pressed. A crash inside it is remembered (crumb stage `motion`): the next start says so in the panel and in `health.enemy_motions.previous_session_ended_inside`.

**Defaults and the panel**
11. **"Reset to defaults" at the top of the panel** (two clicks, the second only for a few seconds): puts every remembered setting back to what a new player gets (the shipped `defaults.json`, or the code's own when there is none). It leaves the exe's Play-as and story boxes alone, and your clips. The debug file says how often it was used.
12. **The shipped defaults were rebuilt** (`data/re7mp/defaults.json`, 44 settings and 16 clips). Testing leftovers and one-player choices are never shipped (`who_choice`, `who_follow`, `story_callout`, `story_watch`, the Mia head boxes) and *face me* is off. **Loading models from their files now ships OFF** (`load_by_path`; see the crash paragraph below): a new install does not load Clancy's body, Mia's own head or enemy models from files until the box is ticked; the scene's own models (Ethan, Mia, the enemies that are in the scene) work as before. `pup_on` (enemy models as puppets, made from scene models) stays on. A player's own settings still win key by key, so a file of yours that has the loader on keeps it on.

**Log noise (found in your framework log)**
13. **34,266 warnings "Invalid number of arguments ... app.Item.getItemDataId"** in 50 minutes: that method takes an argument, and the pointing hook called it without. The script now looks up how many arguments a method takes (once per type and name) and does not call a getter that takes some.
14. **7,308 errors "Failed to load file re7mp_msg_in.json ... unexpected end of input"** (and 489 for `re7mp_in.json`): an empty leftover file of an old exe was read again every 30 ms until the exe started. After a failed read the script now waits 0.1 s, then 0.25, 0.5, 1 and 2 s; one good read starts the normal pace again. A running exe always has the files, so nothing changes while you play. The debug file has `health.bridge_reads` (failed reads, reads that waited).

**The crash you reported (16:13, walking around): not fixed, and I can say what it is not.** The framework log's call stack ends in the game's own code (`re7.exe+0x2407253`, the release of a managed object, called from a worker-thread job); no Lua frame is on it and the breadcrumb file says nothing was in progress. It is the same place as the 9.15 and 9.32 crashes: the game releases an object whose type pointer is already empty (a use-after-free or a count released once too often). I have no way to tell yet which object. **Your note that it was Ethan's body when it crashed agrees with the debug file** (`real_body.built_model` is `Pl0000_Chapter1`): Mia was not on screen at the crash. But that session had made Mia's head from her file earlier (`face.made 1, joined 1`) and loaded Clancy's four files (`loader.attempts`: loaded, then "Pl3000: not usable by path"), and the script keeps resources made from files for the rest of the session. The 9.31 crash (Clancy cloned by path) also involved files. So loading from files is the lead, **not a proven cause**; the loader now ships off. If it comes back, send the same four files again (debug, `lastcall.json`, the trail and trail history, plus `re2_framework_log.txt`, whose last lines hold the crash): they say what the script was doing in the last seconds and whether a breadcrumb was open.

**What I need from you** is in the reply for this build: what to test with your friend and which files to send back.

**Not done:** the animation *names* of enemies (the probe first), which area each player is in (for FORCE), autosave sync, the Nexus mod (credit only for now).

**Checks:** **3007** Lua checks (3050 at 9.32: the checks of the removed item-creation route went with it, the 9.33 ones are in), the C++ suite (new: the bot's health and character, the story box file), the syntax of the exe's window code, and a defaults file rebuilt from your own settings.

## What changed in v9.32 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers; the exe changed too (the 15-save limit), but the script works without it**)

It answers your 2026-11-11 notes for 9.30 (the 12:30 file, eight points), the 9.31 real-game results (12:56: six points) and your 13:03 message (item data in the debug file, a new way to share items). I used your game data folder for the cutscene and save facts. Everything below is tested with the mock game only (**3050 Lua checks**, the C++ suite passes) and is **not tried in the real game**. The exe change is in the core code (`save_sync`, `engine`), which builds and is tested on Linux; `gui_win.cpp` did not change.

**From the 9.31 real-game file**

1. **"Body fails at step mesh" / "the mesh for Ethan fails when I look at the loopback bot" / "Mia's stand-in works but not Ethan".** One cause: in the main house Ethan's object is called `Pl0000`, not `Pl0000_Chapter1`. The body lookup asked the file loader inside the first name's lookup, so the loader answered before the second name was tried: his body was made from his *file* (which the game would not take, 8 times in your file) while the real, loaded Ethan stood in the scene. Now **a model that is in the scene is always made from the scene, under both of his names**, and files are only used after that. Mia's worked because her stand-in comes from a scene object.
2. **"The game crashed when I cloned Clancy (pl3000) to the loopback bot".** The crash came a moment *after* the calls that made the body had returned (in the game's own next frame, where no breadcrumb is written). What I changed, all around bodies made from model files: the resource and its holder are kept by a reference of their own; a body is "ready" only when its mesh is loaded and its joints exist (up to 10 s; otherwise it is given up); **no part is attached and no same-joints link is made to a body that is not ready, and a body made from files gets no extra parts at all** (the same-joints link to an unloaded mesh is my best suspect); the rest pose is read only once the joints exist (the 9.31 file said `joints: null`, 55 missing). A small file `re7mp/loadstate.json` is written when such a body stands and cleared when it is gone or has run fine for a while; **a session that ended with it set switches loading by path off at the next start and the journal says so.** A model the game refused is remembered as bad for the session. This is the least certain fix of the build: if Clancy still crashes, send `loadstate.json`, `lastcall.json` and the debug file (`loader`).
3. **Marker arrow wrong way, and 10 s.** The "right" direction on screen was mirrored (the 9.27 guess); it is now the other way round, and the finder lasts 10 s, not 15.
4. **"It cannot tell which weapon I am holding."** The item states never change in your file (every weapon stayed 2, `INVENTRY_IN`), so the main-hand state is no signal. The held weapon is now looked for in **four places** and the first that gives one answer wins: the item state (as before), the weapon item's own object (the one that is *drawn*), a weapon object (`wp####...`) drawn under my player object, and a field of the player's gun / weapon-change / hands components that points at one weapon. Whenever what the places say changes, a line goes into `health.weapons.evidence_log` and a probe (the fields of the gun components, the objects, the quick slots) is kept (12 at most; a button takes one by hand). Reading only. **Whether any of the four is right is for your next file.**

**From the 9.30 notes**

5. **Cutscene recorder stops on gameplay, and F10 works** (notes 1 and 5). While a cutscene is recorded the player's own base animation is read ten times a second; when it is idle, walk / run or stopping / starting (`0/0`, `0/1`, `0/4`, `0/5`) again for 1 s after the first 4 s, the recording ends and the gameplay part is cut off the clip. A "cutscene" that never leaves those animations is written down as an **event trigger** (counted: `cutscenes.event_triggers_seen`) and its joint clip is not kept. Both are panel options with a grace slider. **F10 now stops any running recording**, the cutscene probe's too (before it only worked with the clip recorder switched on).
6. **The quick-select wheel (`SystemObject/MenuManager/QuickEquipMenu`) no longer sends the Inventory pose** (note 2). It still hides the other player like any menu; every showing menu is looked at now, so the wheel showing next to the inventory cannot hide the inventory from the pose.
7. **Giving needs the other player within 3 m** (note 3). A gift is refused here when the other player is further than 3 m (or the distance can't be told), with a message that says how far they were; the receiver checks again with 4 m of room for lag, so an older or modified script on the other side can't break the rule. **A downed player can still open the inventory and give** (the live player may need what they carry); healing while downed stays blocked. Debug file: `health.items.range_rule` (the distance now, the limits, the refusals). The panel's receive tests are exempt.
8. **Mia's own face** (note 4). The stand-in was a copy of the scene's `Em2000`, the corrupted Mia, with her head `em2050`. The playable Mia has her own head, `pl2050`, with the same skeleton below the neck. With the new box ticked (it is, at the start) the corrupted head is not copied: Mia's own head is loaded from its file and joins the body when the game has loaded it. If it does not load (mesh and material both) within 10 s it is taken away and the scene's head is made instead; after one failure it is not tried again this session. Press *Rebuild the body* after changing the box (panel *Other player's body*). A read-only scan of the heads in the scene (their mesh, material file and material names) is in the debug file (`body_lab.face`). **Whether `pl2050` shows a good face is for your eyes.**
9. **Forcing a cutscene on the other player (EXPERIMENTAL, off, not remembered)** (note 6). New box *FORCE* under *Story sync*, ticked on the computer that should *play* the other player's cutscene: when the other player starts `c03e03_00` the script here calls the game's own `requestEvent(name, transform, null, null)` too, so you can stand aside while they do the cutscene. The transform is a new empty object of mine put at the place *their* game's transform stood (it is taken from the message; your recordings show the event transform is either 1 to 5 m from the player or exactly the origin). Limits, all in the file: only names that look like `c03e03_00`; only when I am within 30 m of where they started it (the area may not be loaded here; the move box takes you there first); one at a time (25 s after any event started here, 20 s between two); never the same one within 60 s; not while a menu is open, downed, or in a tape / flashback; never echoed back to them (it would start it there again). **A crash is remembered:** `re7mp/forcestate.json` is set when one is played and cleared after 90 s of the game running fine, and a breadcrumb stage `force` is written around the call; a session that ended inside it puts that cutscene on a *not-played* list at the next start (a button forgets the list). A call that fails is counted and the cutscene goes on the not-played list; an answer of *no* is only counted. A test button plays the last cutscene *you* started again here. **What I don't know:** whether the game starts a cutscene with a made-up transform and no end callback, and which area each player is in (only the 30 m check). Debug file: `health.story.force` (counts, the last call, the not-played list, how often the game's own callbacks are null).
10. **Save slots: 15 at most** (note 8). The game lists 15 saves; the tool allowed 99, so a co-op save could land in a slot you never see. Now **nothing is put past slot 15**: a received save goes one past your highest save while that is 15 or less, else into the lowest free slot, and **when all 15 hold a file the oldest co-op copy that was installed here is replaced** (the date is in the manifest; a copy can be sent again, a save of your own cannot). **Your own saves, and the saves you sent, are never overwritten.** If all 15 are saves of your own the co-op save is *not* installed and the message says so and what to do (delete one in the game, ask them to save again). The panel's *why it is in this slot* line says which of the cases it was. **Autosave sync is not done** (the game's `data000.bin` is never shared; the slots are what is listed). **Not built:** an opt-in "overwrite my oldest own save (with a backup)": destructive, and the window code can't be compiled here; say if you want it.
11. **Your game data folder** (note 7) was read for all of this: the 17 recorded cutscenes (names, event transforms, the animation ids), the debug file, the settings and the save manifest (14 slots listed, one of them "sent").

**Items (your 13:03 message)**

12. **The 9.31 file had no "you picked up" line although items were picked up.** The pickup hook sat on the one `addItem` overload that `get_method("addItem")` answers (2 parameters); the game's own pickup uses another (4 parameters, in your trace). The hook is now on **every `addItem` overload**; each call is recorded with its overload, and the last 40 are in the debug file (`health.item_sync`).
13. **A new choice for who gets a picked-up item.** Unticked (the start, as before): an item the other player picks up **vanishes on my screen** (the first to pick it up gets it). New box *Both players get it*: it **stays on my screen** and I pick it up too (each of us gets our own). Saved with the settings; the counts of both cases (removed here, kept here, not found, echoes) are in `health.item_sync`.
14. **A new way to hand over items: the ground-only route (EXPERIMENTAL, off, not remembered).** Everything that ever crashed your game went through `addItem` on an object the script had made. With the box ticked a gift of a kind I don't hold, and a discard, become **a drop on the ground** (`createDropItemInstance` only: nothing is made for the inventory and `addItem` is never called); the other player picks it up with the game's own pickup, which the (new) pickup hook sees and syncs. Growing a stack of a kind already held stays the first way. For each of the last 8 drops the debug file says what it was made of, its item state, whether it was still there 2 s later and whether it was picked up (`health.items.ground`). **I don't know that a dropped object shows or can be picked up.**
15. **Item data in the debug file** (`health.item_data`, read-only, 15 s after the start, again within 30 s when a new kind is held): per kind held its count, the biggest stack, its state, the fields of the item and of its save record, and (on an item manager that knows items) the game's own record of the first few kinds; the field layout and the method list (name, argument types, return type) of `app.Item`, its save record, `app.ItemData`, `app.Inventory`, the inventory entry, `app.ItemManager` and `app.InventoryMenu`; and the methods whose *name* says discard, drop, remove, lost, destroy, throw, pick, take, put, add, stack, equip, disable, slot, give, reduce or consume listed on their own. A breadcrumb (stage `idata`) switches it off at the next start after a crash; a button reads it again.

**Found by the tests while writing them:** the force option had a per-minute limit and a six-second pending timeout that could never be reached (removed); the C++ slot picker had a check that could never matter once all 15 files exist (removed); the first slot test for "slot 15 is the only free one" was missing (added); a cutscene my own game started 30 s ago had no test against being played again by the other player's message.

**What I need from you** is in the reply for this build (and in the README's 9.32 section): what to click and which files to send.

**Not done:** autosave sync and "overwrite my own oldest save" (see 10), a way to know which area each player is in (for FORCE), enemy animation, weapon *models* on the other player, the Nexus mod (mods/8, credit-only for later), anything beyond the mock game's reach: every item above says "unverified in the real game" for a reason.

**Checks:** **3050** Lua checks (was 2633 at 9.31) plus the C++ suite, which gained the 15-slot unit checks and a two-engine test with a full save folder (the oldest co-op copy is replaced; with 15 saves of his own nothing is installed). **Deliberate-breakage test:** I broke the new code on purpose in about 190 places this build (the body and loader fixes, the weapon detector, the item sync and ground route, the item data, the 3 m rule, Mia's face, the force option and the 15-slot picker, 19 of them in C++) and ran the tests after each. The tests noticed most straight away; for every breakage they did not notice I wrote a test and ran the same breakage again, and all of them are now caught (the force option alone: 57 breakages, 2 of them needed a new test and one was badly written; the slot picker: 1 of 19).

## What changed in v9.31 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers; the exe changed too (a "Play as" box), but the script works without it**)

It answers your 2026-11-11 notes of 07:39 (enemy models, Clancy as a playable character with a selector in the exe, the item trace, the gun damage table, which weapon is held). Everything below is tested with the mock game only (2633 checks) and **not tried in the real game**. The exe change could **not be compiled here** (no Windows compiler in my workspace): its logic is in `src/character.hpp`, which is built and tested on Linux, but the new window code in `gui_win.cpp` is unbuilt.

1. **The host's enemies are drawn as their real models; the red box is the fallback.** The names the host sends (`Em3000` ...) are your model ids, so the script has your whole list (`CFG.ids`: 36 ids from Mia (Corrupted) to Shipworker David; the label now reads `Em3000  HP 400  - Jack Baker`). For the nearest 6 living enemies within 70 m an object is made with that model's mesh and material (and its head and other parts) the way the Real body is made, put where the box was and turned the way it faces. If a model cannot be made the box stays and the label says why (tried again after 12, 24, 48 s, three times, then the box for the rest of the scene). A scene change drops the models, the script reset destroys them. **They stand in the model's rest pose: no walking, no attacks** (that needs the enemies' animation clips: a later build). Shots still hit the red box's volume. Panel: *Enemy models*, a box (on unless the last session ended inside a make) and *Also draw the red box next to the model (to compare)*. A breadcrumb (stage `puppet`) is written before each make: if the game closed inside one, the next start switches the models off.
2. **Loading a model from its file (EXPERIMENTAL).** Enemy models and the Clancy / Mia body copies need a model that is not in the current scene (Clancy only exists in the tape scene). The script now knows the file paths of your 80 collected models (`CFG.load.KNOWN`) and can load one with `sdk.create_resource("via.render.MeshResource", path)` + `create_holder`, then make the object from it. **Whether the game accepts this is the biggest unknown of the build**: button *Test: load Clancy's body mesh from its file now* (*Who I am*) tries one and the debug file's `loader` section says what happened (`tried`, `loaded`, `failed`, the reason). A breadcrumb (stage `loader`) switches it off at the next start after a crash.
3. **Clancy (and Mia) as playable characters; a selector.** Tape watcher dropped: the tape GUI always read "showing" in your file. What the file did show: the **player object's name** tells who you play (`Pl0000_Chapter1` Ethan, `Pl2000` Mia's flashback, `Pl3000` Clancy at the tape), so the tape trigger is now "the player object is not Ethan's". New *Who I am (character)* panel and an **exe "Play as" box** (Automatic, Ethan, Mia, Clancy): the choice is written to `reframework\data\re7mp_char.json` (`{"character":"clancy","seq":ms}`), the script reads it (a new counter = a new choice), applies it, and sends it to the other player as the event `char`; their body is then drawn as that character (needs the Real body box; a box *Show the other player's body as the character they are*). Automatic follows the player object. The character is repeated every 10 s and forgotten after 45 s of silence.
4. **Which weapon is held, and whether it is a knife or axe.** The inventory is read once a second while someone is connected (or after the button *Read my weapons now*); the item whose state is the main hand (3, `EQUIP_MAIN`: **my guess, not confirmed**) is the held weapon: a gun (id, damage, stun from your table), a knife or an axe. The second-hand state (4) is kept as a second signal, and every weapon-like item with its state is listed in the file so a wrong guess shows. Sent as the event `wpn` (the id, `none` when nothing), shown in the panel as what I hold and what the other player holds. Ammunition (`HandgunBullet` ...) is not a weapon. This is the identifier for weapon and weapon-model sync later; nothing is drawn yet.
5. **Your gun damage table** (22 guns: M19, G17, MPM, Albert Reward, Albert, Albert C, M37, DB, Shotgun_Albert, MachineGun, Magnum, Burner, HyperBlaster, BlueBlaster, RedBlaster and the `_L` upgraded versions; the acid, flame and liquid-bomb numbers are in the file too). Two uses, both optional: *Shots at the host's enemies do the held gun's damage x the scale* (off; the scale is 2.1 because the one real pistol shot measured 210 for a 100 bullet) and **friendly fire**.
6. **Friendly fire (optional, OFF at every start, never remembered).** When I fire and the line of fire crosses the other player's body (an upright box 0.7 x 1.8 m plus a little slack) with no wall in between, they are told (`ff`, the damage = the held gun's table damage x a scale slider, 100 when the gun is not known, never over 600) and take it off their health **never below 1**. **Both players tick it**: the receiver ignores a shot while its own box is unticked. The shot is noticed the way the enemy hits notice it (the gun's `shoot` call, hooked also when friendly fire is on).
7. **Items: a journal for solving the sync.** From your trace: a pickup is `setItemGotFlag(id)` then `createItemInstance(the player, id)`; ammo goes through `applyDifficultyItemNumRate(id, n)`. New *Items: journal* (on by default, read-only): every one of those calls (plus `createDropItemInstance`) is written down with the time, who and where I stood; **every change of the inventory** (entry added, removed, count changed, state changed) is written down, read once a second and every quarter second for 4 s after a call, with the call that came just before; an optional second box writes down **the objects within 5 m at a pickup and again 1.5 s later; the ones that are gone are the item that was picked up** (12 pickups at most; a breadcrumb `scan` switches the whole journal off after a crash). Debug file: `health.pickups`, `health.inventory_changes`. A new button *Trace app.Inventory* sits next to *Trace app.ItemManager*.
8. **Found by the tests while writing them:** the two new sliders (gun damage scale, friendly-fire scale) would never have moved (a `pcall` result was unpacked one value off); the weapon reader needed the inventory to be found by someone else first, so it never read in a game where nothing had asked yet (it now looks itself, every 5 s at most); an ammunition item counted as a weapon.

**What I need from you** is in the reply for this build (and in the README's 9.31 section): what to click and which files to send.

**Not done:** enemy animation (clips for the enemies), the exe window code built and tried, weapon *models* on the other player, the Nexus mod (mods/8, credit-only for later), anything that needs the loader to work first (Clancy's body when he is not in the scene) until it is shown to work, item creation into a live inventory, cutscene sync.

**Checks:** 2633 (was 2313 at 9.30) plus the C++ suite (character file: write, read, damaged, a failing rename, a negative counter, same counter twice). **Deliberate-breakage test:** I broke the new code on purpose 101 times (91 in the Lua script, 10 in the character-file module `src/character.hpp`) and ran the tests after each. They noticed 76 of the 91 Lua breakages and 8 of the 10 C++ ones straight away. For each of the other 14 Lua and 2 C++ breakages I wrote a test and ran the same breakage again: all 16 are now caught. The one left is a Lua breakage that changes nothing a test can see (the friendly-fire switch is checked twice, once at the call site and once inside the function; removing the inner check cannot be seen).

## What changed in v9.30 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **update `re7mp.lua` on both computers and put `defaults.json` next to it (see 4); a rebuilt exe does both**)

It answers your 2026-11-11 notes (04:13 and 04:15, with the *Logs 1* and *My settings* folders and the model list from the file-list thread). Everything below is tested with the mock game only (2313 checks) and **not tried in the real game**.

1. **Items: the inventory is found again.** Your Logs 1 (the main house after the dinner cutscene: the mod "can't tell how many items I have"): the script kept the inventory object it found once and trusted it for the rest of the session; the game builds a new one at that point, and the old reference reads nothing. Now every 0.5 s it checks that the one it holds is alive, every 3 s it compares it with the other candidates, and it searches again when it is dead: the player's own component, then the scene's components (the one on the player first), then the managed singleton, then what a `reduceItem` hook captured. A failed search is repeated at most once a second, and each re-find goes into the item black box (`re7mp/trail.json`). **Creating an item you hold none of is still off by default**: the earlier creation tests crashed the game, and I do not know that creation works into a live inventory. Giving what you hold (the stack grows) is the safe path and is what the fix is for.
2. **Healing items while downed.** The `reduceItem` call is skipped for healing items while you are downed (*Health, downed and revive > Block healing items while downed*, on by default, remembered). The item is not spent; the use animation may still play (nothing known gates it).
3. **The gun pose plays too much.** The learned "gun out" signal in your settings was `PlayerUpperVerticalRotate.PrevWeaponActionID` with the values 2, 700, 701, 702; 2 is the unarmed block, so the pose showed whenever you blocked with empty hands. It is no longer shipped, a learned signal is ignored while `PlayerGun.actionID` is 0, and the pose is let go after 12 s without aiming or firing (slider 0 to 120 s in *Developer tools > Debug model*, 0 = never).
4. **Crouching sank into the floor.** Your crouch recording lowers the hips by 604 mm (985 to 381) and the body was lowered by the crouch as well. When the playing clip has the hip, the body is lifted by `CFG.CROUCH_DROP` (0.5 m) times how far they crouch. The 0.5 is a first estimate from the data; tell me if the feet still sink or float.
5. **No weapon models on the other player.** The body copy only takes pieces from Ethan's own model folder, so no gun was ever drawn; weapon pieces (names `wp####`, *weapon*, *handgun*, or a mesh in a weapon folder) are now skipped by rule. The gun *pose* remains.
6. **A new install animates.** `defaults.json` ships the 16 clips and your settings (the Real body box on, wall and menu hiding off, cutscene probe off). Order for a clip: the player's `re7mp/clips/<label>.json`, the older flat `re7mp_clip_<label>.json`, then the built-in one; for a setting: the player's own file key by key, then the shipped one. The exe installs it (new `install_defaults`, resource 102; **the exe changes are not compiled here**); for a manual install copy `data/re7mp/defaults.json` from the zip. *Animation clips > "Test: use only the built-in clips"* shows what a new install gets. `tools/build_defaults.py` builds it. The clips are the ones from the earlier package (your *My settings* folder had no clips).
7. **A tidy data folder.** The script's own files moved into `reframework/data/re7mp/` (`settings`, `debug`, `models`, `trail`, `trail_history`, `lastcall`, `coop_played`); old flat files are still read when the folder has none; if the game refuses the folder (a file is written and read back at the start) the flat names are used and the debug file says so (`data_folder.used`). The bridge files the exe uses (`re7mp_in/out/msg_in/msg_out/state_in/state_out.json`, `re7mp_coop_saves.json`) stay where they are. I used `re7mp` for the folder rather than `scripts`: it holds data; the script itself has to stay in `reframework/autorun`.
8. **The panel regrouped.** Everyday sections first, one folded **Developer tools** group for the rest (debug model and poses, testing tools, cutscene probe, item hook finder, model collection, model probe, body lab, clips). *Other player's body* has the model, hair, the debug-model box and the hide-near slider. **Removed:** *Avatar (experimental)* and *Probe RE7 types*, and the avatar code behind them.
9. **VHS tapes and Clancy.** A tape watcher (the tape player's screen is the GUI `VideoCameraUI`; it only counts after it was seen hidden once; 0.6 s debounce) tells the other player when you start and stop a tape, collects the models in view while it plays (so Clancy's model gets into `models.json`), and can hold back the other player's body while they are in a tape and you are not. The model checklist now has Clancy (pl3000, pl3100) and the ids from the file-list thread: Mia pl2000/pl2100 and em2000/em2200/em2300, Jack em3000/em3700/em8000/em8100/em8500, Marguerite em3100/em3600/em3800, Lucas em3200/em3900, Zoe em3400/pl3400, Molded em4000/em4100/em4200. (I could not read the thread myself; the ids are from the list you pasted.)
10. **Radmin VPN** instead of Tailscale in the README, the window hint and the connection text.

**Not done:** the Nexus mod (mods/8): credit-only for later, not used, ray tracing support unknown; the arrows-flipped note from 9.27 (not in your latest list); stopping the heal *animation* while downed; item creation into a live inventory; cutscene sync (the tape events are a start).

**Checks:** 2313 (was 2152 at 9.29). Deliberate breaks of the new code (mutations: a small change to see whether a test notices) were tried for the inventory re-find (24), the tape watcher (26), the data folder (23), the built-in defaults (16) and the weapon rule (6), plus the gun, crouch and item-lock changes. Every one is now caught by a test except two judged harmless: the tape's first `start` message not being sent (the 5-second repeat sends it anyway) and the folder check ignoring a failed write (the read-back already catches it). About a dozen of them got through the first time and now have a test (the old-name fallback for the crash breadcrumb and the played-with list, checking the folder only once, the weapon names, the tape hold-back setting being loaded, the 0.45 s blink that must not end a tape, the quiet tape collection, reading the shipped file once and again on 'Read the clip files again').

## What changed in v9.29 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` on both computers**; the exe is the one from the 9.25 package)

It answers your 2026-11-09 message of 13:04 (the clip data, "calibrate", one easy toggle that does the cloning for you, the green man's wall problem). Everything below is tested with the mock game only (2152 checks) and **not tried in the real game**.

1. **One box does the cloning** (top of the panel: *Real body: show the other player as a real in-game model (Ethan or Mia), made for me*). Ticked, it runs the lab's steps by itself, one after the other, once the other player (or the loopback bot) is there and you are in a game scene: make the body object with the model's mesh and material, add its parts, match its height to Ethan's (any model but Ethan's own; it waits while you crouch), make it follow the other player, play the clips on it. The panel shows the step it is on, then `Real body: running (Pl0000_Chapter1 copy, 3 part(s), size 1.000; debug model hidden)`. The model is a text box (`Pl0000_Chapter1` for Ethan, `Em2000` for Mia) with **Use Ethan** / **Use Mia (Em2000)** buttons and **Rebuild the body**; typing alone does not rebuild. A model that is not in the scene yet is waited for (3, 6, 12 ... 30 s between looks), not counted as a failure. **Hide hair parts** (remembered) hides the parts named hair, which are cloth the copy does not have.
2. **The green man steps aside, and only when the real body really stands in.** It is not drawn while the box runs. It is drawn whenever the real body is not up: box off, a step failed, no `idle` clip file (the body would stand in its bind pose; the panel says so and looks for the file again every 5 s), the lab's *It follows* or *Play the clips* unticked, or a new scene being rebuilt. *Also show the debug model (the green man) next to it* brings it back to compare. This is your "the walls are drawn and made correctly for in game models": the game's own depth test hides the real body, so the wall probes only matter for the name tag now.
3. **Any failure falls back.** A step that errors, a mesh the game won't take, a game phase that never comes (three tries of 10 s), a hand-made body with no mesh component, or the clip player switching itself off: the body is taken down (destroyed if the box made it), the green man is drawn, the panel says `Real body: failed - <step>`, the journal and the debug file's `real_body` section say which step. **Try again** starts over. A failure is never retried by itself.
4. **A new scene takes the body away; the box notices and makes it again** after 2 s (`restarts` in the debug file). Unticking takes down the body the box made (parts first), stops the following and the clip player it started, and leaves a body you made by hand alone.
5. **On by default later.** `CFG.REAL_BODY_DEFAULT = false` at the top of the script. Flip it to `true` for the release once it has been tested; the tick is remembered only after *you* have ticked or unticked it (`real_body_set` in the settings), so the new default reaches everyone who has not chosen. Then the green man is the debug model, off by default, as you asked on 2026-11-09.
6. **The rest pose, for calibrating Mia.** Your clips are Ethan's joint angles by joint name, so a model whose rig rests differently is pulled out of shape. When the body is made, the box writes each of the 54 joints' local angles before any clip touches them (the copy has no animation, so that is its own bind pose), the hip position and the head height at size 1, to `re7mp_rest_<model>.json` and to the debug file (`real_body.rest_pose`). The lab has a *Read the rest pose* button. **What I need:** tick the box once with Ethan, once with `Em2000`, and send `re7mp_rest_Pl0000_Chapter1.json`, `re7mp_rest_Em2000.json` and the debug file. This build does not retarget yet; that needs those two files.
7. **Small things.** *Show other player* off now parks the real body under the world too. The lab's *Make* and *Match height* report success or failure to the box (the lab itself behaves as before).

**Your clips (15 files, read in this build):** all 15 are valid and play. What the data says: `gun_crouch` was recorded half-crouched (its crouch flag is false), so it plays like a gun walk with bent legs: record it fully crouched. `crouch`, `crouch_walk`, `run`, `gun_crouch` and `gun_run` have angle noise on the shins (R_Shin jumps by more than 3 rad between neighbouring frames 70 times in `crouch`, 36 in `run`: the same rotation written with a different set of Euler angles near +-180 degrees); the player never blends across such a jump (it takes the nearer frame), and both writings are the same pose, so it should not show. `gun_shoot` is 0.7 s long (15 frames), enough for one shot but it loops quickly; `gun_reload` and `melee` are recorded but nothing selects them yet (no reload or melee signal exists). `heal` was recorded with its heal flag off (the label was set by hand, so it plays when the other player heals). `idle` was re-recorded in 9.28 (8 s, with the gun flag in its header); the other non-gun clips are from 9.27 builds and are still fine.

**Checks:** 2152 (was 2047 at 9.28). The tests found one bug while they were written (the "no idle clip" message never showed) and it is fixed. 104 mutations of the new code were made (a mutation is a small deliberate break of the code, to see whether any test notices); 77 were caught at once and 27 got through. 12 of those 27 now have tests that fail when the break is put back (the lab's *It follows* and *Play the clips* boxes bringing the green man back, a step that errors failing the box once and not again and again, the clip library being read in full, a refused create, the lab's rest-pose button, "hide hair" being saved, the *Use Ethan* button, the 2 s wait after a new scene); 2 went away when the code was simplified (the menu wait before matching the height, a doubled test in the green man's condition). 13 are left and judged harmless: tests that only matter for 0.2 s (`R.on`, `stage == running`, `B.mc` in the green man's condition), a guard behind the panel (`Rebuild` only shows when the box is on), an empty or non-string model name that the name pattern and the input box already rule out, a throw out of `Make` (nothing in it can throw in the mock), two settings type checks that only matter once the default is flipped to true, the `owns` bookkeeping after a lost body, and the 5 s pause before looking for the idle file again and the 0.2 s pace of the box (both only cost time). The tests also found two real bugs while they were written: the "no idle clip file" message never showed, and an error in the very tick the body is made (a refused scale) was swallowed by the box's own error baseline; both are fixed.

**Not done:** retargeting a model whose rest pose differs (needs the two rest-pose files above), hair and cloth physics, reload and melee detection, the Ethan model confirmed in the real game (and with it the default), everything from the 9.28 list that is still open.

## What changed in v9.28 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` on both computers**; the exe is the one from the 9.25 package)

It answers your 2026-11-09 message of 09:50 (the loopback bot facing you, Mia's model, gun poses, cutscene animations, event triggers, host save labels, invincibility, the full model list). Everything below is tested with the mock game only (2047 checks) and **not tried in the real game**. Several things are probes that ask the game questions and write the answers into the debug file; they are labelled in the panel with what to press and what to send back.

1. **"Face me" for the other player's body** (panel > *Testing tools (dev build)*). The stand-in, the loopback bot included, turns on the spot to look at you (sideways only), so you can see the model from the front. Off at every start. Unverified in the real game.
2. **Invincible** (same node; your "toggleable invincibility so I can test with enemies"). Whenever the game takes your health down, it is put back to full at once (the same write the health module already uses for the revive and the heal). Off at every start, never remembered. It does not stop an execution or an instant game over (those are not health). The panel counts how many times it put your health back.
3. **Body size and "Match Ethan's height"** (Real body lab). A size slider 0.50 to 1.50 and a button that sets the size so the copy's Head joint is as high above its feet as Ethan's (he must be in the scene, standing). Your note that `Em2000`/Mia is shorter than Ethan is why. The size is remembered (`body_scale`).
4. **Parts can be hidden one by one** (Real body lab): a "Show part" box for the main body and each part, remembered by name (`body_hidden`), and a button "Hide the hair parts". A part that refuses `set_Enabled` is hidden through `DrawDefault`.
5. **Mia's hair** (your "gray unshaded blocks" and "the hair doesn't fall with gravity"). What is probably happening, from the files: the hair is separate meshes (`em2071_L/R_sim`, `Em2000ChainHair`) that the game animates with cloth / chain physics components on the *original* object; the lab copies the mesh and the materials, not those components, so the copy's hair stays rigid. The grey blocks are most likely materials that were not loaded because she was not in the scene when it was scanned (a material that is not loaded is drawn as a plain default). Neither is verified. What 9.28 does: you can hide the hair, and a **cloth probe** (button in the Real body lab) writes every cloth / dynamics-looking component of the original's parts, with their fields and values, into the debug file (`health.body_lab`). The physics itself is **not** copied. Next, if you want it: copy those components (needs the probe's answer), or sway the hair joints from the script like a pendulum. Also: `Pl2000` (the playable Mia) is already in your collection and is the better thing to copy for her than `Em2000`.
6. **The model collection takes 250 models** (was 70; your list was full) and drops entries that have no mesh at all.
7. **Gun poses.** The flags carry three new bits (**gun out 1024, aiming 2048, just fired 4096**; the exe passes the 16-bit field through, so the protocol is unchanged). *Just fired* is the gun's own `shoot` / `shootStart` call (the hook the hit sync uses; with someone connected it is now hooked on the host too, and nothing else of the gun is), kept for 0.4 s. *Gun out* is, until a recording teaches better, `PlayerGun.actionID` not 0 (a guess from your files: it is 0 for the whole early game and mostly 91 with a gun). *Aiming* has no default: nothing in your files is known to mean it. The other player's label shows `[gun out]`, `[aiming]`, `[shooting]`; the clip player chooses `gun_idle`, `gun_walk`, `gun_run`, `gun_crouch`, `gun_aim`, `gun_shoot` (a missing clip falls back: shoot > aim > idle with gun > idle; run > walk). The clip recorder (F10) files an "auto" recording under these labels from your own pose bits. The green man itself still has no gun arms. The panel's pose preview has three new choices (Gun out, Aiming, Shooting).
8. **The guided gun recording** (button 11, third button: "Record my player with a GUN"). It teaches the script which game field means *gun out* and which means *aiming*: you take the gun out and put it away, aim twice, fire three shots. It reads fields only (no hooks of its own) and works like the block recording: for each field it keeps the values it had in every step, then lists the fields whose values with the gun out (or aiming) never occur in the steps without it. The best one is used at once (a box in the panel, ticked, switches the learned signals off again, and a button forgets them) and is saved with the settings. The debug file lists the candidates, so if the first pick is wrong the others are there. It also counts the shoot calls in the shooting step (3 expected). You said you can do the extra recordings and spawn weapons with a mod menu: this is the one that needs it.
9. **A probe for cutscene animations** (panel > *Cutscene probe*; your "probing what animations are triggered by which cutscenes"). Tick the box and play. Every time the game starts a cutscene or event (the read-only `requestEvent` hook the story sync uses) it records for 25 s (slider 5 to 40): at +0.4, +3, +9 and +18 s every object with a Motion component within 40 m (nearest 25) with its animation bank, id and frame, and which objects appeared since the first look (the cutscene's own actors); and Ethan's own joints with the clip recorder, in case the cutscene drives his object. Files: `re7mp_cutscene_<id>.json` and `re7mp_clip_cs_<id>.json`. The same id again within 60 s is not recorded twice; a cutscene that starts while another is recorded is listed as skipped. Remembered. **Send me those files, and say what the scene was.** If the cutscene uses its own actors (likely), the first file is where their animation ids are; Ethan's joints are probably still in that case.
10. **Event triggers: what is built and what is not** (your "making it happen for the other player, including the host"). Built: the host can now also be moved to the joiner's place when the joiner starts a cutscene (Story sync > "HOST: also move me ...", off at every start; before, only the joiner followed), and the game manager's event methods are written down: `requestEvent`'s parameter types and every method of `app.GameManager` with *event*, *cutscene*, *timeline* or *demo* in its name (panel line and `health.story.request_event_signature`). Not built: playing the cutscene on the other game. That needs the real arguments (`requestEvent` takes a name, a Transform and two objects of unknown type), and a wrong call can freeze or corrupt the story; the signature file is how the next build gets them. The design is in `re7-findings.md`.
11. **Host save labels.** A save you make while connected is now labelled "[CO-OP with Bob]" in the Load screen even when you are the host (before, only the *sent* copy and received saves had a label). It works by watching which slots the game says exist (every 5 s): a new slot while someone is connected is written down as played with them (`re7mp_coop_played.json`). **Limit:** saving over an existing slot looks the same as before and cannot be told; use a new slot.
12. **Wall probes (no code change).** You are right that the game hides a real model behind walls by itself. Once the real body is what the other player shows, untick "Hide the other player behind walls" and "Hide red boxes behind walls"; the probes stay for the green man.
13. **Corrections to the docs.** `Em2000` was written as Jack in several places (from the garage-fight trace). You say your copy of it is Mia, and her height and hair agree; `Em3000` is then Jack. The object names `Em####` are the game's pool names and may not be a stable identity; the docs now say "reported as Mia by the owner" and I have not been able to check it. `Em3100`, `Em3200` and `Em3500` stay unidentified.

**Checks:** 2047 (was 1827 at 9.27). 79 mutations of the new code were made (a mutation is a small deliberate break of the code, to see whether any test notices); 61 were caught at once and 18 got through. 14 of those 18 now have a test that fails when the break is put back: the gun-crouch label and its clip fallback, the learned-signal checks (off switch, damaged entries, name fit), the gun recording's shot count and its limit on fields with many values, the cutscene probe's 60-file limit and its look schedule after a very long frame, invincibility for a downed player and for a health of 0, the rounding of "Match Ethan's height", and a co-op save mark that is replaced by a received save. 4 are left and are not worth a test: a second limit on how many values a field may take in one step of the gun recording (the other limits already reject it), a second clamp on the body size (the slider and the height button already clamp), a range check on slot numbers in a damaged `re7mp_coop_played.json` (a slot that does not exist is dropped at the next check anyway), and a re-entry guard on the invincible refill (the mock game never calls the hook from the script's own write; the real game might).

**Not done:** playing a cutscene on the other game (needs the signature), the cloth physics of the hair, gun arms on the green man, the Ethan model as the real body (and with it the default for the debug model), anything about the friend's crash (still needs `re2_framework_log.txt` and `reframework_crash.dmp` from that computer), the ladder cutscene, the "corner of the room" moves, Jack in the car, key items while down, friendly fire.

## What changed in v9.27 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` on both computers**; the exe is the one from the 9.25 package; `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` is new)

It answers your 2026-11-09 messages of 08:02, 08:05 (the save converter), 08:23 (the release number stays 0.9) and 08:26 (the main-house file). Everything below is tested with the mock game only (1827 checks) and **not tried in the real game**.

1. **Executions end the game for both players** (your note "execution kills should down both players causing game over, because I think it's hard coded"). The v0.8 file shows an execution's game over coming with health above 1 (350 of 1100, 6 s after five `PlayerGrappleEm3000` calls), so the hold at 1 health in "Downed and revive" cannot stop it. When a player who is not downed gets a game over that looks like an execution (health above 1.5, or a grab call in the last 20 s), the death goes out as `exec` and the other player's game goes game over too. Switch: the box "Executions end the game for both players" under Death (on by default, remembered); counters and the last case in the panel and in the debug file (`execution_wipe`); a button tests the receiving side. The rule is a guess and can mistake a normal death with an old health value for an execution. The "execution test" (block the game over) is still experimental and off by default.
2. **Where is the other player?** F9 now does what a choice in "Chat and pings" says: my position to them (the old ping), their position on my screen, or both (default, remembered). "Their position" sends nothing: it shows a marker with the name and distance when they are on screen, and an arrow at the edge of the screen that points the way when they are not, for 15 s or until stopped. The chat line says the distance, the direction as seen from where you look (8 sectors) and the height (floors above/below). Buttons for each, "Keep showing where they are", and a stop button. If there is no position yet it says so.
3. **A panel for collecting model data** (your "I will get other model data during cutscenes or events as long as it is labelled in the GUI"). "COLLECT MODEL DATA for me (F11 ...)" lists what to collect (Ethan, Mia, Jack, Marguerite, Lucas, Zoe, the Molded, and any other enemy or character) with a tick for each one already collected. F11 or the button scans the characters in view, which also works in cutscenes where the menu cannot be used; a note box and the newest cutscene id are saved with it; an optional automatic mode collects every 20 s. Everything adds up in `re7mp_models.json` (70 models at most, the whole joint list for 24), which is read again at the next start. Send me that file. New hotkey: F11.
4. **The green man will become the debug model.** A two-line note under "Debug skeleton (lines)" and a README/findings entry: once Ethan is confirmed as the other player's body, the current 3D stand-in becomes the debug model and is off by default. Nothing was switched now, because until Ethan is the body it is the only thing the other player is drawn with.
5. **Standing rules recorded** (top of `re7-findings.md`): a dev build may be cluttered; when you say a release or release candidate is coming, I declutter the panel for other players; the release number stays 0.9 until you say so.
6. **The save converter** (your question): it is already built into the exe since 9.25 (key-less re-signing on receipt), and the MIT licence allows it. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` carries AutostrongSharp's licence text and the README has a credit paragraph; the licence text has to travel with the code, a credit and a link alone are not enough. This is not legal advice. The re-signing has not been tried in the real game.

7. **Effect volumes named VFX are not walls** (your message 08:26 with the main-house file: "the collider is hiding the other player with anything that says VFX on it too"). In that file 692 wall tests said "behind a wall"; `VFX_c03_OldHouse` was 153 of them and `VFX_c03_MainHouseArea` 9. A name with `vfx` anywhere in it (any case) no longer counts. Other likely non-walls from the same file are listed in the README and **not** changed, because I can't tell which are real: `c03_GardenArea1` and its variants (176), `Cube_Collider` (94), `MainHouse_underfloor_outside_1` (79), `Garden00` (52), `c03_MainHousOutsideSub01` (37), the `CallingPhone_PhoneMessage` trigger (24). The "that is not a wall" button works on any of them.
8. **The body lab and the clip recorder find Ethan in the main house.** His object is called `Pl0000` there and `Pl0000_Chapter1` in the car, the forest and the guest house; both looked for the second name only, so in the main house "Look", "Make" and the recorder would have found nobody. The first name is tried first, then the second. (From the same file: his left hand is `pl0021` there, `pl0020` at the guest house: the hand that was cut off; recorded in the findings file.)

**Checks:** 1827 (was 1692 at 9.26). 18 mutations of the new code were made; 12 were caught at once and 6 got through (the ping's direction sectors at their edges, the 70-model cap, the automatic mode staying quiet when nothing is new, the game-over screen opening not counting as a grab, and the clip recorder's fallback to the name Pl0000); each of the 6 now has a test that fails when the mutation is put back.

**Not done:** the Ethan model as the real body (and with it the default for the debug model), anything about the friend's crash (still needs `re2_framework_log.txt` and `reframework_crash.dmp` from that computer), the ladder cutscene and the "corner of the room" moves, wall false positives (only the probe from 9.26), Jack in the car, key items while down, friendly fire, the green man's gun poses, a better wall method.

## What changed in v9.26 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` on both computers**; the exe is the one from the 9.25 package)

It answers your three files and 19 notes from the v0.8 test with a friend (`f4c10c06`, `fe9f20b2`, `85e1db71`, all script 9.22 / exe 0.8). Everything below is tested with the mock game only (1692 checks) and **not tried in the real game**. What the files showed, and the answer to each note, is in `re7-findings.md`.

1. **Damage is worked out per enemy.** The host's file: `Em2000` lost exactly what it was handed in 7 of 8 hits (x1.00; the first lost 39 of 30), `Em8000` lost 77 of 110 twice (x0.70). One number for all enemies would be wrong for one of them, so the host now keeps a multiplier for each enemy name (smoothed, 0.05 to 20), and an enemy it has not measured yet starts from the last one measured. Panel line `per enemy: ...`, debug field `ratio_by_enemy`.
2. **One move per cutscene.** The same cutscene id came again and again as the host walked (`c03e21_02` is in 10 of the last 12 story events of the joiner's file, from spots up to 6 m apart). The joiner is now moved once per cutscene id within 60 s; the journal says "I was already moved for ... N s ago". The 15 s between moves, the 3 m "already there" rule and the fall check are unchanged.
3. **Red boxes hidden behind walls** (your note "an enemy block option like with hide other player, same rules"). The nearest six boxes are tested four times a second from the camera to the chest, and to the head if the chest is covered, with the same physics test as the other player and the joiner's shots. Two tests in a row both ways are needed; if the test cannot run the box is drawn. Tick box in the Enemies section (on by default), it works only while "Hide them behind walls" is ticked. Debug field `health.enemy_walls`.
4. **Item count refusals say why.** "I can't read how many you hold" is now only said when the inventory could not be read at all; when it can be read and does not hold the item, the message says so and lists what it does hold. The debug file has `health.items.last_count_unreadable` with what the list held at that moment. (The host's file shows 6 of those refusals, all after 3111 s; the cause is still unknown.)
5. **The wall probe records what the colliders are.** "Probe the walls here" now also lists, for each named collider (80 at most), its type, its yes/no and number properties (static, trigger, layer, group ...), its shape type and the method names of each type (`layer_probe.collidables`, `layer_probe.collidable_methods`). Most of what hid the other player in your files has the name of a room or an area (`Garden00`, `Cube_Collider`, `MainHouse1F`, `1F`, `B1`, `CowshedArea`, ...). The wall rule itself is **not changed**; this is only the evidence for a better one.

**Checks:** 1692 (was 1664 at 9.25). 8 mutations of the new code were made; 7 were caught and one (`enemy_walls_update` ignoring the wall option) cannot be seen from outside, because the wall test itself already answers "cannot run" when the option is off, so the result is the same.

**Not done from your notes (and why):** the ladder cutscene and the "corner of the room" moves (no cutscene id or position in the files), the crash on the friend's computer (needs `re2_framework_log.txt` and `reframework_crash.dmp` from it), Jack in the car (no enemy list from that moment), key items while down (needs a probe first), executions that come "out of nowhere", friendly fire, the debug green man's gun poses, the echo location and a new wall method. Pings already exist: F9, or the button "Ping my position" in the panel.

## What changed in v9.25 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **script AND exe: update `re7mp.lua` and rebuild `re7mp.exe` on both computers**; the exe part is the save re-signing described below)

It answers the second half of your 2026-11-08 message: "damage to the host's enemies is too small, and we may need a better model for them than a red box, maybe pull an enemy mesh from the game?"

**Damage.**
1. *Why it was small.* The joiner's hit defaulted to 30. The one real shot I have a trace of (v9.1) took 210 off Jack's 10000 health (the game's call said Damage 100, CalculatedDamage 210). So a hit was about a seventh of a shot.
2. *New default 210*, slider "Damage per hit" 1 to 600, limit 1500 per hit (`enemy.hit.MAX`; both the joiner's send and the host's receive are clamped to it). A 30 saved by the old default is ignored when settings load, so you get the new default.
3. *The host measures the game's multiplier.* When it applies a hit it remembers the enemy's health before, waits a moment, reads it again, and keeps the ratio of health lost to damage handed over (smoothed, kept between 0.05 and 20). The next hits are handed over as `asked / ratio`, so a hit takes off about what the slider says whatever the game's own multiplier is. The panel shows `the game's multiplier is x..`, the debug file `effective_ratio`, `ratio_measured`, `ratio_last`, and the journal says `enemy hit: handed N, health lost M (x..)`.
4. *Tests:* the default (210), saved values (30 and 0 give 210, 1200 is kept, 1501 gives 210), the host measuring a fake x2.1 multiplier and handing 100 on the next hit so the enemy loses the 210 meant, the panel and debug file showing it, and a hit that took nothing off saying it could not tell (the multiplier stays).

**Enemy model (the first step only).** The body lab (9.23) can now copy any scene object's mesh, not only Ethan's:
1. *New box "Object to copy"* (Ethan `Pl0000_Chapter1` by default; an empty box means Ethan) and a button **Use the nearest enemy (Em####)**, which picks the closest root named Em plus four digits that looks active (a placeholder at the world origin, or one without a Status component, is only used when nothing else is there).
2. *Look / Make / Parts* work on that object. An enemy's root has no mesh of its own, so its body is the drawn part below it with the most joints and its other parts are the drawn parts of the same kind (`Character/Enemy/...`); weapons and shadow-only copies are left out. A copy of an enemy is stood beside you, not where the enemy stands. The messages name the enemy instead of Ethan. The debug file's `health.body_lab.copying` says what is being copied.
3. *Not done:* drawing an enemy model for the joiner. That needs the evidence from this test first (does an enemy mesh go onto my own object, and is it loaded on the joiner's side at all), and then either cloning from a local enemy object or sending the mesh path and loading it on the joiner. The red box stays until then.
4. *Tests (1664 checks in all):* nearest enemy (skips the placeholder at the origin, counts only real Em#### roots, says so when there are none), Look on an enemy (no mesh on the root, body = most joints, head as a part, no weapon, no shadow copy), Make (the enemy's mesh holder and material, beside the camera not on the enemy), a name that is not in the scene, the empty box meaning Ethan. Deliberate breakages of the new code were caught by these tests (nearest ignoring the placeholder rule, the copy ignoring the chosen name, the copy standing on the original, the shadow/disabled filter, the family filter, the messages naming Ethan).

**Not tried in the real game:** all of it. In particular whether the host's damage call applies the game's multipliers (the measurement will tell), and whether the enemy mesh can be put on my own object.

**Packaging note:** the save re-signing below is in the same package, so the exe has to be rebuilt too.

## What changed after v9.24 (exe part of v9.25): co-op saves are re-signed for the receiver (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **exe only: rebuild `re7mp.exe` on both computers**; shipped together with v9.25)

It answers your note "saves dont sync properly" (RE7 saves are tied to the Steam account that wrote them) and the files you attached: AutostrongSharp's source, and `data003Slot.bin` twice (the two files are byte-identical, so there was no re-signed twin to compare; it was not needed).

**What I found.** In an RE7 save only two things depend on the Steam account: one 8-byte block of the 48-byte header, and the Murmur3 checksum in the last 4 bytes (everything else, the encrypted save, does not). The checksum code from AutostrongSharp, ported to C++, reproduces the checksum in your real save, in the sample save from the AutostrongSharp repository and in the original tool's two test files. So the receiver can re-sign a save **without the game's key**: take the account block from the saves the game itself wrote on the receiver's account, put it in the incoming file, recompute the checksum.

**Changed.**
1. *`src/save_resign.hpp/.cpp` (new).* The checksum, a reader for the header, `find_own_mark` (learns your account from your folder's saves: first 48 bytes of each `.bin`, copies this tool installed are ignored, the majority wins, a tie is refused), `resign_save` and `resign_for_this_pc`.
2. *`src/engine.cpp`.* A received save goes through `resign_for_this_pc` before it is written. It is installed re-signed, unchanged (not an RE7 save container, so the old behaviour), or **refused with a plain-English reason** (no save of your own in the folder yet, your folder's saves disagree, the file is damaged or encrypted with another key). The manifest and the read-back check use the checksum of what is on disk. No packet changed.
3. *Tests.* `tests/data/autostrong_test_*.bin` (the original tool's two test files, MIT, with a note), unit tests for the checksum, the header, the re-signing round trip (re-signing back gives the original bytes), every refusal, the folder scan; a TCP test where Alice's save arrives naming Bob's account with the body byte for byte hers, files that are not saves still arrive unchanged, and a Bob with no save of his own is refused. 22 deliberate breakages of the new code were caught by the quick unit run (`RE7MP_ONLY_RESIGN=1 ./re7mp_tests`), and two of the engine change by the full run.

**Not tried:** the game loading a re-signed save. **First suspect if it still says corrupted:** the slot number stored inside the encrypted body is not changed (it would need the game's key), and this exe installs a received save into a different slot than the sender's. If that is it, the slot rule changes.

**Dropped:** signing the exe (Authenticode) for the Windows protection dialog, at your request (`tools/sign_exe.ps1` and its README section are gone).

## What changed in v9.24 (release 0.9; same protocol v6; **script only: update `re7mp.lua` on both computers**; the exe still needs the one rebuild for 0.9 that v9.23 asked for)

It answers the file `f53d2c3a` (a 9.23 session) and your note with it: "it all works and can be manipulated, it does spawn the model to my left instead of my right ... it can even follow the loopback bot, do you want to get the animations too?" **Nothing below has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock.

**What the file showed.** The whole body lab worked in the real game, with 0 errors: `GameObject.create`, `createComponent(render.Mesh)`, `setMesh` (reads back Ethan's mesh), `set_Material` (`setMaterial` does not exist; the material reads back `pl0001.mdf2`), the component says `MeshReady`, `MaterialReady`, `Enabled`, `DrawDefault`, `HasJoint` all true with 158 joints; the three parts were parented with the same-joints constraint; Remove worked; Make again worked; it followed the loopback bot; and `R_UpperArm` was found and held turned from the script (`set_LocalEulerAngle` each tick in `UpdateBehavior`; the angles are radians). The new object has no Motion component, so nothing overwrites a joint that is set.

**Changed.**
1. *Build 9.24* (release stays 0.9).
2. *The body now stands on your right.* The formula for "right" was mirrored for this engine (the right of a heading (fx, fz) is (-fz, fx)). The stand-in body's left/right in `compute_joints` uses the same formula and is probably mirrored too; I left it alone because it is cosmetic.
3. *Animation clips (new, experimental, off).* Panel section **Animation clips (experiment: record Ethan's joints, play them on the body)**, switched on by its own box. **Recording (F10):** reads Ethan's joints (local Euler angles, 20 a second, as whole thousandths of a radian): 52 body joints (Hip, Waist, Stomach, Chest, Neck, Head, and per side Shoulder, UpperArm, Forearm, Hand, Palm, Thigh, Shin, Foot, the 15 finger joints; the toes are listed but only used if the skeleton has them), the hip's position, and the arm and finger joints of his two hand objects (20 each; box to leave them out). It also notes his speed (from his position; a jump of more than 3 m between two readings is not counted) and the game's motion ids (bank / motion / frame of the first four layers of his Motion component, 4 times a second) next to the joints. The take is filed under the label you chose, or under the label `auto` finds (inventory / heal / guard / crouch / crouch_walk from the pose flags at the start, else idle under 0.3 m/s, walk under 2.4, run) in `re7mp_clip_<label>.json`, with the list of joints that moved by more than 0.05 rad, most first (that is how we learn where his animation lives). F10 again stops it; otherwise it stops after the chosen time (2 to 20 s, default 8). A scene change drops the take. The start is written to the black box before the first joint is read, and the reading runs in the game phase chosen in the panel. **Playing:** a box on the lab body. The clip is picked from the other player's pose (inventory 512, heal 128, guard 64, crouch 256, else idle / walk / run by speed), falls back when a label has no clip (crouch_walk to walk; crouch, heal, guard and inventory to idle; run to walk), eases from one clip to the next over 0.25 s, loops without a seam (the last 0.25 s is blended into the first), and is played faster or slower in proportion to the other player's speed (0.6 to 1.6 times). Angles are blended the short way round and an angle that flips by more than 1.5 rad between two frames is not blended. No network message and no exe change: both players need the same clip files in the data folder.
4. *Debug file:* `health.clips` (the clips that were read: length, speed, frame and joint counts, the joints that moved, the motion ids, the joints that were not found; the journal lines).

**What to do next:** tick the clip recorder, stand in the game and record: idle (6 s), walk (straight, 4 s), run, crouch, crouch_walk, guard, heal, inventory (open it and look around), aim, shoot, reload, melee; make the body (step 2 of the body lab), tick "It follows the other player" and "Play the clips on the body", and watch it with the loopback bot. Send the debug file and, if you can, the `re7mp_clip_*.json` files (about 200 KB each): the `moving` list says which joints Ethan's body really animates, and the motion ids give me the table from labels to the game's own ids.

**Checks.** 1621 Lua checks (was 1537); 44 mutations of the new code (series AH: the recorder, the files, the player, the angle maths, the extra method lists) are all caught, and the seven older ones whose code or text changed (placement side, version, frame hook, lab buttons) too; three of the new ones needed a stronger test first (the lower limit on the pace, the pose flag that flickers, the render-frame choice). A hip position that can't be read (recording) or set (playing) no longer stops anything: the clip is angles only, or the hip is left alone, and a line says so. The C++ suite was not run again: no C++ file changed since the 0.9 run.

**Not done:** using the hand objects' recordings (only the body's joints are played); live joints over the network (a message kind and an exe rebuild; wanted if clips look too stiff); playing the game's own motions through a Motion component on the body (the engine's motion layer and bank types are now in the method list, so the next file can show whether it is possible); the flags-based auto label is tested only for idle / walk / run; the stand-in body's mirrored left/right; Mia or enemy models, the exe presentation clean-up, the item add crash, teleport.

## What changed in v9.23 (**release 0.9**; same protocol v6; **update the script on both computers, and rebuild `re7mp.exe`** so its version says 0.9 and the two players' version check agrees)

You said 0.8 goes out as the 9.22 build you tested and the number moves to 0.9; this is the first build of 0.9. It answers the file `3af38fb8` (a 9.22 session from the start of the game, next to Ethan's car). **Nothing below has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock.

**What the file showed.**
- *Ethan's model was found:* the object `Pl0000_Chapter1`, mesh `Character/Player/pl0000/pl0001/pl0001.mesh`, 158 joints, 2 materials; a hand model `sm1649_EthanLHand_Event` (`pl0080.mesh`, 61 joints); the enemy meshes `em2000/em2000.mesh` (156 joints), heads `em2050.mesh` (253), `em3000.mesh` (156), `em3050.mesh` (254). Mia was not in that scene (only a prop, `sm2414_MailMia01A`).
- *The scan wasted itself:* the word `pl` matched 68 plants (`Plants...`), which filled the 90 entries in scene order, and the full joint lists went to the three *nearest* objects, which were enemy arm meshes, so Ethan's 158 joints were not written down.
- Inventory pose and the debug skeleton work; the co-op saves are labelled better but not fully tested (the file has `labels_written 7`). The wall check named `c01_2F` as the collider that hid the other player (86 of 814 checks); it is probably a real floor or ceiling, so it is not ignored automatically (the "That is not a wall" button is there for it).

**Changed.**
1. *Release number 0.9, build 9.23* (`RELEASE`, `kReleaseVersion`, the tests and the README).
2. *Model probe, reworked.* Words of one or two letters (`pl`, `em`) now have to start an object's name and be followed by a digit, so `Plants` and `Emitter` no longer match. A new box, **Only meshes under Character/** (ticked), leaves out plants and props and says how many it left out. The same mesh file is listed 3 times at most (counted in `mesh_counts`); unreadable meshes are kept, ten at most, with a note. **The full joint list now goes to the player's own model first** (any mesh under `Character/Player`), then big character models (40+ joints), one per mesh file, up to 6; if there is none of those, the three nearest as before. Joint lists are up to 280 joints (the enemy heads have 250) and each joint now has its place in centimetres from the object (`joint_cm`), which is what a skeleton mapping needs.
3. *New button, "List the engine's model methods".* Writes the methods of the engine types for creating, copying, drawing and posing a mesh object (`via.GameObject`, `via.Scene`, `via.Prefab`, `via.Folder`, `via.render.Mesh`, `via.render.MeshResource`, `via.motion.Motion`, `via.motion.JointMap`, `via.Transform`, `via.Joint`) to `health.model_types`. I do **not** clone the player object: that would duplicate his controls and camera. A second Ethan body has to be a plain mesh object of its own, and that can only be written against what the engine really has.

**Second file, `b0e01f56` (a 9.23 session, Ethan only; "don't be surprised if he has no head").** The scan worked as designed: 27 entries, 15 distinct meshes, 6 whole joint lists, 5 plants and props left out. Ethan = the object `Pl0000_Chapter1` (85 components) with the body mesh `pl0001.mesh` (158 joints, a complete skeleton with names and positions), and children `Pl0000HandL` (`pl0020`, 66 joints), `Pl0000HandR` (`pl0010`, 65), `Pl0000LowerBody` (`pl0002`, 20), the codex (`pl0023`, 8). His head (`pl0050`, 253 joints) and copies of the body, hands and lower body are under `ShadowBody` and are `enabled False`: shadow casters only, so no drawn head, as you said. The game builds those copies as plain objects of a Transform and a `render.Mesh` (plus a tag), which is the recipe for a second body. The engine dump (266 lines) shows `GameObject.create(String)`, `createComponent(Type)`, `destroy`, `Prefab.instantiate(...)`, `Transform.getJointByName/get_Joints/setParent/set_SameJointsConstraint`, and `Joint.set_Rotation/set_LocalRotation/set_LocalEulerAngle`. There is **no** `GameObject.clone` (the old Avatar "Clone and follow" button calls one, so it cannot work in RE7). The dump stopped at 70 lines for `via.render.Mesh`, before its setters.

4. *Body lab (new, experimental, off).* Panel section **Real body (experiment: a second Ethan of my own)**: a tick box that switches it on, then buttons for 1 Look (read-only), 2 Make the body (new object, mesh component, Ethan's own mesh and material holders, stood 1 m to your right), 3 Add the hands and the lower body (children with the same-joints constraint), a "follows the other player" box, a joint test (name, axis, angle, hold) and 4 Remove. Each step is written to the black box before its engine call, runs in the game phase you choose (UpdateBehavior, LockScene or the render frame) and is given up after 8 s if that phase does not come. Objects are dropped (not destroyed) when the scene changes and destroyed when the script is reset. I have not seen any of these calls work in the real game: the setter names (`setMesh` / `set_Mesh`, `setMaterial` / `set_Material`) are tried in turn and read back. See the README, "Real body lab".
5. *The engine-method list is longer:* it now also writes all the setters of `via.render.Mesh`, all of `via.GameObject`, the mesh and material holder types, `via.ResourceHolder` and `via.Component` (the first list stopped before them).

**What to do next:** switch the body lab on and press 1 (read-only) and 2 near Ethan, then 3, the follow box and the joint test if 2 worked; send the debug file (after a crash too: the next start's file has the trail). Press **List the engine's model methods** again for the setters. For Mia, do the model-probe scan in a scene where she is on screen.

**Checks.** 1537 Lua checks (was 1463); the C++ suite passes with 0.9 (no C++ file changed after that run); 36 mutations of the new code (series AF for the probe, AG for the body lab) are all caught; two of the lab's (a shadow-named part that is drawn, the lab's buttons offered while it is off) needed a stronger test first.

**Not done:** using the real body instead of the shaded stand-in (the lab has to work first), animating it from the pose bits, Mia or enemy models in place of the red boxes, the exe presentation clean-up, the item add crash (no new data), teleport (nobody to test with).

## What changed in v9.22 (this is what went out as **release 0.8**; same protocol v6; update the script on both computers, and rebuild `re7mp.exe` for the new save-slot rule and the save-sharing default)

It answers your 12:41 notes and the debug file with them (`9ffdc538`, a 9.21 session). **Nothing below has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock (the C++ part against the Linux test suite; the Windows exe could not be built where I work).

**What the file showed.** The Lab 2 add answered `Put=0`, the inventory copy read `state=2 INVENTRY_IN, stack=1, slot=9`, the trail has an "alive" line 0.30 s later and then nothing: the game died after the add, as before. Nothing in the file says why the add is unreliable, so nothing is changed there. Eight shots in the file were each counted once (all eight pairs merged, gap 0.0 s: the two hooks fire in the same frame), so the 9.21 shot pairing works. `no_box_in_line 8`: none of the eight shots had a red box of the host's enemies in line, so none was sent. The walls recalibrated 16 times in one session ("behind a wall for 30 s while I walked 8 m").

**Your notes:**
1. *Colliders by the guest-house stairs.* I do not know their names (the file's top-15 list has `1F`, `c01_Kitchen01`, `c01_2F_id17` ... but nothing that says stairs).
