Two things: a name containing `stair` no longer counts as a wall, and the panel now shows **the last collider that hid them**, with a button **"That is not a wall: stop hiding them behind '<name>'"** that is remembered in `re7mp_settings.json` (`wall_skip`, at most 40 names) and can be forgotten again. The debug file counts the blockers (`health.occlusion.blockers_counted`, `last_blocker`).
2. *Shot detection, whose game?* On the **joiner's** game. Every shot is counted there; a hit is only sent to the host when a red box of one of the host's enemies is within about 80 m in the line of fire. Enemies "arriving" on the joiner's side means the boxes the host sent. The host's own shots are not looked at.
3. *Step 2 crashes, bigger log.* The journal is 1200 lines (was 600), the full log 4800 (was 3200), the item trail 120 steps (was 80), and the trails of the last 6 sessions are kept in `re7mp_trail_history.json` and listed in the debug file as `health.items.earlier_item_trails`, so a crash followed by a short session no longer wipes the evidence.
4./9. *Clutter.* The item panel lost its old notes (9.16 to 9.20 lines, the give hint, the receive explanations); the lab order is one line. The README is split: this file has what the mod does now, **CHANGELOG.md** has the history.
5./7. *Permanent.* The 3D body is always on and the "hide them for a while after a new game" is always on; both boxes are gone (an old settings file is ignored for them). The debug skeleton box stays.
6./14./15. *Mia and enemy models.* A new panel section **Model probe (Mia, Ethan, enemies)**: one read-only button that lists every character with a mesh near you (object name, mesh file, material count, joint count, distance, position) and, for the three nearest, the whole joint list with parents. Stand next to Mia in her cutscene, the headless Mia on the shipwreck, Ethan, or an enemy, press it and send the debug file (`health.model_probe`). The words it looks for can be changed. Nothing is cloned or moved. I cannot choose a model without that data.
8. *Walls smoother.* Checked 4 times a second (was 10), a change needs two checks in a row both ways (hiding and showing), calibration still runs at 10 a second, and the "calibrate again" rule is 60 s and 15 m (was 30 s and 8 m).
10. *Inventory pose.* New pose bit 512: while your inventory, item box or similar is open the other player's stand-in brings both hands up in front of the belt and leans a little. Preview it with "Stand-in pose preview: Inventory". The shape is a guess.
11. *Debug skeleton never hidden* while ticked: not by walls, menus, the new-game wait, closeness, "Show other player" off or a cloned model. The name tag says why the body is held back.
12. *Saves.* `re7mp.exe`: a received save goes **one past the highest save in the folder** (it used to take the sender's own slot number if free), a resend of the same save replaces the copy installed earlier, and it never touches a save of your own. Save sharing is **on by default** unless the exe finds no Steam save folder (new ini key, so an old `share_saves=0` does not keep it off). Downed: the "experimental" words are gone from the Death list, the weapon-lock box, the Health section and the Death section. **Downed and revive is now the default death option** (new settings key `death_mode_choice`; an old file's choice was the old default, so it is not read). Independent, Shared fate and the rest stay selectable.
13. *Story sync / teleport.* Unchanged.
16. *Chat and ping.* **F9** pings your position; the panel has a **Ping my position [F9]** button at the top and five one-click phrases in "Chat and pings" (Wait for me, Come here, Help!, Found something, Going ahead). One ping per 1.5 s.

**Checks.** 1463 Lua checks (was 1415); the C++ suite passes with the new slot rule; 24 mutations of the new code (series AE) are all caught.

**Not done:** a real Mia or enemy model (needs the probe's data), the exe presentation clean-up (later), the item add (no new data), teleport (nobody to test with).

## What changed in v9.21 (release 0.8; same protocol v6; **script only, nothing to rebuild**: update `re7mp.lua`)

It answers your two 9.20 debug files and the notes with them (Lab 7 crash; update step stops at Lab 2; the flame rounds; two shots per shot). **Nothing below has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock. 9.20 is unchanged apart from what is listed here.

**What the files showed:**
- **Taking out a whole entry is what crashes, for a game-made item too.** `8136ccfe`: Lab 7 (`reduceItem(ChemicalS, 1, true)`) on the script-made ChemicalS: the call returned, the stack read 0, and the trail has no "alive" line after it (the game died inside the first 0.05 s). `a78b07b3`, second session (safe mode, no creation): five single FlameBulletS given to the bot, each with `reduceItem went through (it answered true)`; four were finished off in the journal (`sent_ok 4`) and the fifth was not: the game died right after it. From your note the fifth was the last one you had (the file has no stack counts for these gives). So the common factor over the two items, one made by the game and one by the script, is that the entry was **removed**. A gift that only makes a stack smaller worked every time it was tried (this file alone has four).
- **The game's own removals tell something.** The 60561ee7 trace has 13 `Inventory.reduceItem` calls. Five pass `true` as the third argument: four of them removed an entry (CylinderKey, EthanCarKey, EntranceHallKey, RemedyM) and each of those is followed by **`Inventory.reOpenRequestInventoryMenu()`**, the fifth (4 HandgunBullet) only made a stack smaller and has none. The eight that pass `false` (all crafting: Herb, ChemicalS, Gunpowder) have none either. That call appears nowhere else in the trace. The script has always passed `true`. My reading, **unverified**: `true` asks the game to rebuild the inventory menu after the entry went; the game's own callers are in a state where that works, a call from this script is not. I do not know that; it is the difference I can see in the game's own calls.
- **The update step does not cure the add.** `a78b07b3`, first session (update-step box ticked, labs 1, 6, 5, 2): every step ran, Lab 2 answered `Put=0`, the inventory's copy looked like a normal entry (`state=2 INVENTRY_IN, stack=1, slot=4`), and the game died before the first alive line. So moving the calls into the game's update step did not help (it does not prove the thread idea wrong either: the callback may still run beside other game work). The add is still unreliable by itself: three adds survived, two died (one after 0.3 to 0.6 s, this one at once).
- **Two shots per shot.** I cannot read this from a file (the file has no shot counts); your note says the handgun sends two. The script hooks two gun methods, `shootStart` and `shoot`, and counted each call as a shot; both run for one trigger pull, in different frames, so both counted.

**What I changed:**
- **A give that would remove a whole entry is refused** (your last Herb, the last FlameBulletS, a whole stack, or any amount at least as big as your smallest stack of that kind, since the game seems to take from the smallest first). Nothing is sent, nothing is taken; the panel and journal say why, the file counts it (`health.items.whole_entry.refused`). If the inventory cannot be read it is refused too. Giving fewer than your smallest stack works as before (`reduceItem(..., true)`, proven).
- **A box, in every mode: *Allow giving the LAST one of a kind (a whole entry), removed the crafting way*** (not remembered). Ticked, the whole entry is offered and, when the other player confirms, taken out with the third argument **false**, as crafting does. This is a guess at the cure. It is checked again when the answer arrives; if you unticked meanwhile, nothing is taken. The trail line before the call says "the whole entry: third argument false".
- **Lab 8** is Lab 7 with the third argument false. **Lab 7** now says in the trail whether it takes the last one ("the entry goes") or one of several, and whether the script made the kind; after a removal that leaves some it writes the inventory's copy again.
- **Lab 6b** makes the waiting object a stack of 2 (Lab 7 once: one of two, the entry stays; again: the last one).
- **A kind box in the lab** (type an item id, press *Use this kind for the lab*; letters, digits and `_`; not while an object is waiting). Lab 5, 7 and 8 work on any kind in your inventory, so you can try the removal on something the **game** made, with no add before it. The Lab 1 button names the kind.
- **A hook on `Inventory.reOpenRequestInventoryMenu`** only to see whether our own call asks for it: within 5 s of a reduceItem of ours it writes `the game asked for the inventory menu to be re-opened N s after my reduceItem call (inside the call)` to the trail file (the trail file only, no debug flush, since it runs inside the game's call). The file has `health.items.whole_entry`: `giving_allowed`, `refused`, `removed_with_false`, `menu_reopen_hooked`, `menu_reopen_requests_seen`, `menu_reopen_within_5s_of_my_call`.
- **One hit per shot.** The first of the two gun calls opens a shot; the **other** call within 0.45 s is the same shot and is only written down; the same call twice is two shots. The file has `health.enemy_hits.gun_hook.second_call_of_a_shot_merged`, `first_call_of_a_shot` (which of the two comes first) and `gap_between_the_two_calls_s` (min and max), so the next file shows the real order and gap.

**What I need from you next (in this order):**
1. **Shots:** with the handgun a shot should be one hit now. Please try the shotgun once too and say if a shell gives more than one hit (I do not know whether `shoot` runs once per pellet).
2. **Gifts:** give some of a stack as before; try giving the last one: it must be refused with the reason, no crash.
3. **Lab 8 on something the game made (no add involved):** pick a spare single item you do not mind losing, type its id in the lab kind box, press *Use this kind*, press **Lab 5** (it should say you hold 1), then **Lab 8**. Wait 5 s. If the game survives, that is the first time a last unit left the inventory without a crash from this script. Send the file whichever way it goes. If it dies, tell me and I will stop trying variants of this call.
4. If 3 survived: tick the *Allow giving the LAST one* box and give your last unit of something to the bot.
5. Optional: the same Lab 7 (the old way) on a second spare single item, to see whether the true/false difference is real.

Internal build label is now 9.21; the public release is still **0.8**. Tests: 1415 Lua checks (up from 1373) and the C++ suite (no C++ changes). New ones cover the shot pairing (either order, two quick shots, the same call twice, a late second call, a closed pair, the numbers in the file), the whole-entry refusal (default, with two stacks, ticked, unticked before the answer, an unreadable inventory), the false flag on a whole entry and the true flag on a partial one, Lab 8, Lab 6b, the kind box (typed text does nothing until the button, bad text, not while an object waits), the Lab 7 wording and the copy after, and the re-open hook (within 5 s, not later, the counts in the file). Checked by mutation: 30 deliberate breakages of the new code: 28 were caught by the tests at once and 2 (the count of whole-entry removals, and the trail line before a whole-entry call) needed a stronger test, which now catches them.

## What changed in v9.20 (release 0.8; same protocol v6; **script only, nothing to rebuild**: update `re7mp.lua`)

It answers your 9.18 debug file (`e7d5b550`) and your five notes. **Nothing below has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock. 9.19 (the `Pl0000_Chapter1` name, the static cutscene slot, the wall probe) is unchanged apart from what is listed here, so you can carry on with it.

**What the file showed:**
- **The add with a stack of 1 works** (Lab 1, 6, 5, 2 in your note 3): `addItem` answered `Put=0` and the inventory held 1 ChemicalS, in both sessions. So the 9.17 crash really was the stack-0 add. That is the first time a created item has been added in the real game.
- **But the second session looks like a crash of the add itself (note 5), not only of the give.** Its trail has the alive lines at 0.13, 0.15 and 0.31 s after the add and then nothing; the 0.6 s line never came. A button press cannot happen in 0.3 s, and nothing about a give is in the file (no `you offered` line). In the first session the object lived 4 s. So the add is **possible but not reliable**: it survived once, and once the game died 0.3 to 0.6 s later with nobody touching anything. I do not know why. The ideas I can test: the game's update thread meeting calls made from the render frame (see the update-step box below), or the inventory's own copy of the item being incomplete (the trail has never looked at it; it does now).
- **The give of the created item (note 4) crashed and left no trace**, because the step that takes the item out of your inventory (`Inventory.reduceItem`) wrote nothing before it ran. Gifts of items the game made worked three times (bullets, 87 s to 106 s in the file).
- **The wall test found no floor in the trailer house** (`floor_hit_layers_by_way` is empty for all three ways, 211 rays over three rounds, the only thing ever hit was `SafeRoomCollider_TrailerHouse`, a trigger volume) and then waited 45 s (note 2).
- **Item box moves were taken for discards** (note 1): `reduceNum_calls` 2 and `menu_discard_calls` 0, and the first trail has `drop: making Gunpowder / CylinderKey / ShotgunBullet / HandgunBullet / RemedyM for the ground` for items you put in the box, each one told to the other player as a drop.

**What I changed:**
- **Item box (note 1).** `app.Item.reduceNum` is called by the item box, crafting and using items; the real discard goes through `InventoryMenu.discardItem` (confirmed in the 9.14 file). While `discardItem` is hooked (it is) a `reduceNum` is never taken for a discard any more: nothing is put on the ground and nothing is sent. The journal says `reduceNum(Gunpowder x1) is not a discard I drop: the item box ...` and the debug file counts them (`health.items.discard.reduceNum_ignored`). If a build has no `discardItem` the old route stays, as the only one.
- **Walls (note 2).** The wait after a round that found no floor is now **3 s, then 6, 12, 24, at most 45**, and **a walk of 3 m starts the next round at once** (another spot, another floor). Those waits are in the status line. **A fourth way of casting** is tried from the second round on (`castRay` through the current scene instead of the default one); if it is the only one that sees the floor it is used (`way 4`), and if it fails it gives up quietly and never counts toward "the ray test is dead". Where the first three ways work (the main house) nothing changes and the fourth is never called.
- **The probe now also asks the floor question (note 2).** *Probe the walls here* additionally casts one ray straight down on every layer by **each of the four ways** and lists what each hit (`health.occlusion.layer_probe.floor_by_way`, and the journal line `straight down, way N: ...`). If nothing under your feet is hit by any way, the note says `NOTHING under your feet on any layer by any of the four ways`; then this area's collision is simply not reachable with these calls and the walls will need another idea.
- **The inventory's own copy is now described (notes 4 and 5).** After an add the trail has `the inventory's copy: state=..., stack=..., slot=..., object=...` and the same text is in the 0.15 s, 0.6 s and 4 s alive lines. Until now the trail only ever looked at the object the script made, never at the entry the game keeps. A state other than 2 (`INVENTRY_IN`), a slot of -1 or a stack of 0 would be the first thing to explain a crash a moment later.
- **The give writes to the trail before it takes anything.** `give: offering ...`, then, **before** `reduceItem`, `give: Bob has ChemicalS x1; taking it out of my inventory with reduceItem`, then the answer. If the game dies inside `reduceItem` that line is the last one in the next file. (The file is flushed at each line.)
- **Items the script made are not given away** unless you tick *Allow giving an item this script made* (in the item lab; not remembered). A kind counts as made when the script created the entry (not when a stack only grew); the mark goes when you hold none of that kind. The journal says why a give was refused. This is a safety net, not a fix: it also means a created Chem Fluid cannot be handed on.
- **Lab 7** takes the held ChemicalS out again with exactly the call a give makes (`Inventory.reduceItem`), with the copy described before and the line written before the call. It is the quickest way to tell whether `reduceItem` on such an item is the crash.
- **The lab steps follow the update-step box.** With *...do it inside the game's update step* ticked, Lab 1, 2, 4, 6 and 7 run in the game's update step instead of the render frame (a step that does not run within 8 s says so in the trail). One add worked and one died with nobody pressing anything: if that is the two threads meeting, the box is the cure, and a run of labs with it off and on tells.

**What I need from you next:** (1) put an item in the item box and take one out: the journal should show `reduceNum(...) is not a discard I drop`, nothing on the ground, nothing at the bot; then really discard something (drop it from the inventory): that must still drop and tell the bot. (2) In the trailer house: the status should say `trying again in 3 s`, then 6; walk a few metres and it should try again at once; press **Probe the walls here** in the save room and by the car and send the file (`floor_by_way`). (3) The item lab, a few times: Lab 1, 6, 5, 2, then wait 5 s, and **send the file whether or not the game dies**: the trail now has the copy's state. Try it both with the update-step box off and on and tell me which crashed. Lab 7 is a separate test (it may crash): if it does, the last trail line is the answer.

Internal build label is now 9.20; the public release is still **0.8**. Tests: 1373 Lua checks (up from 1325) and the C++ suite (no C++ changes). New ones cover the item box move (nothing dropped, nothing sent, counted, still a drop through `discardItem`, the old route with no hook), the wall waits (3 s first, growth, 3 m walking, a 1 m step does not count), way 4 (found, not tried in the first round, a missing scene does not kill the ray test, a later floor still works), the probe's floor test, the copy text, the trail around the give, the made-by-the-script mark (set by a gift, set by the lab, not set by a stack that only grew, dropped when none is held, refused, allowed by the box), Lab 7, and the labs in the update step. Checked by mutation: 30 deliberate breakages of the new code: 29 were caught by the tests; the 30th (resetting the wait to 3 s when the layers are calibrated again) cannot change anything, because the wait is already 3 s whenever the layers are calibrated, so that line is only a safeguard.

## What changed in v9.19 (release 0.8; same protocol v6; **script only, nothing to rebuild**: update `re7mp.lua`)

It answers your 9.17 debug file and the nine notes that came with it. **Nothing below has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock. 9.18 (the item, door and execution-test build) is unchanged apart from what is listed here, so keep testing it.

**What the file showed, and what I changed:**
- **Your player has a different name in the early game (note 7).** The debug file lists the player's own components as `[on Pl0000_Chapter1]` for the car, forest and guest-house part, and `Pl3000` (Mia) in the derelict house. The script only knew `Pl0000`-style names (`Pl2000` and `Pl3000` worked), so in the early game it found **no player at all**: the recorder said *"your player object (Pl0000) was not found"*, and everything that needs the player (health, the block/heal/crouch poses, the weapon lock, the item owner) had nothing to hook on. `Pl0000_Chapter1` (and `_Chapter` plus a number) is now a player. This is the best fit for "the guest house is treated differently"; it is a name, not different logic. **Please check it:** in the guest house the panel should show your health, and the journal should say `player object: Pl0000_Chapter1 (1 found)`.
- **The cutscenes never reached the bot (note 2).** The script saw 10 real cutscene starts (`c01f01_00`, `c01f04_01`, `c01f04_00` ...) and rejected all 10 as a "bad id". The cause: `app.GameManager.requestEvent` is a **static** method, so its first parameter (the name) is argument 2, and the hook read argument 3, which is the Transform. The hook now asks the method whether it is static, reads the name from the right slot, and tries the other slot if the text it found is not a plausible name. The debug file now says which slot it used (`health.story.id_read_from_argument`, `method_is_static`, `other_slot_used`) and lists up to 8 rejected names (`rejected_ids`) with the text that was read. The receiving side was never the problem (the bot's test cutscene arrived).
- **Walls (notes 1 and 8).** In your file the rays hit `Em3000` (an enemy, 1,006 hits), `NewZoneGroup` (1,840) and `InMainHall` (524) as walls; enemies (`Em` and four digits), `...zonegroup...` and names starting with `In` and a capital letter are no longer walls (`Interior_1F`, `InnerWall` still are). `Door` (1,612 hits) was counted as a wall in 9.17; 9.18 already ignores it.
- **Why walls work in one place and not another (notes 1 and 8): not fixed, but now measurable.** The wall test only casts on the two or three collision layers its calibration found on the floor (this time 5 and 6, earlier files 3 and 5), and a wall on any other layer is invisible to it. That fits "the guest-house doors hide the bot but the walls don't" (door sensors are on the layers it uses, the walls aren't) and "no walls by Ethan's car". It is a guess. There is a new button in *Hiding the other player*: **Probe the walls here**. It casts 8 rays of 6 m all round you on **every** layer 0 to 31 and writes down, for each layer, which names were hit and whether the script counts them as walls (`health.occlusion.layer_probe`, and the journal). **Press it standing next to a wall, next to a door and next to the car, in the main house, the guest house and outside, then send the debug file.** With that I can choose the layers per area instead of guessing. (The hit-name table also kept only the first 40 names, so outdoor names were probably never recorded; it holds 120 now.)

**Your other notes, answered and not yet built:**
- **3 (show part of them as a door closes):** possible without the door's state, by testing more points on their body (left, middle, right and four heights, instead of four in a column) and drawing only the parts that are visible. A door that swings shut covers them from one side, so that is what a wider test would see. Not built; it needs the probe results first, otherwise it would be as unreliable as the walls.
- **4 (Mia and the real character models) and the internet:** yes, I can search the web in this session (to read documentation and forum pages, not to download game files). Most of what you want is already in your game: **Mia exists as a player object (`Pl3000`)**, so cloning her model for the second player (the *Avatar* experiment) needs her real components rather than the web. I found one useful tool, **EMV Engine** (a REFramework Lua library; its readme lists a *Prefab Spawner* for RE7 among other games and a *GameObject spawner*), as something to read for how others create objects from prefabs. I did not read its code. Spawning a second Mia in the real game is untested and a crash risk, so it would be another opt-in experiment.
- **5 (guest house as a different area):** the story trace you are doing will tell. The `c01` and `c03` prefixes on the wall names suggest different scenes (outside and early game versus the main house), which also fits the layer difference.
- **6 (a dedicated chat/ping button):** not built. It needs a small separate window or a hotkey; I would rather add it once the cutscene and wall fixes are confirmed.
- **9 (forest and derelict-house logs):** read. They are what showed the `Pl0000_Chapter1` and `Pl3000` names and the static `requestEvent`; the three `requestEvent` calls in the trace (`c01f01_00`, `c01f04_01`, `c01f04_00`) all have the name as the first parameter.

**What I need from you next:** (1) in the early game: the panel should show your health, the recorder should start, and the journal should say `player object: Pl0000_Chapter1 (1 found)`; (2) cutscenes: with story sync ticked, start a cutscene with the bot connected: it should now show up as *"I started the cutscene c01f01_00 ..."* (if not, `health.story.rejected_ids` says what was read); (3) press **Probe the walls here** in the places above and send the debug file.

Internal build label is now 9.19; the public release is still **0.8**. Tests: 1325 Lua checks (up from 1295) and the C++ suite (no C++ changes). New ones cover the `Pl0000_Chapter1` name (and that `Pl0000_Hand` is not a player), the static `requestEvent` (name from argument 2, the other slot as a fallback, an instance method still read from argument 3, rejected names listed), the new wall names and their lookalikes that must still count, and the probe (layers with walls found, a sensor listed as "not a wall", a wall behind you, walls on layers the test does not use, the ray test broken). Checked by mutation: 21 deliberate breakages of the new code (the player name, the argument slot and its fallback, the rejected-name list, the new wall names and their lookalikes, every part of the probe), all 21 caught by the tests at once.

## What changed in v9.18 (release 0.8; same protocol v6; **script only, nothing to rebuild**: update `re7mp.lua` on the computer you test on)

It answers your 9.17 crash file and your three notes. **Nothing below has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock.

**1. The Lab 2 crash: the new item has a stack of 0.** Your file shows it exactly: Lab 1's object survived 10 s, but it read `state 6 (LOST), stack 0, slot -1`. Lab 2 then called `addItem`; the game answered **Integration** (it thought the item had merged into an existing stack), added nothing, the object's parent became `?`, and the game died within about 50 ms. The game's own items in your inventory have state 2 and a real count, and when the game crafts it also fills in the item's data (`setItemSaveData`) before `addItem`. So the best guess now is **adding an item that has a count of 0 is the poison** (not proven; it fits every crash file). What 9.18 does about it, with creation still **off by default** (safe mode unchanged):
- A gift of a kind you don't hold now always calls `setStackNum(n)` (before, only when n was more than 1), reads the stack back, and **does not call `addItem` if it doesn't read n**. The gift then falls back to the ground at your feet, as before.
- **Lab 6** is new: it calls `setStackNum(1)` on the waiting object and nothing else, writes a snapshot and watches the game for 4 s. **Lab 2 and Lab 3 refuse an object with a stack of 0** (the trail says "Press Lab 6 first"; the object stays waiting).
- New suggested lab order: **Lab 1, wait 5 s, Lab 6, wait, Lab 5, then Lab 2.** If Lab 6 alone crashes, setting the count is the problem; if Lab 2 crashes with a stack of 1, the next suspects are the leftover source object (the game itself hides it and creates a second item inside `addItem`), the missing item data, and the list argument. Details in the findings file.
- Snapshots now also show the item's own `ItemStackNum` field, and the debug file lists the item state names (`health.items.item_state_names`).

**2. Doors never hide the other player.** The debug files show what the wall rays actually hit: mostly **door trigger sensors** (`CloseSensor` 43,648 hits, `KnockSensor` 20,356, `PassSensor`, `PushSensor`) plus safe-room volumes, a water plane, smoke and dust effects and a few trigger zones, none of them solid. They all counted as walls, so doorways hid the other player. Now a hit does not count if its name contains `sensor`, `safezone`, `saferoomcollider`, `kinshi_zone`, `activateobject`, `dust_`, `water`, `smoke`, `Door` or `Gate` (`Outdoor_Wall` / `Indoor_Wall` still count). The debug file lists what was ignored (`health.occlusion.not_walls_hit`). **What I could not do:** "a closed door hides them, an open one doesn't". I have not read a door's open/closed state, and no door *panel* has ever appeared in a debug file (only sensors), so in 9.18 a door never hides them, open or closed. The tick box is now called "Hide them behind walls (doors never hide them)". If something door-like still hides them, `things_the_rays_hit` in the next file will name it.

**3. Execution test (new, EXPERIMENTAL): do finishers end the game by a different call than a death?** Nothing like it has ever been recorded: no debug file so far contains a real death or finisher. Panel, *When a player dies*, two boxes, **both off at every start and not saved**:
- **Log the calls a grab / finisher / game over makes.** Hooks up to 24 methods of the grab classes the game has (`Em3000Grapple`, `PlayerGrappleEm3000`, `Em3100Grapple`, `Em4000Grapple`, `Em2000Grapple`, `PlayerGrappleEm2000`) and the whole game-over screen class, never getters or per-frame updates, and records each call, each game-over call the script already watches (`setGameOverFlag(true)`, `doDie`, the game switching to its Gameover mode) **with your health at that moment**, and the 20 calls before the first game over. The panel and `health.execution_test.verdict` then say, for example, *"the first game over (...) came 2.0 s after app.Em3000Grapple.startGrapple, with health 600/1000 (not 0: holding health at 1 cannot stop this kind of death)"*, or *"... with no grab or finisher call before it: an ordinary death"*. Turn it on, get grabbed and killed (Jack's grab is the easy one), restart, send the debug file from that session.
- **...and block the game over and go down instead.** Needs Downed mode, the other player connected and not down. It **skips** `setGameOverFlag(true)` / `doDie`, sets your health to 1 and puts you down as after a hit. At most **3 blocks a minute** (a game that keeps asking can still end), and the follow-on calls of a blocked game over (the pause flag, the switch to the Gameover mode) are not sent to the other player as a death. **What the game does after a skipped game over is unknown. The grab animation may carry on and leave you stuck**, which is exactly what this finds out. Use it with the loopback bot first, on a save you don't mind losing.
- With both boxes off nothing is hooked and no game-over call is ever skipped.

**Not changed (still open):** the bot reviving you when you are down, the "keep every version" co-op option, Jack frozen after the dinner cutscene, the heal animation id, door and event replay, the co-op save data issue, the F8 revive and the real `requestEvent` hook still waiting for a real-game run, and the exe writing `re7mp_in.json` in place (a rare "unexpected end of input").

**What I need from you next (loopback is enough):** (1) safe mode as before; (2) doors: walk past a few doors with the other player on the far side, tick or untick the wall box, and tell me if anything still hides them (send the debug file); (3) if you take the risk: creation box, then Lab 1, Lab 6, Lab 5, Lab 2, **send the debug file after any crash from the next start** (the trail names the last step); (4) the execution test: log box first with a grab, then the block box once the log looks sensible.

Internal build label is now 9.18; the public release is still **0.8**. Tests: 1295 Lua checks (up from 1184) and the C++ suite (no C++ changes). New ones cover the stack handling (gift always sets and verifies the stack, refuses an item that won't take it; Lab 6; Lab 2/3 refusing a stack of 0; a mock that behaves like the real game, with a new object at stack 0 and `addItem` answering Integration), every wall name from your debug files and the lookalike names that must still count, the debug lists, and the execution test (off at start, hooks only after ticking, getters and updates not hooked, verdicts with and without a grab, the first game over kept, the 40-call and 60-event limits, blocking only for the two game-over calls, only with Downed mode and a connected partner, the 3-a-minute limit, health at 1 immediately, follow-on calls not sent as a death). Checked by mutation: 62 deliberate breakages of the new code; 53 were caught at once, 7 got new tests and are caught now (an unreadable hit name, blocking every call, the health write at the moment of a block, the first game over being overwritten, the 60-event limit, recording while the test is off, the blocked call being listed), and 2 cannot change behaviour (no other hook returns a value, and settings are written from a fixed list of keys) and stay uncaught on purpose. Writing those tests found one real flaw in my own design: after a blocked game over the game's follow-on calls (the pause flag, the switch to the Gameover mode) were still sent to the other player as a death; they are now recorded but not sent, for 5 s after a block, and only while the block box is ticked.

## What changed in v9.17 (release 0.8; same protocol v6; **script only, nothing to rebuild**: update `re7mp.lua` on the computer you test on)

It answers your two 9.16 files. **What they showed:** safe mode works (the Herb and ChemicalS gifts were refused and the sender keeps them, bullets and a Herb went onto stacks you already hold), and **creating an item still crashes the game, in the render frame and in the update step.** So the update-step box does **not** help; the panel now says so. What I could not read from the files is *which step* dies: both files end before the attempt (the journal is written to disk every 2 s and the game dies about 0.1 s after the add), so there is no line about it. **I still do not know the cause.** This build is about getting that, not about a fix. **Nothing below has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock.

**Safe mode stays the default**, as you asked: creation and the update-step box are off at every start and are not saved. Nothing in this build runs unless you tick the creation box.

**1. A black box for item steps (`re7mp_trail.json`).** Every step of anything that creates, adds or places an item (making the object, reading it, `isAddItem`, making a list, each `addItem` form, the answer, what you hold afterwards, the drop's placing) is written **at once** to its own small file in `reframework\data`, the last 80 steps, and the debug file is rewritten at the same moment (it normally waits up to 2 s). Each line also goes to the journal as `trail: ...`. After a crash, **start the game again and send the debug file from that start**: its first journal line is `previous session's item trail: N steps, the last at T s: <the last step>`, and the whole old trail is in `health.items.previous_item_trail`. (The old crumb file only said which step was running; the trail also says what each step answered.) In safe mode nothing is written to it.

**2. "Alive" lines.** After every add, drop or lab step the script writes `... alive 0.05 s later`, then 0.15, 0.3, 0.6, 1, 2 and 4 s, with what you hold. The crash crumb says `done, watching whether the game survives the next seconds` until the 4 s are over. So if the game dies, the last `alive` line dates the crash to within a fraction of a second, and tells us whether it is an instant failure or a delayed one.

**3. The add itself changed in three ways** (all from your 9.15 log, where only the 2-parameter `addItem` ran and answered `Integration` before the crash):
- The 4-parameter form `addItem(item, player, false, list)` is now called with its **full signature**, so the game picks the right overload (REFramework refuses a call with the wrong number of arguments and answers 0, which the old code read as "Put"). It is tried **first when a `List<GameObject>` can be made**. In your game `create_instance` for that list type answered nothing, so there are now three ways to make it (`sdk.create_instance`, the type's own `create_instance`, `create_instance(type, true)`); the trail and `health.items.list_made_with` say which worked, or that none did (then only the 2-parameter form runs).
- **Once the game has answered, no other form is tried on the same object.** The old code tried the next form after an `Integration` answer; if the first call had already used the object up, a second add is exactly the kind of thing that could be the crash.
- A snapshot of the new object (state, stack, id, slot, flags, its GameObject) is written before and after the add.

**4. The ITEM LAB** (panel, *Items: give and drop*, only visible when the creation box is ticked). It runs the same steps **one button at a time**, so the crash can be pinned to one of them. Use it with a kind you hold none of (it is `ChemicalS`):
- **Lab 1** makes the object (`createItemInstance`) and does nothing else.
- **Lab 2** adds the waiting object with `addItem(item, player)`; **Lab 3** adds it with the 4-parameter form and a real list. **An object can be added once only** (a second press says so): press Lab 1 again for a fresh one.
- **Lab 4** removes it again with `Item.destroyItem` (an untested call; it is a separate step on purpose). **Lab 5** only writes down what it looks like and what you hold.
- Press one, **wait about 5 s** (the `alive` lines run), look at the line under the buttons, press the next. **If the game dies, the step that was last in the trail is the culprit** (or the one just before it, if the last `alive` line is old).
- Suggested order: Lab 1, wait, Lab 5, then Lab 2, wait, then Lab 5. If Lab 1 alone kills the game, making the object is the poison and nothing after it matters; if Lab 1 survives and Lab 2 kills it, it is the add. Try Lab 3 on a fresh Lab 1 object afterwards (not in the same run as a crash).

**5. Found while testing the lab:** the first version of the 4-parameter lab button wrote its steps as "lab 4" in the trail. Fixed before it left my machine; mentioned because the trail numbers now always match the button names.

**Not changed (still open):** the bot reviving you when you are down, the "keep every version" co-op option, Jack frozen after the dinner cutscene, finishers, the heal animation id, door and event replay, the co-op save data issue, the F8 revive and the real `requestEvent` hook still waiting for a real-game run, and the exe writing `re7mp_in.json` in place (a rare "unexpected end of input" if it is read half-written).

**What I need from you next (loopback is enough):** (1) safe mode as before (nothing should have changed); (2) if you want to take the risk: tick the creation box, then use the lab, one step at a time as above, and **send the debug file after every crash** (from the next start) plus REFramework's own `re2_framework_log.txt` if you can (it keeps every `[re7mp]` line, including the ones the debug file loses); (3) if nothing crashes in the lab, also press *Test: receive 1 ChemicalS* and tell me how it differs.

Internal build label is now 9.17; the public release is still **0.8**. Tests: 1184 Lua checks (up from 1113) and the C++ suite. The new ones cover the trail file and the debug file being written at the moment of each step (also from a button press, outside the frame), the 80-line and 400-character limits, one-line text, the previous-session report (and not reporting your own session, an emptied trail, a trail with no steps, or a damaged file), the three ways of making a list and the no-list path, the "alive" lines and the crumb that is cleared only when the 4 s are over, the drop path, every lab button and its guards, one add per object, and the lab being hidden in safe mode. The new code was checked by mutation: 50 deliberate breakages, 45 caught at once; the other five (the 400-character cut, the debug file being rewritten inside the step itself, an old trail with no steps being reported, the debug file calling a used lab object "waiting", and Lab 5 not describing the waiting object) got new tests and are caught now. Writing the lab tests also found one real bug in my own code (the Lab 3 button wrote "lab 4" in the trail); fixed.

## What changed in v9.16 (release 0.8; same protocol v6; **rebuild the exe AND update the script on BOTH computers**: the exe's release number changed, and the script has to carry the same one)

You released v0.7, so this build is **v0.8**. It answers your 9.15 file: **receiving the loopback bot's Herb crashed the game**, and you said the story/cutscene idea may go in as long as it is switchable and can become the default once it works. **Nothing below has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock. Where something is a guess, it says so.

**1. The Herb crash: what I can and cannot say.** Your 9.15 file has no data from the receive: the session ends before any of it. I did not find a cause, so I did not pretend to fix it. What I know from all your files: the only receive path that has ever worked in the game is **making a stack you already hold bigger** (that is how you got the bullets). **Creating an item the script does not find in your inventory has never been shown to work, and every session where creation returned an object crashed afterwards.** A Herb is created because you had just used your last one. So:
- **Safe mode is now the default.** A gift of a kind you hold is added to your stack (the way that worked). A gift of a kind you hold **none** of is *refused*: nothing is created, nothing crashes, the sender **keeps** the item and is told why. The same for drops on the ground and your own discard (the game's normal discard stands; nothing is put on the ground). The panel (*Items: give and drop*) says `Safe mode: a gift can only make a stack you already hold bigger...` and counts `refused in safe mode`.
- **Experimental creation** is a tick box in the same panel: *EXPERIMENTAL: let the script CREATE items*. It is off every time the game starts and is not saved. When on, the order is changed to the game's own: first the stack, then `addItem(item, player, false, new empty list)` (the shape in your trace of the game's own call), then `addItem(item, player)`. The old calls with a missing list are gone. A second box, *...and do it inside the game's update step*, runs the creation in `UpdateBehavior` instead of the render frame. I added it because **all script work runs in the render frame**, and creating game objects from there is my best guess for the crash (a guess, not a finding). If creation crashes with the first box, try the second.
- **New crumbs**: a note is written before each creation step, so **if the game crashes again, send me the debug file from the NEXT start**: its first `previous session:` line names the exact step.
- **Try it with the new button** *Test: receive 1 ChemicalS*: you hold no Chem Fluid, so with safe mode on it is refused (check), with the box ticked it tries to create one. The Herb and bullet buttons stay: with a Herb in the inventory the Herb button just grows the stack.
- A stale crumb `hooking get_isMoveAccepted` at the start of your file: I could not reproduce it. The burst limit no longer applies to the hook and clean notes, and one more clean note is written at the end of the hook install. The first line of your next file will show whether it is gone.

**2. Story sync (EXPERIMENTAL; two switches, both off at the start, not saved).** Your idea: when the host starts a cutscene, move the joiner to the host. I built it in layers, because a wrong teleport into a room that is not loaded could drop you through the floor:
- Panel node *Story sync (EXPERIMENTAL)*. Box 1 *Story sync*: hooks `app.GameManager.requestEvent` (**only when you tick it**; nothing is hooked while it is off) and tells the other player the cutscene's id (`c03e03_00`) and where you were and where the cutscene is. New message kind 12 (an older script ignores it; the protocol stays v6). It shows `Bob started the cutscene c03e03_00` in the journal and chat. Nothing else.
- Box 2 *JOINER: move me to the host's place*: only on the joiner, only when box 1 is on. It moves you to the host's position (a hand's width above the floor) when they start a cutscene, **unless** you are the host, you are down, a game menu is open, you were moved in the last 15 s, you are already within 3 m, or the place is nonsense (further than 2000 m). **1.5 s after a move it checks where you are**: if you fell more than 4 m below the place (area not loaded) you are put back where you were, and it says so.
- It does **not** play the cutscene on your game. I do not know what the other game does when a cutscene is started by a script (the area may not be loaded, the actors may not exist). Moving you next to the host is the safe half; replaying `requestEvent` comes only after the journal shows what happens (`recent_events` and `last_settle` in the file).
- Limits: a repeat of the same id within 5 s is ignored, at most 8 messages a minute are sent, ids longer than 40 characters or with odd characters are dropped.
- **To make it the default later**: three constants near the top of the script, `ITEM_CREATE_DEFAULT`, `STORY_SYNC_DEFAULT`, `STORY_TELEPORT_DEFAULT` (all `false`). Flip one when the journal says it works.
- **Test with the bot**: tick box 1 (and box 2 to see the move), press *Test: pretend Loopback Bot started the cutscene c03e03_00 where they stand*; you are moved next to the bot (the test is allowed to move the host too), and 1.5 s later the file records where you ended up.

**3. Release 0.7 -> 0.8.** `RELEASE = "0.8"` in the script and `kReleaseVersion = "0.8"` in `src/version.hpp`; the tests check they agree. The exe window, the panel, the updater's user agent (`re7mp/0.8`) and the version check use it. Because the number is in the exe, **rebuild the exe and update the script on both computers**, or the check will tell you the releases differ.

**Not changed (still open):** the bot reviving you when you are down, the "keep every version" co-op option, Jack frozen after the dinner cutscene, finishers, the heal animation id, door and event replay, the co-op save data issue, and the F8 revive still waiting for a real-game run.

**What I need from you next (loopback is enough):** (1) restart, then press the Herb, bullet and ChemicalS buttons in safe mode (they should all be refused or grow a stack, with no crash); (2) if you want to take the risk, tick the creation box and press *Test: receive 1 ChemicalS*; if the game crashes, start it again and send the debug file from that start; (3) with the bot: tick the story boxes and press the story test button; (4) send `re7mp_debug.json`. In it: `health.items.creation`, `last_add`, `health.story` (seen / sent / received / moved / put back, `last_settle`, `recent_events`), and the first `previous session:` line.

Internal build label is now 9.16; the public release is **0.8**. Tests: 1110 Lua checks (safe mode in each path, the stack-first order, the new addItem forms and the missing-list case, the update-step mode and its 8 s give-up, the crumbs and the burst limit, the whole story module: switches, rate limits, bad ids, repeats, the host never moved, the cooldown, the menu and downed cases, the fall and put-back) and the C++ suite. The new Lua code was checked by mutation 41 deliberate breakages for the new code: 38 were caught at once; the other three (an open game menu not stopping the joiner's move, a crumb limit that swallowed the "clean" note, and the story hook sending from inside the game's own call) got new or stronger tests, one of which showed that my old burst test never made a burst, and all 41 are caught now. Two more ideas (the extra final "clean" note after the hook install, the cap of 8 queued cutscene starts) cannot change anything the mock can see and were not run.


## What changed in v9.15 (release 0.7; same protocol v6; **script only, nothing to rebuild**)

This build answers one report: **the game crashed when the F8 revive reached 3 seconds** (your 9.14 file ends 3 s after `reviving Loopback Bot: stay within 3 m for 3 s (the RemedyM is used when it is done)`), plus your decision that **F8 should cost no item, the time is the penalty**. Walking away mid-revive cancelling it worked. **Unverified in the game**, as before.

**F8 now uses no item and touches nothing in the game.** Press F8 (or the panel button) next to a downed player and stay within 3 m for 3 s (slider, 1 to 10 s). It checks only the two positions; at the end it tells the other player and that is all. The inventory is never read or changed, so a revive works with an empty inventory. It stops for free when they get up, you walk out of range, you go down yourself or you press Cancel (holding the key down starts one countdown, not one every 3 s). **Using a Herb / Chem Fluid / Remedy from the inventory menu is still immediate and costs the item**, so the two routes now differ the way you wanted: time versus a heal.

**About the crash.** The only game call at the 3-second mark in 9.14 was `Inventory.reduceItem("RemedyM", 1, true)` (your inventory had no Herb or Chem Fluid, so it picked the Remedy). That call is the same one that gifts use, which has worked in the game, so I can't prove it was the cause; what I know is that it was the only thing that happened at that moment and it is gone now. If the game still crashes at the end of an F8 revive, the cause is somewhere else: a crumb is written just before the final step (`revive: finishing the F8 revive of ...`) and the next start says **`previous session: The last session ended while the script was finishing an F8 revive`** if it died there. Tell me what that line says.

**What the revived player sees.** `Bob helped you up 1.1 m away` in the panel and the journal (instead of `Bob used a Herb`); the message carries no item name (`e|revive||x|y|z`; an older script on the other side would say "used a healing item", still getting you up). A heal event from the inventory route is unchanged. The same distance rule applies: 3 m, plus 25% slack for lag.

**Not changed (still open):** everything from 9.14 that I could not test (the three `addItem` forms, the dropped item's visibility, the item manager adoption) because the crash ended the session first.

**What I need from you next (loopback is enough):** (1) F8 on the downed bot, staying put, and once walking away halfway; (2) the receive buttons in *Items: give and drop* (Herb, HandgunBullet), then (3) a discard, then send `re7mp_debug.json` (`health.items.last_add`, `last_drop`, the `drop:` / `add:` lines). If the game crashes, send the file from the next start.

Internal build label is now 9.15; the release stays **0.7**. Tests: 1002 Lua checks (F8 never touches the inventory, works with an empty inventory, the receiving side's "helped you up", the slack, the new crumb and its report on the next start); the new code was checked by mutation (9 new breakages, all caught). One C++ test (`test_save_loopback_echo`) failed about half the time on a slower machine: it saved 1.5 s after start but the loopback bot only appears after about 2 s, and a save made before the other player is there is by design not sent. The test now waits for the bot; the exe itself is unchanged.

## What changed in v9.14 (release 0.7; same protocol v6; **update the script; rebuild the exe only if you want the bot's new reach**: the exe change is just the loopback bot's revive distance)

This build answers your 9.13 notes and file: revive works but should be 3 m and F8 should not be instant; **a dropped item did not show up for the person who dropped it** (the other side saw it); **the game crashed while I tried to give you a Herb and bullets, and nothing arrived** (an earlier version at least delivered the bullets). I read your 9.13 debug file first. **Nothing below has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock. Where a fix is a guess it says so, and the next file will show whether it was right.

**1. Revive distance is 3 m (was 4).** Both for using a Herb from the inventory next to someone and for F8. The panel slider "Revive distance (m)" still goes from 1 to 10. A saved distance of exactly 4 (the old default) is not kept, so you get the new 3; if you had picked another number it is kept. The player who is down still accepts a heal from up to 25% further than the distance (3.75 m) to allow for lag; the loopback bot uses the same 3.75 m.

**2. F8 is a countdown, not an instant revive.** *(9.15: it also uses no item any more, see above; the next sentences describe 9.14.)* Press F8 (or the panel button) next to a downed player: you must stay within the distance for **3 seconds** (slider "F8 revive: seconds to stay within that distance", 1 to 10 s) and only then is the Herb / Chem Fluid taken from your inventory and the other player told. The screen shows `Reviving Bob... 1.2 / 3 s`; the panel shows the same with a **Cancel the revive** button. It stops at no cost if they get up on their own, you walk out of range, you go down yourself, the Herb is gone (checked twice a second), or you press Cancel. Pressing F8 again while it counts does nothing, and holding the key down starts one countdown, not a new one every 3 s. **Using a Herb from the inventory menu on someone is still immediate**, as you wanted: that is the costlier, riskier way (you open the menu next to them), F8 is the safe one that now takes time. The debug file's `health.revive` has `radius`, `hold`, `started`, `done`, `cancelled`.

**3. Items: what the 9.13 file showed, and what I changed.** Unverified in the game.
- **A copy of the item manager that knows nothing replaced the good one.** At the end of your file the scene component and the singleton both know the items, but the copy *in use* was "the one the game's own `createItemInstance` call used", which answers nothing for `findItemData`. It took over as soon as the game created an item, and the third gift (HandgunBullet x5 at 305.8 s) failed with "createItemInstance gave nothing". Now a copy handed over by the game is only used if it knows items, and a copy that works is never replaced.
- **`addItem` was called wrongly and its answer ignored.** It is `app.Inventory.addItem(Item, GameObject, Boolean, List)` and it answers an `app.Inventory.AddItemResult`; the old call passed no list and said "done" whatever came back, so an item could be "delivered" without ever being in the inventory: your file says `gave you HandgunBullet x5 (in the inventory)` and `Herb x1 (in the inventory)`, and you saw neither. Now three forms are tried in turn (the 2-parameter one, the 4-parameter one without a list, the 4-parameter one with a new empty list), what each answered is written down, and **the gift only counts as delivered when the inventory really holds more of that item than before**. If none works the item is put on the ground instead and the sender is told so.
- **The crash.** I cannot see the crash itself, so this is a suspect: the creation calls were also being tried with the object an inventory entry carries as "owner" (that is an item's own object, `sm1852_AntisepticSolutionMedium_Item` in the file), which is not something the game's creation code expects. Only the player object is passed now. And a crumb (`re7mp_lastcall.json`) is written before every item call, so **if the game crashes again, the next start says which call it was** (`previous session: The last session ended while the script was handling an item: ...`). A stale crumb from an old hook install was also blamed for no reason in your file; those are cleared now.
- **The dropped item that nobody could see.** The game makes a dropped item at its *owner* (a drawer, in its own use); here the owner is you, so the item was probably created inside you, or as a child of you, or hidden. Now the object is let go of the player, put 0.8 m in front of you (just above the floor), switched to drawn and updating, and read back; **if it is still hidden afterwards the drop is reported as failed instead of "ok"**. The journal gets a `drop:` line saying what the object was and what it became, and `health.items.last_drop` keeps it. This applies to your own discard and to what you receive.

**Confirmed by your file:** the Discard button runs `app.InventoryMenu.discardItem` (`menu_discard_calls: 1`, `reduceNum_calls: 0`), the hook saw it and queued the drop (`discard: HandgunBullet x20 will be put on the ground`), and the item manager in use at that moment knew the items (`created` and reported dropped at 208.1 s). So detection and creation worked; what was wrong was where the object ended up (see the last bullet above).

**Not changed (still open):** finishers; Jack frozen on the host's screen after the dinner cutscene; the heal animation id; the "keep every version" co-op option; the co-op save data issue (waiting for a file with two real players); door/event replay.

**What I need from you next (loopback is enough):** (1) in *Items: give and drop* use **Test: receive 1 Herb** and **Test: receive 5 HandgunBullet** (and give the bot something from the inventory list); (2) discard an item and say whether it appears on the ground for you; (3) make the bot go down, stand within 3 m, press F8 and stay put; try walking away halfway; (4) send `re7mp_debug.json`. In it I'm looking for `health.items.last_add` (which form of `addItem` worked, and what each answered), `add_item_result_names`, `last_drop` and the `drop:` / `add:` journal lines, and `health.revive`. **If the game crashes, send me the debug file from the next start** (it has the "previous session" line).

Internal build label is now 9.14; the public release number stays **0.7**. Tests: 996 Lua checks (55 new: item manager adoption, the three `addItem` forms and the landing check, the drop's parent / visibility / placement, the crumbs, the 3 m distance, the countdown and each way it stops, the saved settings) and the C++ suite (the bot's 3.75 m reach). The new Lua code was checked by mutation (50 deliberate breakages; the 16 that first went unnoticed got their own tests, and all are caught now).

## What changed in v9.13 (release 0.7; same protocol v6; **rebuild the exe AND update the script**: the exe's loopback bot changed, the script changed for everything else. For two real players only the script matters; the exe change is only the bot)

This build answers your notes from the 9.12 file: going down works but you want the status shown, the loopback bot did not confirm gifts, you want to test a revive alone, and **discard still deletes the item instead of dropping it**. I only have your debug file and the loopback bot to go on, so every item below says what is **unverified in the real game**.

**1. Going down: the lock works, nothing to change.** Your file: the gun refusals fired 2946 times, the position pin put you back 55 times, the game re-opens the gates inside `updateActionTrack` (the hook there is what closes them). The knife still works while you are down because it is not one of the gun's calls; I kept it that way on purpose, since your partner can defend you and clear the way for a revive. I did remove two hooks that did nothing and were very hot (`update` and `lateUpdate` on the sequence manager: 2.4 million and 68 thousand calls in one session, all muted); the lock keeps the 8 useful sequence-manager hooks, the 3 gun hooks and the move getter.

**2. `[downed]` after the name.** `Bob [downed]` (red) over the other player's head, the same place and style as `[crouching]`, `[healing]` and `[blocking]`. The pose labels are not drawn while they are down. (The panel's Health section still says `DOWN` next to their health numbers.)

**3. The loopback bot answers gifts and drops, and can go down.** `re7mp.exe` (C++): the bot now answers a gift with the confirmation your script waits for (`Loopback Bot has Herb x1 (the loopback bot took it)`), so the item now leaves your inventory when you give it to the bot; a drop is answered too. A new message kind, 11, tells the bot to go down or get up (only the bot understands it; a real player's script ignores it). In the panel (Health and downed, only when the other player is the Loopback Bot): **Loopback bot: make it go down** and **make it get up**. While down the bot stays where it fell, shows `[downed]` and says so every second. To revive it: walk within 4 m and press **F8** (takes a Herb / Chem Fluid from your inventory) or use a Herb in the inventory next to it (any heal event within 5 m gets it up). The bot says it got up when it does. A downed bot does not move or do poses.

**4. Discard: what I found and what I changed. Unverified in the game.** I could not tell from the 9.12 file why discard only deleted: the journal had no discard line at all. Two things stood out, and 9.13 fixes both plus makes the next file explain itself:
- **The item manager the script used was an empty shell.** `findItemData("Herb")`, the name list and every create call answered nil, which is why *nothing the script creates ever appears* (gifts you receive, drops on discard, drops from the other player) while "grow the stack you already have" works. The real manager is a component on the object `SystemObject` (older traces show it called there). The script now collects every copy it can find (components in the scene, the one on `SystemObject`, the singleton, the one the game's own creation call used), asks each whether it knows an item, uses the first that does, and tries again later (15 s, 30 s ... up to 2 min) while none does. `health.items.item_manager` says which copy is in use and whether any knew an item; the journal says `item manager: using ...` once; the probe lists every copy.
- **Discard is also hooked at the menu.** `app.InventoryMenu.discardItem(item) -> Boolean` is in the game's method list: it carries the item with the call and says whether the discard happened, so it needs no "highlighted item". It is hooked as well as `reduceNum`, and the two agree (one drop per discard). Every discard-like call writes a `discard:` journal line (25 at most): what it saw and why it was or wasn't treated as a discard. `health.items.discard` counts the calls.
If discard still deletes, the next file will say which part failed: no `discard:` lines and `menu_discard_calls: 0` means the Discard button runs neither call; a line saying it will be put on the ground followed by a failed creation means the item manager still can't create objects.

**5. Smaller fixes from the file.** `app.Inventory.getExtendLevelState` is a static call in this game, so the hook that remembered the inventory from it threw on every call and flooded the log; it is guarded now. Any hook that fails logs at most 3 errors.

**Not changed (still open):** finishers; Jack frozen on the host's screen after the dinner cutscene; the heal animation id; the "keep every version" co-op option; the co-op save data issue (waiting for a file with two real players); button 9; door/event replay.

**What I need from you next (loopback is enough):** (1) give the bot an item from the inventory list: the item should leave your inventory and the log should say the bot took it; (2) press **make it go down**, check the `[downed]` tag, stand within 4 m and press F8 (or use a Herb), and check it gets up; (3) discard an item in the inventory, then send `re7mp_debug.json`: I'm looking for the `discard:` journal lines, `health.items.discard` and `health.items.item_manager`; (4) say whether a dropped item appeared on the ground this time.

Internal build label is now 9.13; the public release number stays **0.7**. Tests: 941 Lua checks (44 new: the item manager choice, the SystemObject and late-manager searches, discard before and after, the journal caps, the bot buttons, the bot's exact messages and the `[downed]` label) and the C++ suite (the bot's gift and drop answers, going down, the frozen position, the health report, the far and near heal). The new Lua code was also checked by mutation (deliberately breaking it and seeing a test fail).

## What changed in v9.12 (release 0.7; same protocol v6; **rebuild the exe AND update the script on BOTH computers**: the exe's save code changed this time)

This build answers your 0.6 notes ("going down still lets you move and shoot", "saves are sent and labelled correctly but don't contain the right save data"), your screenshot of the false version warning, and your 0.7 notes ("receiving via dropping and going to the inventory still doesn't work", `createIteminstance failed: nil`, "firing the gun 3 times ... doesn't find anything for the gun"). I read both debug files (builds 9.9 and 9.11) first; what they showed is in `re7-findings.md`. **None of this has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock. Where a fix is a guess, it says so, and it writes down what it did so the next file can tell us.

**1. The false "version is mismatched" warning.** Not caused by changing the number halfway through. The script reads `re7mp_in.json` to learn the exe's release, but the exe only writes that file while the other player's packets arrive, so the file left behind by an older exe was read as "the exe you are running now". The warning was also latched, so it never went away. Now the exe's release only counts after the file has been seen to **change**; the warning clears as soon as the exe reports the matching release; and the first version message to the other player waits (at most 2 s) until the exe's release is known, so it no longer goes out with `?`.

**2. Going down: the lock is rebuilt around what your 0.6 file showed.** The file: `app.PlayerSequenceManager.IsAimAccepted` and `IsAttackAccepted` were open again at `post:UpdateBehavior` in 706 of 5241 frames and nowhere else, i.e. **the game re-opens them inside its own update**, after my once-a-frame write. The type has no method called `update`, but it has `updateActionTrack`, `updateMovementTrack`, `updateDeadTrack`, `updateJogTrack`, `updateTurnTrack`, `updateCodexTrack`, `updateLaserSightTrack`, `onMotionUpdated`, and getters such as `get_isMoveAccepted`. Now, when you go down:
- the two gates are closed again **right after each of those update methods** (hooked the first time you go down, not at start-up), as well as at the frame callbacks as before;
- **the gun's own `tryAttack`, `tryAimStart` and `trySemiAimStart` are refused** (skipped, and the answer is "no") while you are down;
- **`get_isMoveAccepted` says no** while you are down, and if the game moves you anyway you are **put back where you fell** (sideways only; a jump of more than 4 m is the game moving you, e.g. a cutscene, and becomes the new place);
- a hook that fires more than 6000 times a second is muted (so a wrong guess about which method is hot cannot cost frames), and every part has its own tick box in the panel and its own line saying what it did.
The debug file's `health.weapon_lock` now has `hooks` (installed, not found, fired while down, muted), `gun` (installed, calls refused), `move` (how often the move gate was asked, how often it said no, how often you were put back and at which point in the frame) and the existing per-place leak counts. **If something still works while down, this tells me where.** What I cannot know: whether the game reads the gates through these exact methods (it may inline them), whether the position pin fires at a point after the game moved you, and whether skipping a gun call leaves the gun in a bad state. If any of the three misbehaves, untick that one box.

**3. Gifts and drops.** The 0.7 file: `createItemInstance` and `createDropItemInstance` both **returned nil without an error**, and 9.11 treated "no error" as "it worked", which is why it said "dropped at your feet" when nothing appeared. Now:
- **only an object that really came back counts.** nil is a failure with a reason, and the answer to the sender says so;
- the item is created with each plausible owner (the player object, then the owner an inventory entry carries) and each plausible name form (plain, managed string); the first that returns an object wins and `health.items.create_worked_with` says which;
- if creation still fails, the gift **grows a stack you already have** (`getStackNum` / `getMaxStackNum` / `setStackNum`, only if the whole amount fits, and read back to check). `Herb` into a Herb stack works that way; a new kind of item cannot;
- when creation fails the script writes a **probe** into the journal (once a minute): what `findItemData` returns for the name, its fields, whether the game lists the name in `get_ItemDataIDNames`, and a sample of the names. That is the data I need if the owner/name variants are not the answer;
- **a gift now leaves your inventory only after the other game says "ok".** Before this, the item was taken at once and a failure handed it back (if it could); now nothing is taken until they confirm. If they cannot take it, you keep it. If nobody answers in 10 s, it stays with you. A gift is never lost; the worst case is that you hear "no" and keep it.
- the panel and `health.items` count what was sent, received, dropped, bumped and failed.
Both players need 9.12 for the confirmation: a 9.11 receiver does answer, so it works across 9.11 and 9.12; a 9.10 receiver does not answer, so you keep the item and are told after 10 s (note that their older script may have added it as well).

**4. Shots from the gun's own call.** The 0.7 file: the shot learner watched 15 s and found no number that dropped by one per shot, so the damage-to-host's-enemies feature had no signal. The type is `app.PlayerGun`, and it has `shoot` and `shootStart`. Now the joiner hooks those two (pre-hook only notes the time; the ray test runs in the next frame) and one call counts as one shot. The learner also counts those calls and says, e.g., `the gun's own shoot call ran 3 times while you fired: shots are taken from it`. It is on by default for a joiner in enemy mode; a panel tick box turns it off, and the panel line shows `shoot calls seen: N, used as shots: M`. `enemy_hits.gun_hook` in the file has the same. If `shoot` fires for something that is not a shot (a dry fire), you will see calls > shots; send the file.

**5. Co-op saves: I do not know yet, so the build now records everything.** In 9.11 I wrote that the behaviour you saw was "as built". That explained which slot a save goes into, not why the data is wrong, and **I withdraw it as an answer**. Your report: Andre saves in slot 10, where you have a save of your own; the label and his name appear, but the data in the slot does not change and no new slot has the data. By design the receiver puts that save in the lowest free slot instead (it never overwrites a save of your own). Possible causes I can see from here, none confirmed: the label was attached to the wrong row (your own slot 10), the game does not list a file that was written while it was running, the exe was watching a different Steam account's folder, Steam Cloud put the old file back, or the slot is beyond what the Load screen shows. What the build does about it:
- after writing a received save the exe **reads the file back and checks its size and checksum**; if it does not match, the save is **not** marked CO-OP and the message says so;
- every 5 s it checks the received copies again; if something wrote the file since (the game, Steam Cloud), it says `Slot N no longer holds the save that was received` and drops the label;
- each received entry records **why it went where it went**: `same slot number as on Bob's PC (it was free here)`, `it replaced the earlier copy from Bob in that slot`, `slot 10 here holds a save of your own, so it went into the lowest free slot`. The panel's Co-op saves list shows `Bob's slot 10 -> your slot 3: why`;
- the script asks the game itself (`SaveDataManager.isExistData`) whether it lists that slot, and the panel says either `the game lists this slot: Load Game shows it as slot N` or `THE GAME DOES NOT LIST THIS SLOT`. That line is the most important one: if it says "does not list", the file is there but the game does not see it (restart the game, or Steam Cloud is interfering);
- a save **you made and sent** is labelled `[CO-OP: sent to Bob]`, never with his name as if it were his;
- `health.coop_saves` in the debug file has all of it (folder, entries with from-slot and why, whether the game lists each slot, label status).
**What I need:** have Andre save once while you are connected; then open the panel's Co-op saves section and tell me what it says for that save (the slot numbers, the why, and whether the game lists it), look at the Load screen, and send the debug file and `re7mp.exe`'s log.

**6. The journal no longer fills with method lists.** The 0.7 file's journal was almost all indented lines of the method dump; the journal now leaves out any line that starts with a space (the debug file's `lines` still has them).

**Not changed (still open):** finishers; Jack frozen on the host's screen after the dinner cutscene; the heal animation id; the "keep every version" co-op option; hands 16/17 as an inventory pose; button 9; door/event replay.

**What I need from you next:** send the debug file right after (1) going down and trying to move, aim and fire, (2) a give of a Herb when the receiver has a Herb stack, and one of an item they do not have, (3) a discard-drop, (4) firing the gun three times as a joiner in enemy mode, (5) a co-op save made by the other player. The sections to look at: `health.weapon_lock` (`hooks`, `gun`, `move`), `health.items`, `enemy_hits.gun_hook`, `health.coop_saves`, and the journal lines starting `give probe` and `weapon lock:`.

Internal build label is now 9.12; the public release number stays **0.7**. Tests: 897 Lua checks (108 new: the stale exe file, the warning clearing, the version message waiting for the exe; the lock hooks installing only after going down and only for the boxes ticked, the gates closing after each update method, the gun calls refused and answered "no", the move gate, the position pin and its tolerance, a teleport as the new place, the hot-hook mute and every per-place count in the file; the two-phase gift (nothing taken before "ok", taken after, kept after "fail", kept after the 10 s timeout, the guard against the health module seeing the removal); creation variants, nil as a failure, the stack fallback with its full-stack and read-back cases, the probe lines and their rate limit; the shoot/shootStart hook for a joiner and not for the host, the learner counting hook shots; the journal leaving out indented lines; the sent tag, the game-lists-this-slot lines and the `coop_saves` block) and the C++ suite (new: provenance and `why` kept in the manifest, tags for received/sent saves, the read-back check, the re-check that drops a label when the file changed, and an end-to-end case with a slot held by a save of your own).  The mutation file now has 175 mutations. For this build I wrote 60 new ones for the new code (each breaks one rule on purpose and a test must fail): 39 of the first 58 were caught at once; 19 were not. For 17 of those I added tests (the move and gun boxes unticked after the hooks exist, the gate-hook box off, the position put back only at the end-of-frame places, a stack that does not change when asked to, the probe's one-minute limit and its 12-entry list, an answer about a gift not settling a drop, a gift counted as sent, a Herb given away not being read as a heal, the exe never telling its release, the gun hook for the damage switch and the hook box, the game's slot list being asked again) and they are caught now; 2 cannot change what the script does (the position pin can only be reached through the function that already checks you are down; the gun's skip flag is cleared before every call anyway). Two more were added later: the probe never being written again (caught) and a second host check on the gun hook (cannot change anything: `receiving_enemies()` already excludes the host). I also re-ran 30 older mutations that touch changed code: 24 are caught; 4 no longer apply (their code was replaced by what the new mutations cover); 2 are not caught: Q18 (only a counter changes, the hit itself is refused when hits are off) and Q29 (the shot learner waiting a second before it decides; an old gap I have left).


## What changed in v9.11 (release 0.7; same protocol v6; **update the script on BOTH computers; the exe's code is unchanged (a rebuilt exe carries the new script)**)

This build answers your 0.6 debug file (`96ca23bd-re7mp_debug.json`, build 9.9) and your list of notes, plus "items are taken from the inventory but cannot be given to the other player or dropped at their feet". I read the file first; what it showed is in `re7-findings.md`. Short version of what is in it and what I did about each thing. **None of this has run in the real game**; it runs against my mock and the real GUI names from your file.

**1. Give and drop: the receiver now answers, and nothing is lost.** The file cannot say why items did not arrive: the give/drop lines had been pushed out of the log by the tracer's lines before the file was written. What I could see: the script only looked for a player object called `Pl0000` (Ethan). Mia is `Pl2000`, so in her chapters the receiving side could not find a player object or an inventory, and a gift had nowhere to go. Changes:
- The receiver finds the player object by pattern (`Pl` and four digits) and, if there are several, takes the one nearest the camera.
- It tries to put the gift in the inventory, then on the ground at its feet. If the player object is not there yet (a loading screen), the gift waits and is retried for about 4 s.
- It then **tells the sender what happened** (a new message, kind 10): `Bob has Herb x1 (in the inventory)`, `... (on the ground (inventory full))`, or `Bob could not take Herb x1 (reason): it is back in your inventory`. A gift that could be neither added nor dropped is **handed back to the sender**, so nothing is lost. Drops get an answer too.
- If the sender hears nothing for 10 s it says so: `no answer from Bob about Herb x1: if it did not arrive, their script is probably older than build 9.11`. **So both of you need 9.11 for the answers; a 9.10 receiver still receives gifts, you just will not be told.**
- New: the **journal** (below).

**2. The debug file now has a journal.** `events` holds the last 600 lines of everything *except* the tracer's lines (give, drop, death, menu, wall, player-object messages). Before, a trace flushed them out within a minute. `lines` is as before.

**3. The other player no longer vanishes behind the map or the tape.** Cause, from the file: the old rule called any GUI with "menu" in its path a menu, and about 40 harmless ones sit under `SystemObject/MenuManager/`. `MapMaskGUI` was seen hidden, then showing, and stayed showing after the tape, so the stand-in was hidden for 81,953 frames. Now:
- the real menu names from your file are listed (`InventoryMenu`, `PauseMenuGUI`, `QuickEquipMenu`, `SaveMenuGUI`, `FileMenu`, `DetailSearchGUI`, the map `ui5000`/`ui5001`, `VideoCameraUI` (the tape) and so on); other names count only if the last part has a menu word (inventory, pause, option, notes, save ...) and nothing from the skip list (mask, fade, movie, logo, icon, guide, reticle, hud, tips, loading ...);
- a GUI that was never seen hidden is not a menu (an always-on one cannot hide you for ever);
